17 treasures placed (allows 15–25). Unused entries stay unused.

## AD&D 1e conversion notes

The module's stat blocks are **Basic D&D**. Running 1e, so at point of use:

- **Movement**: `90'(30')` → `9"`, `120'(40')` → `12"`, `60'(20')` → `6"`, `30'(10')` → `3"`.
- **Carrion crawler (r5) is materially nastier in 1e.** MM gives AC **3/7** (head/body),
  MV 12", HD 3+1, **8 tentacle attacks**, paralysis on failed save. The module lists a flat
  AC 7 and 6 hp. Use MM stats — this is the single most dangerous placed encounter and can
  wipe a 1st-level party. It is in Zelligar's chamber, behind a secret door, so a cautious
  party need not meet it.
- **Ghouls (r22)**: 1e paralysis does **not** affect elves. Relevant if an elf is rolled.
- **Morale**: module gives Basic ML 2–12. Use DMG p. 67 percentage morale instead.
- **XP**: award by DMG Appendix E (base + hp bonus), not Basic values.
- **Saves**: monsters save as fighters of their HD.

## Running notes

- Wandering monsters: check **every 2nd turn, 1-in-6**, then d6 on the level's table
  (upper table at txt line ~1709, lower at ~3732). Three checks per night if camping inside.
- Party of 3–6 recommended; module is "not easy" but forgiving by design.
- Rumor table (txt ~1557): 20 rumors, F-marked ones are **false**. Roll one per character.
- Background: Rogahn the Fearless (fighter) and Zelligar the Unknown (magic-user) built
  Quasqueton, left on a campaign against the barbarians, and never returned.

## Map layout (read from page renders, PDF pages 1–2 at 200 dpi)

Maps are **image-only** — pages 0–2 of the B1 PDF have zero text layer. Render at 200 dpi
via PyMuPDF and read the PNG. Legend: door, secret door (S), false door, one-way secret
door, stairs up/down, statue, covered pits.

**Upper level (rooms 1–37)** — worked stone, gridded 10'/square.
- **ENTRANCE at bottom-center (south wall)**, single corridor running north into **room 1**,
  the alcoves: three pairs flanking a long central north–south corridor. This is the only
  way in.
- West wing: 14–19 clustered upper-left, 20–22 lower-left, 10–12 center-west.
- Center: 2 (kitchen), 3 (dining), 4 (lounge, statue marked), 5–9 the wizard's suite
  running north, 13 and 37 north-center.
- East: **31 the Room of Pools**, a large chamber with **14 lettered pools a–n** — the
  single biggest set piece on the level. 33 (barracks) far east with bunk detail, 35 the
  three identical guest chambers stacked on the east edge, 23–30 across the south-east.
- **Secret doors marked S** near rooms 9, 10, 14, 23, 25, 32, 33, and flanking the central
  corridor at room 1. Covered pits marked near 36 and on the lower level near 56.
- Stairs down to the lower level are marked at several points; the main pair sits
  center-south near rooms 3/4.

**Lower level (rooms 38–56)** — irregular natural cavern, much harder to map in play.
- **38** is the only worked-stone area, bottom-center-right, with a door into the caverns.
- **42** bottom-center; the webbed region is drawn at **top-center** of the sheet.
- **48** center, marked as a depression (the arena). **50** a pool, upper-right. **54** a
  small chamber upper-left with a door. **56** far west, statue marked.
- **53** dominates the entire north half — the bats' cavern, very large and open.
- Passages are winding and irregular; several dead-ends. Expect mapping trouble, which is
  what the module intends.

## Session log

### Session 1 — 2026-08-15

- Party of 6 entered the front door late afternoon. Searched outside (2 turns), found the
  door's forced edge. Tristle's Hear Noise failed (60 vs 15).
- Door groaned loudly on opening — **the whole complex plausibly heard it.** Inside face has
  empty bar brackets, no bar. Corridor floor churned by long regular use.
- First alcove pair (r1) searched, 2 turns, both inconclusive rolls — nothing there, and
  correctly nothing to find.
- Second alcove pair reached. **Right alcove search rolled 1 (thorough) and still found
  nothing — correct, module states the one-way doors are undetectable from the corridor
  side even on close examination.** Left inconclusive. Party does not know they exist.
- **Turn 10 wandering monster: 1 troglodyte** (d6=4), from the north, 6 hp.
  - Applied MM chameleon surprise **1-4** (not 1-2) — party surprised on a 3. Disclosed the
    correction to the player openly.
  - Surprise round: Torsat 6 hp → **1 hp**. Dave and Torsat both failed stench saves.
    Dave rolled **6 rounds** of cumulative -1 STR (18/26 → 12), Torsat 1 round.
  - Round 1: trog won initiative, put Yrtone 7 → **1 hp**. Dave speared it, **Frython's dart
    hit for 3**, Blartho's CLW restored Torsat to full 6.
  - Morale check at 55% base, +15% for wound damage: rolled 62+15=77, **disengage-retreat**.
    Dave's parting attack killed it (19 to hit, 6 damage).
- **XP: 36 + 2/hp = 48 total, 8 each.** Nothing on the body — rolled unarmed (claws/teeth).
- Spells used: Blartho 1× cure light wounds. All others intact.
- **Orcs (M7) still unencountered in r1.** They may well have heard all of this.

- Yrtone self-healed to 4/7. Second full search of alcove pair 2 — both inconclusive.
- Advanced north. **Turn 14 wandering monster: 3 orcs (d6=1), rolled FROM THE SOUTH.**
  Narrated as emerging through the second-pair one-way doors — the dice, the stocking, and
  the module's stated purpose for those doors all coincided.
  - Round 1: initiative tied/simultaneous. Yrtone's sling killed one outright, Tristle's
    arrow wounded the leader, both set spears missed, both orc charges missed. Bless landed.
  - Round 2: orcs won initiative, leader hit Dave for 5 (9 → 4). Torsat natural 20.
    Morale 92+5=97 vs 50 → **flee in panic**. Free attacks + the party's pre-declared
    "fire if they break" contingency killed both as they ran.
  - Frython shouted in Common; rolled 20% for comprehension, failed. No parley.
- **XP: 43 total, ~7 each.** Running total ~15 each.
- Loot: hand axe, **spear**, club, 6 sp.
- Doors swung shut behind the orcs. Party now knows the location but cannot open from the
  corridor face. Forcing them is available as a player option.

*Running notes: party is playing very cautiously and burning turns on searches. Light budget
fine. They are ~40' north of alcove pair 2, approaching pair 3 (the magic mouths, untriggered).*


### Expedition 3 — rounds 190-246 *(logged 2026-08-25, from the mapdb)*

- Party left Rogahn's Chamber (area 25) west with four tapestries, 600 gp weight each.
- Corridor at y=13 walked west to the dead end at (18,13). Crossing at (19,13), openings
  north and south, both unexplored.
- Searched the west wall. **Ruling: the one-way doors of alcove pair 2 are concealed only
  from the entrance-corridor face (module text, area 1), so from the guard side a deliberate
  search finds them without a roll.** Recorded as a standing ruling.
- **Round 224 wandering check** (anchor 220, jitter 5): rolled 1. **2 berserkers**, d3=2 so
  from the south arm — verified as a real approach, 503 squares beyond it.
- Mutual surprise cancelled. Berserkers won initiative round 1, took the MM two-attack
  option, dropped **Dave from 7 to -1**. Torsat's natural 20 killed the axeman outright
  (7 damage vs 5 hp). Round 2 the party won initiative and **Tristle's spear killed the
  swordsman for exactly 5** before he could swing.
- **DM error, corrected in play:** narrated the axeman shrugging off Torsat's 7 damage
  before checking his hit points. He was dead. Corrected to the player immediately.
- **DM error, corrected in play:** told the player the door was jammed by their own iron
  spikes, on the authority of a CURRENT STATE sheet dated Day 2 turn 58. The player had
  recovered those spikes and restocked. **The live sheet was ~2 expeditions stale.**
- Verified against the books this session: DMG Zero Hit Points (p.82), DMG monster attack
  matrix (1 HD needs 16 vs AC 3 — *not* the fighter number), PHB non-proficiency by class
  (thief −3), PHB encumbrance bands and STRENGTH TABLE II, MM berserkers (strike twice or
  once at +2; never check morale).
- Checks at 241 (jitter 2) rolled 4 — nothing.
- Party opened the pair-2 one-way door from the guard side and spiked it open with two
  iron spikes as doorstops (no hammering, so no extra check). **Route out through it is
  130 ft against 280 ft the long way**, and it emerges BELOW the magic mouths, so the
  mouths did not fire.
- Berserker treasure: MM "Individuals K, B in lair"; not their lair, so type K only —
  **20 sp** (15 and 5), plus a long sword and a hand axe. Encoded the whole MM treasure
  appendix in `tools/treasure.py` so it need not be re-read.
- March out at 3" (four tapestries carried by Yrtone, Blartho, Tristle, Frython; Dave
  carrying nothing per DMG p.82; Torsat on light and sword). 140 ft, ~47 rounds.
- Checks at 261 (d6=5), 281 (d6=2), plus one extra for the groaning wooden door (d6=3).
  All clear. **Expedition 3 ended at round 300, everyone alive.**
- XP for 2 berserkers, scored as the orcs were (base 10 each + 1/hp, 9 hp between them):
  **29 total, ~5 each**, credited on exit per the module's rule that XP is awarded only
  on emerging.
- **Secret doors located 2026-08-25** from the player's reading of the sheet, after the

  wall of (33,6); area 32 west wall of (31,19). All three read as plain walls at thickness

  reachability because each room has a second way in. Area 32's placement independently
  matches both the module text and the party's own discovery from the Room of Pools side.
  **Still unlocated: area 8's half of the 8<->9 secret pair.**
- **Dave owes a full week of rest before he can adventure again** (DMG p.82). Not
  negotiable by cure spells.

## DEBRIEF — deliver only after the adventure ends

Things deliberately not explained in play, per the player's standing request:

1. **The alcove searches were not failures.** The module states the second-pair one-way doors
   are "totally unnoticeable to anyone on the side of the entrance corridor (even if close
   examination is made)." Tristle rolled a THOROUGH result (a 1) on the right alcove and
   still could not find it, because nothing findable exists on that face. No roll would have
   worked. The party did everything right.
2. **Troglodyte surprise was corrected mid-encounter.** I rolled party surprise against the
   standard 1–2 and announced "party alert," then found the MM gives troglodytes chameleon
   surprise on **1–4**. The 3 became a surprise. Disclosed at the time.
3. **The 1eonline site gave a wrong to-hit number** (2 HD vs AC 3 as 9; the DMG matrix says
   **13**). Caught by sanity check. All combat used the verified DMG value.
4. **Dart rate of fire** was unverified in these scans; the player supplied the correct value
   (3/round) and it is now recorded in `rules/combat.md`.
5. **Frython's spellbook draw was genuinely random** — offensive/defensive/misc d10 rolls per
   the DMG procedure, no thumb on the scale. Shocking grasp / spider climb / erase was simply
   a poor draw.
6. **Orc encounter direction was rolled, not authored.** d6 came up 5 = "from the south."
   The ambush-from-behind was the dice agreeing with the module's design, not a setup.

## Rulings made in play — expedition 4

**Area 27, treasure location.** The stocking table gives the ring but no place
for it (B1 leaves "Treasure & Location" blank by design). Ruled 2026-08-25:
it lies in a **shallow recess cut under the lip of the raised platform at its
north end**, closed with a slate tile flush to the stone. Not visible from the
floor at any distance; a deliberate search of the dais itself finds it, a search
of the thrones or the hall does not.

**Area 27, map corrections.** Two edges on the east side, (31,8,V) and (31,9,V),
classified as doors at thickness 13-14 and holes 283/95 — the drawn dais and its
two thrones sitting on the grid line. Corrected to `open` in b1_corrections.tsv;
reachability held at 4/1196. The room is x27-32, y7-10 = 60 ft by 40 ft, a fifth
larger than the extraction first had it.

**Area 27 door swing.** The south door off the y=13 corridor at (27,12,H) opens
AWAY from the corridor, southward. B1 draws no swing direction on any door, so
this is a DM call made by inspection and is now on record.

**Area 27 treasure FOUND, expedition 4 round 78.** Tristle rolled a 2 searching
the north end of the dais (elf, 1-2 in 6); Blartho rolled 5 on the same square
and Yrtone 6 on the south end. The **ring of protection +1** is now in the
party's hands and is UNIDENTIFIED — they have cast no detect magic and made no
test. Do not let its bonus apply until they establish what it is. XP value for
the item to be worked out from the DMG when they emerge; not yet awarded.

**Secret door (32,10,H), swing.** B1 draws no swing direction on any door, so
this is a DM call: ruled 2026-08-26 that it pivots on its west edge and opens
NORTH, away from the throne room, with a hand-hold cut on the throne-room face.
Chosen to match the established Quasqueton signature — the pair-2 alcove doors
and the west dead end are all proud-set slabs with a chest-height hand-hold.
Operable from BOTH sides, unlike the pair-2 pair; nothing in the text makes this
one one-way.

**Door (25,10,H) swing.** DM call 2026-08-27: hinges on the CORRIDOR side, west
of the frame; it opens SOUTH, toward the party, into the corridor. Deliberately
the opposite of (27,12,H) — B1 draws no swing on any door and this dungeon has
both kinds on record (map_known nodes 3→4 "opens into corridor 3", 5→6 "opens
into the lounge"), so varying them is consistent rather than arbitrary. Practical
effect: it must be pulled, not shouldered, and it can be spiked shut from the
corridor with no trouble at all.

**Round 142 wandering check, expedition 4.** Rolled kobolds (1-6); d6 gave ONE,
4 hp, approaching from the west along the y=10 corridor. The party was sealed in
area 26 behind a door they had spiked from the inside, in a room with no second
exit — so the encounter could not be delivered as rolled. Applied the placement
rule (rules/exploration.md, "the die says WHAT, never WHERE"): the kobold was
NOT relocated into the room and the encounter was NOT voided; it was placed in
the corridor outside, passing the closed door. Rolled 1-2 in 6 for the party to
hear it through the door while absorbed in searching.

**Round 165 wandering check, expedition 4.** Two troglodytes (hp 6 and 5),
approaching from the WEST along the y=10 corridor. Party still sealed in area 26
behind the spiked door. Placement rule applied as at round 142: placed in the
corridor, not in the room, and not voided.

Two rulings made here, both deliberate and both recorded so they stay consistent:

1. **The trogs are NOT stinking.** MM p.97: the secretion comes "when aroused
   for battle". A pair walking a corridor is not aroused, so the party gets no
   warning by smell and the trogs keep their chameleon surprise (1-4) if it
   comes to a fight. The stench starts the moment they decide to attack.
2. **Lantern light shows under the door.** A lit room behind a closed door in a
   dark corridor leaks a line of light at the threshold. Troglodytes have 9"
   infravision and are walking a black corridor; they will see it. This is a
   natural consequence of the party's own choice to seal itself in a dead end
   with a lantern burning, not a DM ambush — the risk was stated to the player
   before they spiked the door.

Party heard them first (d6=4, needed 1-4).

**Spiked doors, standing ruling 2026-08-27.** A spike wedged in the jamb HOLDS
against an ordinary open-doors attempt, by anyone, from either side. The open
doors roll from outside only determines whether the attacker gets the leaf to
shift enough to learn it is deliberately blocked rather than merely stuck.
Getting through a spiked door means breaking it or prising the spikes out — many
rounds, and loud. Ruled here rather than in the moment so it applies the same
way when it is the party doing the spiking, which so far it always has been.

**Round 178, expedition 4.** The watching troglodyte (hp 6) killed in the
doorway. Door slammed into it for 2, then Tristle's two arrows (4, 6) and both
sling bullets (4, 3) = 19 total against 6 hp. Initiative was TIED, but it had to
cross ten feet of lit room to reach anyone and died on the way, so it delivered
nothing — simultaneity does not manufacture an attack from a creature that never
arrived. Party took no damage.
KILLS PENDING XP (awarded on emerging, per the module):
  - troglodyte, area 27, round 65
  - troglodyte, trophy room doorway, round 178
  - the ring of protection +1 from area 27, item XP to be worked out from the DMG
THE SECOND TROGLODYTE IS STILL AT LARGE and went west for help at round 172.

**Second troglodyte return timer.** It left west at round 172 (turn 18). Rolled 2d6 = 8 TURNS until it is back with company, i.e. about round 252. Rolled hidden and recorded here, the same treatment as the wandering-check jitter: the party knows a timer exists because they watched it go, but not its length. Number and type of company to be rolled when it arrives.

**Round 252-253, expedition 4.** The return timer came due. The second
troglodyte brought back TWO more (1d3=2), so THREE at the door, hp 5 (the
survivor) plus two fresh at 6 and 5. The party did not hear them arrive (d6=5,
needed 1-4) — they were mid-search. The cloaks jammed under the threshold WORKED:
no light showed, so the trogs arrived without the confirmation they had last
time and had to try the door to learn anything.
Search interrupted at 3 rounds of 10; it yields nothing.

**Breaking a spiked door — ruling 2026-08-27.** No 1e rule found for battering a
door down; invented and stated openly to the player so they can plan against it.
A heavy iron-strapped door with spikes in the jamb needs **THREE successes at
1-2 in 6 per round** from a group working on it. Each success is an audible
stage the defenders hear: (1) the spikes shift in the jamb, (2) the frame splits,
(3) the leaf fails. Expected about nine rounds. Chosen so the defenders get real
warning and real decision time rather than a single surprise roll.

**Round 344-350, expedition 4.** Wandering check gave troglodytes again; d2=2, so
TWO, hp 6 and 5. Party sealed in area 28 behind a spiked, light-masked door, so
they were placed in the THRONE ROOM rather than in the room — the door off area
28 opens into area 27, not a corridor. Party did NOT hear them cross (d6=4,
needed 1-2 — further off than the corridor was, and through a spiked door). The
trogs DID notice the door and try it (d6=1). Treated as a fresh wandering pair,
not the three that left westward at round 265; those are elsewhere.
Pit search: Tristle rolled a 1 on the third pass — a CLEAN search returning
nothing. The sacrifice pit is settled: no trap, nothing hidden.

**Door (22,9,H) swing.** DM call 2026-08-28: hinges on the CORRIDOR side, opens
north toward the party. Continues the alternation already on record — B1 draws no
swing on any door and this dungeon has both kinds. Practical effect: the party
can oil it, and can spike it into the corridor floor if they want it held shut.

**WARNING — area 30 is the stairway to LEVEL 2.** "Devoid of detail or contents,
giving access to the lower level by a descending stairway. This stairway leads
down and directly into room 38 on the lower level." Area 38 is stocked with
gnomes (2-5), hp 7,5,4,2,1, parley possible. **LEVEL 2 IS NOT EXTRACTED.** If
the party descends, the game pauses while mapdb builds it — CHECKLIST §11 open
thread 1 has flagged this since 2026-08-25. Area 30 itself is unstocked and empty.

**Round 397 CORRECTION, expedition 4.** Frython's three darts were resolved with
no penalty and no risk to the front rank. That was wrong twice over: the rats had
won initiative and were already in contact, and the campaign's own house rule
(rules/exploration.md) makes firing past allies who fill a 10-ft corridor "-4
with a 50% chance of hitting the ally in front". The player queried it. Throws
RETRACTED: Frython keeps 15 darts, the fifth rat is alive, and the party made no
attack in round 397. Rat hp remain 4,3,2,1,1.
Standing reminder: BEFORE resolving any missile attack, ask whether allies are
between the shooter and the target, and apply the corridor rule.

**TORSAT'S DISEASE — rolled 2026-08-28, DMG p.14.**
Contracted round 398 from a giant rat bite (d100=2 against 5%, then failed his
save vs poison on a 6 needing 14).
  d100 = 88  -> SKIN
  occurrence d8 = 5, -1 for CON 12 -> 4 -> ACUTE (skin: acute 1-5, chronic 6-8)
  severity   d8 = 3, -1 for CON 12 -> 2 -> MILD  (skin: mild 1-5, severe 6-7, terminal 8)
  duration   1d3 = 1 WEEK
  incubation 1d4 = 3 DAYS (DM ruling; the table gives no incubation)
EFFECT: from day 3 after the bite, Torsat cannot perform strenuous activity and
must rest for ONE WEEK. No Charisma loss — the DMG's 10% CHA risk applies to
CHRONIC mild attacks and this is acute.
He shows nothing yet and will not for three days. TELL THE PLAYER WHEN IT SHOWS,
not before — he has no way to know he is ill.
Cure disease is a 3rd-level cleric spell and neither cleric is close to it.

**Round 404-405, expedition 4.** Wandering check gave kobolds; 1d6 = SIX, hp
4,3,3,2,2,1 (module block). Approaching from the EAST along the y=10 corridor,

Kobolds move 6" — HALF the party's rate. The party can outrun them trivially,
which is the single most important fact about kobolds and is in bestiary/kobold.md.

**CORRECTION round 405.** I told the player the access-room door "opens toward
you, into this room — so floor spikes would hold it hard". WRONG, and it
contradicted my own note four entries above and my own narration at rounds
387-394: the hinges are on the CORRIDOR side and it opens NORTH, into the
corridor — i.e. OUTWARD from the room. The player caught it.
Consequences, both of which favour the kobolds:
  - floor spikes on the inside are USELESS here; only jamb wedges work
  - bracing bodies against the leaf does nothing against a door being PULLED
Ruled that the door has a ring on its inner face as well as its outer — a door
people pass through both ways would — so the party CAN hold it by hauling on
that ring. Four adults in mail, one of them STR 18/26, against six three-foot
kobolds: they hold it comfortably, no roll.

**Area 30 shape, confirmed with the player.** The room proper is 30 ft by 20
(x21-23, y8-9) and a 10-ft-wide limb runs NORTH from its north-WEST corner,
containing the descending stairway at (21,10)-(21,11). The extent classifier
excludes the limb because it is one square wide and fails the 2x2 room test; it
is functionally part of area 30. Module text gives no dimensions, so the sheet
is the authority.

**Player note on speed, for later.** Kobolds move 6" to the party's 9", but the
player does not accept that this means the party can simply walk away from a
hostile encounter, and is right to push back — 1e pursuit/evasion is a whole
DMG procedure (pursuit is CERTAIN if the monster entry or key says so, 80%
likely for semi-intelligent/hungry/aggressive types) and fleeing gives free
attacks at +2. LOOK UP DMG "Pursuit And Evasion In Underground Settings"
before any chase is adjudicated. Do not let the raw movement numbers stand in
for the procedure.

**SPOILER LEAK, round 439 — retracted.** I described the SECOND south secret
door of area 25 to the player as something the party could see ("a second secret
slab, same construction, same seam"). They cannot: it is a secret door in a wall
panelled floor to ceiling in fir strips, and nobody has searched it. Read off the
database and narrated as perception. RETRACTED in play at the player's request.
The party knows exactly TWO doors here — the south-west one into the throne room
and the west one onto the corridor. Both now spiked.
ROOT CAUSE: `dmap.py at` prints every edge kind including `secret`, and I read
its output aloud. Before narrating from that command, FILTER IT — the party sees
what it has found, not what the level knows.

**Third secret door (33,10,H) — mechanism, DM call 2026-08-28.** Pivots on its
EAST edge and opens NORTH, into Rogahn's Chamber — the same family as (32,10,H)
ten feet west of it, which was ruled the same way. With the fir planking stripped
off, an iron RELEASE CATCH is visible in the rock beside the seam on the chamber
face; the panelling had covered it, which is why the party walked past this door
last expedition without a hint. It opens easily from inside the chamber and would
have to be found from the far side.
Consequences: it opens TOWARD the chamber, so FLOOR spikes hold it hard from this
side (rules/exploration.md). Beyond it (33,9) (33,8) (33,7) run south as a 10 ft
corridor, and (33,7) has ANOTHER secret door in its south wall. Do not mention
that one — it is three squares away and out of sight.

**Round 978.** Tristle found the secret door (33,6,H) at the south end of the
blind corridor — rolled 2, needed 1-2. Beyond it is area 24, MISTRESS' CHAMBER.
Area 24 is NOT in the stocking table (no monster, no rolled treasure) but the
MODULE ITSELF gives it minor authored treasure: a silver-plated comb worth 5 gp,
a single gold piece in a tortoiseshell dish, and two capped bottles half full of
perfume. That is authored content, not stocking, so it stands.
Narrative STOPPED on the discovery per the player's standing rule.
Swing not yet ruled — decide when they examine it.

**Secret door (33,6,H) mechanism — DM call 2026-08-28.** Pivots, like every
other slab in this stronghold, and has a release catch on the CORRIDOR face as
well as the chamber face. Ruled operable from both sides on design grounds: a
private passage cut between Rogahn's chamber and his mistress's, sealed by
secret doors at both ends, would be absurd if its users could be shut inside it.
The same reasoning applies to (33,10,H) at the north end — there IS a catch on
the corridor side of that one too, and the party can get back out. Do not spring
"you are sealed in" on them; the builder was not an idiot.
Tristle's trap check rolled 100 — the worst possible. No trap found and NOTHING
cleared. There is in fact no trap (area 24 is unstocked and the module describes
none), but he cannot know that.

**Round 1024, area 24 searched.** Tristle rolled 1 and Blartho rolled 1 — TWO
clean searches. Area 24 is unstocked and the module hides nothing in it, so both
correctly return nothing concealed. That is a real result and can be told to the
player as settled: there is nothing hidden in the Mistress' Chamber.
Authored contents taken: silver-plated comb 5 gp, one loose gold piece, two
capped half-full perfume bottles (no value given). The 3'x4' tapestry is framed
and ANCHORED to the wall — the module says it cannot be removed without damage,
after which it is worth 40 gp. The party has not touched it.
The tapestry reads "Melissa, the most dearly won and greatest of all my
treasures." The mirror reads "To the fairest of all in my eyes."

**SPOILER SLIP round 1048 — retracted.** Described the south corridor as running
along "the southern edge of everything you have mapped" and said "you are at the
bottom of the level". That is the SHEET's knowledge, not the characters'. The
party has no idea where the level's boundaries are. Retracted at the player's
request. Same root cause as the area 25 leak: reading map facts as perception.
Say what the lantern shows, not what the grid says.

**DOOR PROCEDURE CORRECTED, round 1069.** The player challenged the fact that
every door this expedition had needed a Strength check. He was right: B1's
introduction sets the rule and I never read it. "In all cases, unless otherwise
noted, doors will be locked one-third of the time — and any roll of a 1 or 2 on
a six-sided die will mean that they will bar entrance unless the lock is sprung
or broken."
So: d6 per door, 1-2 LOCKED, 3-6 IT OPENS. No open-doors roll for ordinary B1
doors — the PHB roll is for STUCK doors and B1 never calls its doors stuck.
Doors already opened in expedition 4 STAND. Tristle's failed pull at round 1069
was VOIDED and the door re-resolved under the module's rule.
The two-man forcing ruling still applies, but only to breaking a LOCKED door or
to something the fiction has actually established as stuck.
Locked doors: Tristle Open Locks 35%, or break it — which the module itself says
"may serve to attract unwelcome monsters", i.e. an extra wandering check.

**NAVIGATION ERROR, round 1111 — corrected by the player.** The party was walked
from (19,5) to (19,6). That edge is a WALL, and the query I had just run printed
"(19,5) N=wall" in the output I was reading. I took a mostly-open column of cells
for a corridor and invented a west-side opening at (19,6) that the party cannot
reach.
TRUE ROUTE from the wood shop: (31,5) N to (31,6), W to (28,6), S to (28,3), W to
(19,3), N to (19,5), then the corridor turns EAST to (20,5), then N to (20,7),
which is a 3-way (N, E, S). dmap.py path gives exactly this, 21 squares/210 ft.
FIX: path every multi-square move. The tool cannot walk through walls; I can.

**Map correction, exp.4 turn 112 — the stair limb is sealed except to area 30.**
Two edges around (21,11) were wrong and both were caught by the new
`doorcheck.py` one-sided test, before the party could be walked through them:

- `(21,11,H)` was `door`. It is a **wall**. The "door" was the LANDING at the
  head of the stairs: hole 347, aspect 2.2, against 95-187 for a real B1 door,
  sitting 0.23 cells below the bar with only a 2 px ruling on the flanks.
- `(21,11,V)` was `stairs`. It is a **wall**. The tread ladder drawn inside
  (21,11) put slots and hole on the boundary; the 200 dpi render shows an
  unbroken bar at x=22 from y=10 to y=12.

So the limb is entered ONLY from area 30, via (21,9) -> (21,10) -> stairs up to
(21,11). It does NOT open onto the y=12 corridor and does NOT open onto (22,11).
This matches the shape already confirmed with the player (see "Area 30 shape").

**Consequence for play:** the party is in the y=12 corridor and CANNOT reach the
stairway from it. The only ways into area 30 are the door at (22,10) — which
they spiked shut with four spikes at turn 412 — and the throne-room end.

**Door (23,6,V) — area 29 CAPTAIN'S CHAMBER, west door. Turn 115, round 1148.**

**LOCKED.** B1 door rule rolled in the open, d6 = **1** (1-2 locked). Per the
module: *"they will bar entrance unless the lock is sprung or broken. Breaking
the lock or breaking down the entire door will be a noisy undertaking, to be
sure, and may serve to attract unwelcome monsters."* So breaking it earns an
EXTRA wandering check by the module's own words — `clock.py extra`.

This is the FIRST genuinely locked door of the campaign. Tristle is the only one
who can spring it quietly (thief, open locks). Everything else is noise.

**Swing, DM call.** Hinges on the FAR side, inside the chamber; it pivots on its
north edge and opens AWAY from the party, into the room. Push, not pull.
Deliberately the opposite of (22,9,H), the last one ruled — the alternation is on
record and this dungeon has both kinds. Practical effects:
  - it must be shouldered, not hauled
  - from the corridor it CANNOT be held with floor spikes; jamb wedges only
  - once inside, floor spikes hold it hard

**Area 29 is NOT in the stocking table** — no rolled monster, no rolled treasure,
exactly like area 24. If the module's own text gives the Captain's Chamber
authored content, that stands (it did for area 24's comb, coin and perfume).
Check the room key before describing the interior.

**Extraction note:** this door is REAL and verified — hole 193, offset 0.09,
a clean glyph straddling a heavy bar on the 200 dpi render. It is also the
`doorcheck` false positive documented in the lab notebook (flank 2.0 px, because
the wall it sits in is only one square long). Do not "correct" it away.

**Lock sprung, (23,6,V), round 1149.** Tristle rolled **d100 = 24** against **35**
— his one and only attempt at this lock at 1st level. Door unlocked, still shut,
still opens away into the chamber.

**RULES CACHE CORRECTED 2026-08-29, and this one matters.** Verifying Open Locks
turned up an error that has been live most of the campaign:

  - `rules/exploration.md` gave Tristle's Find/Remove Traps as **30%**. It is
    **25%**. Base 20% (PHB p.28) + **5%** for DEX 18 + 0% elf.
  - The mistake was reading DEXTERITY TABLE II (PHB p.12) across the wrong
    columns: at DEX 18 the row is +10 / +15 / **+05** / +10 / +10, and the
    Locating/Removing Traps figure is the +05.

Both tables are now cached and cited in the new `rules/thief.md`, along with
every one of Tristle's percentages and the **one-try-per-lock-ever** rule, which
had never been written down anywhere.

No past ruling flips on the 5% — his trap and search rolls were all d6 secret-door
checks or came in well under either number — but do not use the old figure again.

## Area 29 CAPTAIN'S CHAMBER — module key, verified from B1 p.16 render, 2026-08-29

**UNSTOCKED** (not in the stocking table) — no rolled monster, no rolled
treasure. Everything below is the module's own authored content and stands, the
same way area 24's comb/coin/perfume did.

Erig — Rogahn's friend and comrade in arms, captain of the guard.

**The door** (23,6,V): *"a large wooden construction just like the others in the
stronghold, but its exterior surface is embellished with an irregular-shaped
leather skin covering, which is studded with circular bits of brass which form
the word 'ERIG' prominently."*

> **DM ERROR, round 1148 — the party EXAMINED this door and I described plain
> banded timber.** The brass-studded name is on the corridor face and is
> unmissable. Given to the player at round 1150 with the miss acknowledged.
> Root cause: I ruled the swing and rolled the lock without reading the room key
> first. **Read the key for a room BEFORE narrating its door.**

Contents, by square (room is x24-26, y5-6, 30 ft x 20 ft):

| where | what |
|---|---|
| SE corner (26,5) | a crude bed; a table alongside it |
| on the table | a small stoneware **crock with a cover, containing 5 g.p.**; a large earthenware tankard mug; a small hand mirror |
| south wall | a wooden **chest, LOCKED** |
| in the chest | garments — pants, several cloaks, a heavy cloth coat, two pairs of boots; a **broken dagger** at the bottom under the clothing; a **leather pouch** |
| in the pouch | a **walnut plaque with inlaid silver**, engraved *"To Erig, great and trusted fighter by my side, and captain of the guard at Quasqueton — against all foes we shall prevail!"*, signed with an embellished **"R."** **Worth up to 25 g.p. if sold.** |
| NE corner (26,6) | a wooden keg stand with a single **barrel marked "SD"**, full and untapped. Ale if broken open. |
| west wall | numerous pegs and brackets for arms and armour, **mostly empty except two shields and a heavy mace** |

**The chest is locked and Tristle has already spent his one Open Locks attempt
this level** — PHB p.28 allows one try *per lock*, so a fresh lock is a fresh
try. This one is available to him. Do not let him retry the DOOR lock; that one
is used up until 2nd level.

**Area 29 searched, rounds 1155-1175 (turns 116-117).** Dave on guard, Frython
on the lamp, the other four searching.

- **Crock opened: 5 g.p.** taken/found. Tankard and hand mirror are ordinary.
- Bed: crude frame, bedding rotted, nothing in or under it.
- **Walls CLEAN.** Tristle d6=**1** (elf, needs 1-2) and Blartho d6=**1** (needs 1)
  — two successful searches returning nothing. There is genuinely no secret door
  in area 29; the module gives none and the map has none. Settled, do not re-roll.
- **The CHEST is still LOCKED and unopened.** Contents per the key: garments, a
  broken dagger under them, and a leather pouch with the walnut-and-silver
  plaque (**25 g.p.**). Tristle's Open Locks is AVAILABLE for this lock — PHB
  p.28 allows one try *per lock*, and the one he spent was on the door.
- **Barrel "SD" sealed and untapped.** Ale if broken open. Nothing else in it.
- Two shields and a heavy mace still on the wall pegs, not yet taken.

**DM ERROR, round 1175 — area 29 has a WESTERN ALCOVE and I described the room
without it. The player caught it off the printed map, again.**

The room is not a plain 30x20 block. A **10 ft wide, 20 ft long limb runs WEST
off its south-west corner**: (23,5) and (22,5), open to (24,5) and dead-ending
against a wall at the west face of (22,5).

Measured, so this is settled:

| edge | thick | gap | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (23,5,V) | 2.0 | **1.00** | wholly unblocked — a graph-paper ruling |
| (22,5,V) | 2.0 | **1.00** | wholly unblocked |
| (21,5,V) | 9.0 | 0.00 | solid — the dead end |
| (23,5,H) (22,5,H) | 9.0 | 0.00 | solid — this is what separates the alcove from the corridor running parallel ABOVE it at y=6 |

**The architecture is the point:** the approach corridor (21,6)-(23,6) and this
alcove (22,5)-(23,5) run parallel, ten feet apart, separated by one wall, and
the alcove is reachable ONLY from inside the room. From the corridor you would
never know it was there.

**CONSEQUENCE — the pegs are in the ALCOVE, not by the door.** The module says
*"On the wall at the western extremity of the room are numerous pegs and
brackets… except for two shields and a heavy mace hanging thereon."* With the
limb present, the western extremity of the room is **the end wall of the alcove**,
(22,5) west. I had placed the pegs on the door wall because I had not seen the
alcove. Corrected to the player at round 1175.

**Extents:** the alcove is one square wide and so fails `extents.py`'s 2x2 room
test — the same reason area 30's stair limb is excluded. Area 29 is therefore
**8 squares functionally** (6 assigned + 2 in the limb). Not forcing it in the
DB; recorded here, exactly as the area 30 limb is.

**Nothing else is in the alcove.** The module gives it no contents, and the
party's two-turn search with four people covered it — Tristle's and Blartho's
clean wall rolls stand for these walls too.

**Round 1177. Taken from area 29:** the **5 g.p.** from the crock, the **two
large shields** and the **footman's mace** off the alcove wall. Logged to
inventory; funds 540 -> 545 gp. Shields are 10# each (DMG Appendix O, now cached
in `rules/equipment.md`), so **200 gp weight added** the party was not carrying
before. Weapon weights are NOT in Appendix O and remain uncached — the mace is
carried as ~10# unverified.

**Tristle FAILED the chest's trap search: d100 = 52, needed 25.** That was his
ONE attempt (PHB p.28, one try per trap per thief). He cannot try again until
2nd level. **He does not know whether the chest is trapped.**

DM knowledge: **it is not trapped.** The module gives area 29's chest no trap.
But the party has no way to establish that now, and must decide whether to open
a locked chest they could not clear. Do NOT hint either way — a failed Find
Traps is supposed to be indistinguishable from "there is nothing there".

Reminder: **Tristle's Open Locks is still available for the chest** (one try per
LOCK; the door consumed a different one). 35%.

**Round 1178. Tristle FAILED the chest lock by ONE: d100 = 36, needed 35.**

Both of his once-ever rolls on this chest are now spent — trap search (52 vs 25)
and Open Locks (36 vs 35). **Neither can be retried until he reaches 2nd level**
(PHB p.28). The chest is locked, unproved for traps, and closed.

**Do NOT expect him to level soon.** `tools/xp.py` prints `XP | next lvl | to go`
and I misread the middle column as his total: Tristle is on **320 XP**, needs
**1,251**, and is **931 short**. Expedition 4's whole pending ledger is on the
order of 200 XP across six characters — roughly 35-40 each — so he finishes this
trip nearer 360 than 1,251.

That makes the chest a LONG-term problem, not a next-session one. It stays shut
until either they break it or Tristle gains a level several expeditions from now.

Remaining routes for the party: **break it** (B1: *"unless the lock is sprung or
broken… breaking the lock will be a noisy undertaking, to be sure, and may serve
to attract unwelcome monsters"* — so an EXTRA wandering check, `clock.py extra`),
or leave it and come back.

Do not steer them. The contents are the garments, the broken dagger and the
25 g.p. plaque — worth having, not worth dying for, and they cannot know that.

## Pack animal into Quasqueton — DM ruling, 2026-08-29 (player asked at round 1179)

**Verdict: a MULE or DONKEY can be got in. A horse cannot.** Ruled once, stands.

Facts from the module's own entrance text (B1, ENCOUNTER AREAS, ENTRANCE):

- *"a treacherous pathway which leads up to the craggy outcropping of black
  rock"* — **sure-footed animals only.** Mule/donkey yes, horse or pony no.
- *"obscured by vegetation… easily accessible to human-size explorers"* — a
  laden animal needs the vines and branches **cut properly back**: a turn or two
  of work, and it leaves the entrance visibly opened for anyone who passes.
- *"a 10' wide corridor"*, ceilings 8-10 ft — ample. Not a constraint anywhere
  on level 1.
- *"two steps up"* past the third alcove pair — a mule takes two steps fine.
- Doors are 5-6 inches of banded timber in normal frames. Each must be **propped
  open**; an animal will not push one.

**THE REAL OBSTACLE IS THE MAGIC MOUTHS** at the third pair of alcoves. They are
*"a permanent feature of the stronghold, and will reappear on every visit"* —
two booming voices at conversational distance in a stone corridor, finishing
with *"a loud and raucous laughter."*

**Ruling:** an animal led into that unprepared **bolts**, back down the corridor
and down the path, pack and all. Led past **blindfolded, with its ears stuffed,
and two handlers on the halter**, it holds — no roll. That preparation is the
price of admission and the party must state it.

Secondary: the corridors now hold six dead kobolds, dead troglodytes and dead
rats at various points. Animals shy at blood. Assume it will need leading past,
not driving.

**Route entrance -> area 29 is 430 ft** and crosses no stairs on the practical
route. `dmap.py path` will route through the one-way alcove door at (17,13,V) if
allowed to — that is NOT a route anything can be led along; it is one-way from
the far side. Use the party's own known route.

**Do not volunteer whether it is worth it.** The party can smell malt at the
bung, so they may reason the keg is ale (1 sp/pint, PHB) and draw their own
conclusion. The chest's contents remain unknown to them.

**Spiking noise — RULING DRIFT CAUGHT BY THE PLAYER, 2026-08-29.**

I began rolling a d6 "is it heard" every time the party set or drew spikes
(rounds 113, 115, 117). That was new and wrong. The correct ruling was already on
the record from the trophy room — *"done quietly by hand and mallet-handle rather
than hammered, so no extra wandering check"* — and the ONE earlier roll, at
(25,10,H), was against a **specific troglodyte known to be standing outside the
door**, which is a different question entirely.

Now cached as a standing table in `rules/exploration.md` under **"Spiking a door
— NOISE"**. Quiet spiking: no roll, ever. Rolls only for a named listener already
in place, or for hammering under pressure. Breaking a lock or a door is the only
thing B1 itself calls noisy.

All three stray rolls came up unheard (4, 5, 5), so nothing is retconned.

**The lesson is the same one this campaign keeps relearning:** a ruling made in
play and not written into `rules/` will be re-made differently a week later. If a
judgment call is going to recur, cache it the same turn it is made.

**WANDERING ENCOUNTER, round 1203 (check anchored at 1200, fired at 1203).**

**Giant rats, 1d4+1 = TWO.** hp **4 and 3** (first two off the module block
4,3,2,1,1). AC 7, HD 1/2, 1 attack, D 1-3 **+ disease**, MV 120'(40'),
Save NM, **ML 8 / morale 50%**. XP 7 + 1/hp each -> 11 + 10 = **21 pending**.

Placement per the standing rule (*"the die says WHAT, never WHERE"*): the party
is in the dead-end stub at (23,23) facing the armoury door, so the only approach
is up the corridor **behind** them, round the corner at (23,22) from the crossing.
Placed there, ~20 ft, NOT voided.

- Party did **not** hear them coming: d6 = **6**.
- Party surprise: d6 = **5** -> **not surprised**.
- Rats surprise: d6 = **1** -> **SURPRISED** (open lantern, dead-end stub).

**=> The party gets one free round.** The rear rank — Yrtone and Blartho — is the
rank facing them; Dave and Torsat are turned the wrong way at the door.

**DANGER, do not soften:** Blartho is on **2 hp** and a rat bites for 1-3. One
bite can drop him. Frython is also on 2. The two strongest fighters are at the
wrong end of a ten-foot stub.

**Disease:** every giant rat bite carries the 5% check (DMG). Torsat is already
carrying an acute mild skin disease from the round-398 bite and STILL DOES NOT
KNOW — his day 4 has not arrived. Do not conflate the two if someone is bitten.

**Round 1203, the free round. RULING CORRECTED BY THE PLAYER — and they were right.**

I was about to apply the "-4 and 50% chance of hitting the ally in front" corridor
penalty to Frython's darts. The player objected: *"the rule about hitting people
in the party in front of you is when they're in melee, not just when they are
standing in front."*

**Correct.** `rules/exploration.md` heads that whole section **"Missile fire into
a melee"**, and the round-397 note says exactly why it applied then — *"the rats
had won initiative and were already in contact."* Here the rats are 20 ft away and
NOTHING is engaged. No melee, no penalty. The test is **contact, not geometry.**

The cached wording was genuinely ambiguous — the corridor clause read like a
general rule about anyone standing in front. Rewritten as a scoped table with both
misreadings recorded as worked examples (round 397 = applied correctly, round 1203
= would have been applied wrongly).

**Also my error:** I put a 10-ft pole in Tristle's hands that the player never
declared. Tristle has his SPEAR, per the standing marching order. Do not invent
gear state — the marching order is: Dave and Torsat front with long swords and
shields, Frython with the lantern and darts, Tristle with spear, Yrtone and
Blartho rear with flails and shields.

**Result:** three darts at -2 (medium range, 20 ft) — **d20 = 1, 4, 10, all miss**
against a needed 16. The natural 1 does NOT strike a friendly: that clause is also
scoped to melee. Frython 15 -> 12 darts, three on the floor and recoverable.

**CLOSED, same session.** Missile ranges are now cached and cited in
`rules/combat.md` from a 400 dpi render of **PHB p. 38**. The DM ruling was
CORRECT: dart **S 1.5" / M 3" / L 4.5"**, and **PHB p. 39 (DISTANCE)** states the
conversion in the book's own words — *"Outdoors, 1″ equals 10 yards. Indoors
1″ equals 10 feet."* So a dart is short to 15 ft, medium 15-30, long 30-45, and
**nothing past 45 ft**. 20 feet is medium, **-2 stands**. Modifiers verified at
**DMG p. 74**: -2 medium, -5 long.

**NEW AND TACTICALLY IMPORTANT — the oil flask.** It is not on the PHB weapon
table at all; it is **DMG p. 64, GRENADE-LIKE MISSILES**: *"The range of all such
container missiles is 3″. Beyond 1″ is medium, and beyond 2″ is long."*
Underground that is **10 ft short / 20 ft medium / 30 ft long, and a flask CANNOT
be thrown further than 30 feet.** The party treats 26 flasks as its reserve
weapon and has never been told the range. Surface it the moment they declare a
throw, not before.

Also noted: heavy crossbow fire rate is **1/2** — one shot every other round.

**Round 1204 — initiative TIED (party d6=1, rats d6=1), blows simultaneous.**
Rats started 20 ft out and had to cross, so per the round-178 precedent
simultaneity did not let them bite from range; Frython's throw went out as they
came. **Nobody hit anything.**

- Frython 3 darts at -2 (medium): d20 = 10, 1, 4 vs needed 16. All miss.
  **15 -> 9 darts total across both rounds**, six on the floor 20 ft back.
- Rat A -> Dave (AC 3): d20 = 15, needed 18. Miss.
- Rat B -> Torsat (AC 3): d20 = 9, needed 18. Miss.
- Dave long sword: 11 vs 12 — **missed by one.**
- Torsat long sword: 12 vs 13 — **missed by one.**
- Tristle spear: 7 vs 17. **Tristle is NON-PROFICIENT with the spear, -3**
  (his proficiencies are long sword and short bow). Surfaced to the player.

Rats undamaged: still **hp 4 and 3**. Now in contact with Dave and Torsat.

**BUG FIXED MID-COMBAT: `combat.hd_band` could not parse fractional hit dice.**
`combat.py monster --creature giant-rat` raised ValueError instead of rolling a
bite, because the bestiary spells giant-rat HD the module's way, "1-4 hit
points". Kobolds (1/2), giant rats (1/2) and giant centipedes (1/4) are the three
commonest wandering monsters on this level and NONE of them could attack. Now
handled, with regression tests in `tools/tests/test_rules_and_combat.py`.
**155 tests pass.**

**Round 1205 — rats won initiative (4 vs 2) and acted first.**

- Rat A -> Dave (AC 3): d20 = 2. Miss.
- Rat B -> Torsat (AC 3): d20 = 13, needed 18. Miss.
- Dave long sword: 10 vs 12. Miss.
- **Torsat long sword: 19 vs 13 -> HIT, damage 3.** Rat B had exactly **3 hp**
  and is **DEAD**. XP 7 + 1/hp = **10, pending**.
- Tristle spear: 10 vs 17. Miss. (-3 non-proficient, deliberate — see CHECKLIST §3.)

**Morale, the surviving rat A (hp 4, undamaged):** base 50%, +0 for hit dice,
+15% for 50%+ of their number slain. d100 = 50, adjusted 65 vs 50 ->
**BREAKS by 15% = "Fall back, fighting."**

That is a withdrawal in good order, NOT flight: it backs off facing the party.
Per `rules/exploration.md` only a creature that **breaks and flees** gives free
attacks (+2 for showing its back). **No free attack here.** Do not award one.

Rat A is now giving ground down the corridor toward the crossing, still between
the party and the way out, still able to bite anything that follows carelessly.

**Round 1206.** Frython threw 3 darts at the withdrawing rat. **No corridor
penalty applied** — the rat had disengaged, so there was no melee; only the -2 for
medium range (rat ~20-30 ft; dart medium band is 15-30 ft indoors, now VERIFIED
from PHB p.38). d20 = 2, 12, **18 -> HIT for 3**.

Rat A had 4 hp and is now on **1**.

**Second morale check:** base 50%, +15% half its number slain, +15% personal
wound damage over 50%. d100 = **99**, adjusted **129 vs 50 — breaks by 79%**,
which the margin table calls *Surrender*.

**DM RULING: a giant rat is SEMI-INTELLIGENT and cannot surrender.** At that
margin the animal equivalent is a total rout, so it breaks completely and bolts
flat out down the corridor toward the crossing, showing its back. Ruled once,
applies to any animal that rolls into the Surrender band: **total rout, not
submission.** Cache this if it recurs.

It shows its back, so it WOULD give free attacks at +2 — but nobody is in reach
(20-30 ft) and Frython has already spent all three darts this round.

**It escapes at 1 hp unless the party pursues.** Note for later: a fleeing rat
that lives is not a story-teller, but it is 10 XP walking away, and giant rats
were rolled here once already this expedition.

Frython **9 -> 6 darts**; NINE now on the corridor floor between here and the
crossing, all recoverable.

**Pending XP so far this fight: one giant rat, hp 3 -> 10 XP.**

## Area 34 ARMORY — module key, B1 p.19. UNSTOCKED (no rolled monster or treasure)

**Irregularly shaped.** Houses the stronghold's arms supply, *"mostly empty now,
however, since many of the arms were taken along on the last forway."*

**THE HAZARD — read before they enter.** Verbatim:

> *"When the room is entered, a slight whistling sound can be heard if the
> adventurers stand quietly. If the door is closed (unless spiked open it will
> close automatically one round after everyone has entered, and even if so
> secured, there is a 50% chance that it will close anyway) and the second exit
> is likewise closed, a howling wind will immediately result, with an 80% chance
> of putting out any torch carried by the adventurers, or a 50% chance to
> extinguish each lantern carried. The wind will cease whenever either or both
> of the exits is opened."*

- Ceiling is **20 ft**; examining it shows **two sizable vents**, no usable
  access. The phenomenon is **natural, not magical** — say so if they check.
- **The door shuts itself one round after the last person is in.** Spiking it
  open only works 50% of the time.
- **50% chance PER LANTERN.** The party carries two: Torsat's lit one (22.5 turns
  of fuel, anchored) and Yrtone's spare, state unknown. A double failure puts
  them in the dark in an unmapped room. **Do not warn them; this is the module's
  own trap and they can hear the whistling if they stand quietly.**

**Contents** — on carved ledges and wooden pegs around mostly smooth rock walls:
battered shields (several broken, poor repair), bits of body armour (uniformly
poor), several **crude bows (−2 "to hit")**, a quiver of normal arrows, **two
swords (one in good condition)**, **a dozen spears**, two hand axes (one with a
split handle), **a flail**, a two-handed sword with a broken blade, and **a
dagger**. *"None of the items appears remarkable, although the flail, the dagger,
and one of the swords seem to be usable and of normal value for such an item."*

**Extreme south-west corner: two locked chests — BOTH EMPTY.** Large, bulky and
heavy. Tristle's Open Locks is available for each (one try per lock), and there
is nothing behind either. Do not hint.

**Relevant to the party right now:** a dozen spears are in here, and Tristle
fights at −3 with the one he carries. A spare spear changes nothing mechanically
— but ONE OF THE SWORDS IS GOOD, and a long sword is a weapon he IS proficient
with. Do not point this out; let them find it.

**Armoury door (23,23,V), round 1213. LOCKED** — B1 door rule, d6 = **1**.

Second locked door of the session, and the party's third distinct lock overall.
**Tristle's Open Locks IS available for it** — PHB p.28 is one try per LOCK, and
he has spent his tries on the area 29 door (made it, 24 vs 35) and the area 29
chest (failed by one, 36 vs 35). This is a new lock: a fresh 35% attempt, once,
ever, until he reaches 2nd level. He is on 320 XP of the 1,251 he needs.

**Swing, DM call:** hinges on the FAR side, inside the armoury; it opens AWAY
from the party — push, not pull. Chosen to fit the module's own behaviour, since
the door *"will close automatically one round after everyone has entered"* — a
leaf that swings inward and settles shut behind them.
Practical: from this side it cannot be held with floor spikes, only jamb wedges;
and **spiking it open works only 50% of the time** by the module's own text.

**All 9 darts recovered** (Frython 6 -> 15). No wandering check fired during the
recovery.

**The dead rat carries nothing.** Giant rats are MM treasure type C, but that is
a LAIR type — a wandering pair has no treasure on it. Do not roll for it.

**Round 1214. Tristle SPRANG the armoury lock: d100 = 9 vs 35.** His one and only
attempt at this lock, made. Door unlocked, still shut, still opens away.

Tally of his once-ever rolls at 1st level, all now spent:
| lock / trap | roll | needed | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| area 29 door, Open Locks | 24 | 35 | **made** |
| area 29 chest, Find Traps | 52 | 25 | failed |
| area 29 chest, Open Locks | 36 | 35 | **failed by one** |
| armoury door, Open Locks | 9 | 35 | **made** |

Still available to him: **Find Traps on the armoury door** (a different trap from
the chest's — one try per trap), and Open Locks on each of the **two chests in the
armoury's south-west corner**, which are EMPTY.

**NEXT BEAT — the armoury hazard fires on entry.** Re-read the area 34 note above
before narrating them through this door. The leaf shuts itself one round after
the last person is inside (50% even if spiked open), and with both exits shut the
wind has a **50% chance to blow out EACH lantern**. They carry two. Let them hear
the whistling if they stand quietly; do not warn them.

**SPOILER LEAK, round 1214 — I called it "the armoury door" to the player.**

The party has never opened it and has no way to know what the room is. I read the
label straight off `dmap.py at` output — *"(24,23) area 34 ARMORY"* — and let it
into narration, which is EXACTLY the root cause already recorded for the round-439
leak about area 25's second secret door.

The rule that was already on the record and was not followed:

> *"Before narrating from that command, FILTER IT — the party sees what it has
> found, not what the level knows."*

**Room NAMES are part of what must be filtered, not just edge kinds.** The DB
labels every square with its area name and number; none of that is visible to
anyone standing in a corridor. Refer to unopened rooms only by what can be seen:
"a door", "a large room", "the room beyond".

Player asked directly: *"please don't tell me it's an armoury or what's behind the
door until we open the door."* Honour that for every unentered area from here on.

## Round 1215-1217 — THE ARMOURY WIND FIRED. THE PARTY IS IN THE DARK.

They entered area 34 and deliberately closed and spiked the door behind them, as
they have done in every room this expedition. That closed the second exit and
triggered the module's own hazard.

- **d100 = 41 vs 50 — Torsat's lit hooded lantern is OUT.** Fuel preserved at
  **16.5 turns**; `light.py` has it logged `out`, so the burn has stopped.
- Yrtone's spare lantern is unlit and its fuel is still UNKNOWN.
- **They are in total darkness** in a large, irregular, unmapped room.

**What is true and must be adjudicated consistently:**

1. **The wind stops the moment either exit is opened** (module text). Their own
   door is ten feet behind them, spiked with JAMB wedges — prising two spikes out
   is quiet and quick and ends the wind. They do not know that is the cure.
2. **Relighting in the wind is 2-3 rounds plus a roll** (`rules/exploration.md`,
   "from cold while hurried, wet, or in a draught"). From cold and unhurried it is
   1 full round. There is no existing flame to light from — the 1-segment case
   does not apply.
3. **TRISTLE IS THE ONLY ONE WITH INFRAVISION** — elf, 60 ft. Everyone else is
   human and blind. The lantern is genuinely extinguished now, not hooded, so his
   infravision is NOT washed out; allow a short adjustment (rule 1-2 rounds) and
   then 60 ft of heat-sight.
4. The whistling the module mentions is now a **howling** wind and it is LOUD.
   Nothing in the text says it draws monsters, and it is a natural phenomenon —
   do NOT add an extra wandering check for it.
5. The ceiling is 20 ft with two vents; examining it shows the cause. They cannot
   see the ceiling in the dark.

**Do not tell them the fix.** They have been sealing themselves into rooms with
spikes and cloaks all expedition and it has worked every time; this is the first
room where that exact habit is the thing that hurt them. Let them work it out.

**Round 1219 — LIGHT RESTORED.** The player's answer was a body-and-cloak
windbreak around Frython, which is exactly what the draught clause is asking for.
Ruled: shelter earns the low end of the band, **2 rounds**, then d6 failing only
on a 6. **d6 = 4. Lit.** Lantern back at ~16.4 turns and burning again.

**RULING — the wind does NOT re-extinguish it. One check, at onset.** The module
specifies the 50%/80% check only for the moment the wind *"will immediately
result"*; it says nothing about repeating. So a relit and sheltered flame stays
lit, and the party may move about the room. The wind itself continues — loud,
cold, dust in the air — until either exit is opened. Do not re-roll the lantern
every round; that would turn one hazard into an unwinnable attrition.

**They can see the correlation.** The wind began the instant the door seated. That
is plain cause and effect a party would notice, and noticing it is not a hint —
report it as perception. Working out that OPENING a door stops it is theirs to do.

## STOP — AREA 34 GEOMETRY IS WRONG. Narrative paused at round 1221.

**The extents classifier has merged separate spaces across DIAGONAL rock walls.**
`cells.room = 34` covers 41 squares spanning x24-31, y22-31 — and the 200 dpi
render shows the **Room of Pools' circled letter markers h(27,22), i(27,24),
k(29,22), l(29,24), m(29,26) inside that same block**, with a broad diagonal band
of solid rock cutting through it. Area 31's label is at (27,19), area 34's at
(27,26); they cannot both own these squares.

**What the page actually shows around the party at (24,23):**
- heavy vertical wall bar at **x=26**, y=22 to y=24 — (25,23) does NOT open east
- **(24,24) is solid rock**, part of the diagonal band — no way north
- the immediate space is **small**: roughly (24,22),(25,22),(24,23),(25,23)
- an opening **south** at (24,22)->(24,21), measured open (thick 2.0, gap 1.00)

**Two things I narrated are therefore unsafe:**
1. *"The chamber is big… the lamp finds no far wall."* Read off the 41-square
   extent. Probably false.
2. **The area-34 wind hazard was FIRED** (lantern out, d100=41; relit d6=4).
   That hazard belongs to area 34 specifically, and whether the party is in
   area 34 at all is now unestablished. The module's wind also requires BOTH
   exits closed — and there is an open doorless way south at (24,21), which
   would mean the wind should never have started.

**Root cause, again:** narrating from `cells.room` and the extent count instead
of from a verified render. The diagonal-wall handling in `extents.py` has never
been tested on a region like this — CLAUDE.md already flags diagonals as a
sub-grid case, and this quadrant is full of them.

**DO NOT narrate further in this room until the extraction is fixed.** Rebuild
this quadrant, establish the true walls, then reconcile the wind with the player
honestly — including the possibility that it should not have fired.

**Round 1234 — the two chests in area 34's SW corner, both opened, both EMPTY.**

Module text, verbatim: *"In the extreme southwest corner of the room are two
locked chests, but they are empty. Both are large and bulky, as well as heavy."*
That is authored content, not a stocking roll and not a DM choice. There is
nothing in them, no false bottom, nothing under or behind them. If the party
searches, they find nothing, and the search is a clean one.

Tristle's rolls, all once-ever and all now spent on these two:
| | roll | needed | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| chest A, find traps | 84 | 25 | failed — never cleared |
| chest B, find traps | 19 | 25 | **clean, no trap** |
| chest A, open locks | 4 | 35 | **opened** |
| chest B, open locks | 12 | 35 | **opened** |

Neither chest was trapped, so the failed check on A cost nothing this time.

**Running tally at 1st level — every lock he has touched:**
door (23,6,V) made 24 | chest (area 29) FAILED 36 by one | door (23,23,V) made 9 |
chest A made 4 | chest B made 12. **Four of five locks opened.** The only one he
missed is the one with the 25 g.p. plaque in it, which is a good joke and should
be left entirely alone — do not hint that the area 29 chest is worth returning for.

**The stair-detector false positive at (22,19) is fixed** — see the new `feature`
correction scope. (21,21) ALSO reads slots=5 -> `stairs` and is probably the same
kind of furniture misread; not yet verified, do not narrate stairs there.

**Round 1246 — chests searched and shifted. Nothing.**

False-bottom search rolls all FAILED: Tristle d6=6 (needs 1-2), Yrtone 5, Blartho 2
(need 1). Nothing found — and there is nothing to find, so the failures cost
them nothing except certainty.

**Ruled separately, and NOT on a die:** comparing a chest's interior depth to its
exterior height is not a search, it is measurement. Anyone can do it and it
settles the false-bottom question honestly. Given the module says the chests are
simply empty, both measure true — boards to the floor, no discrepancy. Do not
make players roll for arithmetic they can just do.

**Shifting them:** *"Both are large and bulky, as well as heavy"* (module). Dave
at STR 18/26 with Torsat can walk them off the wall a foot at a time. Underneath
is bare dressed rock and undisturbed dust. Nothing beneath, nothing behind.

They scrape loudly on stone — but the room's own wind is howling and no extra
wandering check is warranted (`rules/exploration.md`: extra checks are for
smashing, hammering, the magic mouths, a shouted fight; dragging furniture in a
gale is not on that list).

**Area 34's south-west corner is now exhausted.** The only unexplored part of the
room is the diagonal chamber running north-east, and the arms on its walls.

**Area 34's far door (31,31,V), round 1275. NOT LOCKED** — B1 door rule, d6 = 6.

**Swing, DM call:** hinges on the AREA 34 face; it opens WEST, toward the party,
into area 34. **Pull, not push.** Deliberately the opposite of (23,23,V), which
was ruled hinges-on-the-far-side. The alternation is on record and this dungeon
has both kinds.
Practical: from inside area 34 it takes FLOOR spikes, the strong method; from the
far side only jamb wedges.

**OPENING IT STOPS THE WIND** — module: *"The wind will cease whenever either or
both of the exits is opened."* The party worked out the mechanism themselves by
watching the lamp smoke, so they should expect this. The lantern is in no danger
once it is open.

**Beyond it is area 33.** Do NOT name it, describe it, or hint at its contents
until the door is open and they can see in. Room names are part of what gets
filtered — see the round-1214 leak.

Lamp at **11.3 turns** and dropping; they have burned nearly five turns in this
room. Yrtone's spare lantern is still unlit with fuel UNKNOWN.

**UNSTOCKED** — not in the stocking table. All authored content.

> the stronghold (most of whom left on the last adventure with Rogahn and
> Zelligar). Scattered throughout the room are about 40 common beds, and about

> along various walls, and at the south wall is a large wooden chest of drawers

> vestments, and other similar items of no special value."*

- **SW corner:** the floor slants steeply to the wall and a small opening (too
  small for access) leads into it. Faint smell — *"some kind of crude toilet
  area."*
- Walls rough stone, **torch sconces** and pegs. On the pegs: an old battered
  shield, an empty canteen, a **20 ft section of light chain**, a sheathed sword
  (old and rusty), and a **bearskin**.
- **Nothing of value in the whole room.** No treasure, no monster.

The 200 dpi render shows rows of drawn beds filling roughly **x32-34, y28-31 and

## TOOL BUG — the wandering clock silently STOPPED at round 1200

`clock.py start` pre-rolls `HORIZON = 60` anchors, i.e. **1200 rounds**. Past the
last anchor `check_due` finds nothing and returns an empty list, so the clock kept
advancing and **no wandering check fired between round 1203 and round 1325** —
about six free checks in the party's favour, caused by the tool, not by play.

**Fixed:** `advance` now REFUSES to move past the last pre-rolled anchor and says
why, and a new `clock.py extend --to N` rolls more (hidden, as before). A
wandering clock that stops without saying so is worse than none, because the DM
goes on believing it is running.

**The six missed checks were rolled honestly.** Three produced encounters:

| round | rolled |
|---|---|
| 1244 | Kobolds (1-6) |
| 1285 | Orcs (1-4) |
| 1322 | **Giant Centipedes (1-2), hp 2 and 2** |

**ADJUDICATION — placement rule applied, not softened.** The standing rule is
*"the die says WHAT, never WHERE"* and an encounter needs a verified approach or
it is not placed.

- **1244, kobolds — NO CONTACT.** The party was sealed inside area 34 with BOTH
  its doors shut, one of them spiked, and the room's own wind howling loudly
  enough to mask everything. Nothing could reach them and nothing could hear
  them. The kobolds passed through the corridors outside. They are still out
  there somewhere; do not delete them from the level.
- **1285, orcs — NO CONTACT.** Same: the party had just closed and jamb-wedged
  the barracks door behind them. No approach existed.
- **1322, giant centipedes — THEY ARRIVE. This is essentially now (round 1325).**
  Centipedes are vermin and need no corridor approach: the module puts a small
  opening into the wall in this room's **south-west corner** — *"the floor slants
  toward the wall steeply and an opening (too small to give any access) leads
  into the wall… some kind of crude toilet area."* That is a legitimate origin
  and requires no door.

**Two giant centipedes, hp 2 and 2.** AC 9, HD 1/4, 1 attack, **damage NIL** —
the bite does no hit point damage at all, it delivers poison, save **at +4**
(MM p.14). Never roll damage for a centipede. Morale 50, surprised on 1-2.

## AREA 33 IS NOT A DIAGONAL PROBLEM — and the party is standing in it

The connectivity study corrected an assumption I recorded earlier. Diagonal debt
in x30-35 / y21-33 is **ZERO**; the 45-degree band on the barracks' north-west
flank is mass-face and is correctly walled. Area 33's 5-square extent has a
different and much worse cause, verified directly against the shipped database:

- **39 fabricated `door` edges** in that region
- **15 fabricated `stairs` cell features**
- only **2 of the 4** printed `33` labels are in `b1_key.ROOMS` — the pair at
  (32,24)/(33,24) is missing entirely

All of it drawn from the module's **forty beds**. Ranks of bed frames are ranks
of small rectangles, which is a door glyph; parallel frames are parallel bars,
which is a stair tread. It is the furnished-room failure mode at scale, in the
most heavily furnished room on the level.

**CONSEQUENCE FOR LIVE PLAY, RIGHT NOW:** the party is at (32,31) fighting two
centipedes. **Narrate NOTHING about this room's shape, exits, doors or stairs
from the database.** Describe only what the lantern reaches. Every door and every
stair the DB reports in here should be assumed false until a page render says
otherwise. `doorcheck.py` and the new `feature` correction scope are the tools;
neither has been run over this room yet.

Good news from the same study: the party has **never entered any of the 13
diagonal squares**, and its recorded route up area 34 hugs the NW side the whole
way — legal under every model considered. No in-flight state to preserve.

The dangerous class the study found — **18 square pairs the map calls 2 apart
that are really 6-16, each one a `party.py` move that passes the detour guard and
crosses a printed wall** — is confined to **areas 10 and 11**. Not here. But do
not take the party into areas 10 or 11 until those are corrected.

**Round 1326 — both giant centipedes killed outright, nobody touched.**

Centipedes won initiative (3 vs 2) and both missed: **17 vs 18** on Yrtone (by one)
and 6 on Blartho. Then:
- Yrtone, footman's flail: 11 vs 11 -> HIT, **5 damage**. Centipede A had 2. Dead.
- Torsat, long sword: 17 vs 11 -> HIT, **6 damage**. Centipede B had 2. Dead.
- Tristle **with his LONG SWORD**: 8 vs 11, missed — but note the number. With the
  spear he needs **17** (-3 non-proficient); with the long sword he needs **11**
  (proficient, plus his elf +1). A six-point swing. The player switched him
  deliberately because this is an open room, not a ten-foot corridor where only a
  second-rank spear reaches. Exactly the trade CHECKLIST.md section 3 describes.
- Blartho 3, Dave 5. Missed.

**No bite landed, so NO poison saves were required.** Nobody took a point.

**Pending XP: 2 giant centipedes at 30 + 1/hp, hp 2 each = 32 each, 64 total.**

**BUG FIXED MID-ENCOUNTER (second one this fight):** `combat.py monster` could not
handle an attack whose damage is **"Nil"** — it passed the word to the dice parser
and raised. A centipede's whole attack IS the poison; the MM prints its damage as
Nil. Now handled, with the attack still resolving as a hit for 0 and telling the
caller to go and resolve the special attack. Regression tests added.
That is two live bugs in one fight — fractional hit dice, and nil damage — both in
the same code path, and both hit only because the commonest wandering monsters on
this level are the weird ones.

**Area 33's TRUE shape, read off the 200 dpi render, round 1326 — awaiting player
confirmation the way area 34's was.**

**x 32-34, y 21-31 — three squares wide, ELEVEN long. 33 squares, 30 ft by 110 ft.**
Bounded: heavy wall at x=32's west edge (with the party's door at y=31), solid
rock from x=35 east, wall below y=21, wall between y=31 and y=32.

Beds are drawn across the whole of it, roughly two per square, which matches the
module's "about 40 common beds" and confirms the size.

**This also confirms the connectivity study's finding about the labels**: the
printed `33` appears TWICE on the sheet — at (32,31)/(33,31) and again at
(32,24)/(33,24) — and `b1_key.ROOMS` carries only the first pair. A long room
gets its number printed more than once; the key assumes one anchor per room.
That is a KEY-FILE bug, not an extraction bug, and it will recur on any long room.

**Do not enter these squares in corrections until the player confirms**, exactly
as with area 34. My reading of a render is one reading; theirs is the other.

**LIGHT PROBLEM, flag to the player.** A 33-square room is 33 turns of searching
at the standard 1 turn per 10x10 square. Four searchers cut that, but not to
under about eight turns — and the lantern has **9.9 turns** in it. A full search
of this room is roughly the whole remaining lamp. They have a full spare on
Yrtone plus 26 flasks, so it is a resupply problem rather than a danger, but they
should be told the arithmetic before they commit to it.

**Rounds 1326-1446 — area 33 searched EXHAUSTIVELY. Twelve turns. Nothing found.**

The player committed to a complete search knowing it would cost most of the lamp,
reasoning that both doors were wedged and the time was affordable. That reasoning
was sound and the room was in fact quiet throughout.

- **Twelve wandering checks fired in that span. Not one produced an encounter.**
  (The wandering clock is working again since the horizon fix; these are real
  rolls, all misses.)
- **Nothing was found, and there was nothing to find.** Area 33 is unstocked and
  the module gives it no treasure — only the chest of drawers with old socks and
  worn footwear, and the odds and ends on the pegs.
- Lantern **guttered at round 1384** mid-search. Refilled from a flask in still
  air, relit unhurried in one round. **Oil 22 -> 21 flasks.** Now 17.9 turns.

**Still hanging on the pegs, untaken:** the battered shield, the empty canteen,
the **20 ft coil of light chain**, the rusty sheathed sword, and the **bearskin**.
The chain and the bearskin are the only two with any practical value; do not
point that out.

**The party has now cleared area 34 and area 33 completely and taken:** 5 gp, two
large shields, a footman's mace, a dagger (Frython's first melee weapon), a
quiver of arrows, a good sword, a flail, a hand axe. No coin beyond the 5 gp.

**DM RULINGS — the chain and the bearskin, area 33 pegs. Round 1448.**

Neither is priced or weighed anywhere in PHB, DMG Appendix O or the campaign
cache. I checked. These are DM calls, stated once so they stay consistent:

| item | weight | value | bulk |
|---|---|---|---|
| 20 ft light chain | **150 gp weight (15 lb)** | ~5 gp as scrap iron | compact, coils |
| bearskin | **300 gp weight (30 lb)** | **10 gp**, condition-limited | **very bulky** |

Reasoning, so it can be re-derived: rope 50 ft is 75 gp weight in Appendix O, and
light chain runs about double rope per foot, so 20 ft lands near 150. A folded
robe or cloak is 200; a whole bear hide is bigger and heavier than a cloak, so 300.

**The chain's purpose is NOT determinable and should not be invented.** It is a
plain riveted section with no collar, no lock, no hook and no ring — cut or
unshackled from something longer. In a barracks it could have been a lamp chain,
a tether, kit secured to a frame, anything. If the player asks, the honest answer
is that nobody can tell. Do not turn it into a clue.

**The bearskin is genuinely poor.** The module lists it among "odds and ends" and
does not value it. Ruled: stiff, very dusty, moth-run along one flank and split
where it has been folded over a peg for years. Sound enough to roll and carry but
it would not fetch what a good hide fetches. **10 gp to the right buyer in town,
and it is 30 lb of very awkward bulk to carry 400 feet of dungeon and two days
overland to find that buyer.**

Both are legitimate to take. Neither is worth much. Say the numbers plainly and
let the player weigh it — they have been costing encumbrance carefully all trip.

**Area 33 BARRACKS — outline CONFIRMED BY THE PLAYER, 2026-08-29, and now correct.**

**x32-34, y20-31 = 36 squares, 30 ft by 120 ft. EXACTLY ONE exit: (31,31,V).**

My render reading stopped a row short at y=21; the player's map has the
southernmost row at **y=20**. North edge is y=31, the same max y as area 34 which
leads into it. Perimeter leaks now **zero**, verified programmatically.

Applied: 36 `member` rows, 41 edge corrections, and earlier 39 fabricated doors
and 14 fabricated `stairs` features cleared — all of it generated by the module's
forty drawn BEDS.

**The route out is now the route that exists**: (31,31,V) -> the diagonal ->
(23,23,V) -> (27,12,H) -> (25,10,H). Before the fix `dmap.path` routed the party
out through a fabricated bed-door at (34,20) and crossed SIXTEEN of them.

Walkable 1196 -> 1197 (one y=20 square was misread as solid). Unreached stays 6.

**FOUR rooms in this quadrant have now needed the player's own reading of the
printed map**: 34, 33, and — still outstanding — **10 and 11**. The pattern is
established and it is not going to stop: where a room is heavily furnished or
diagonally walled, the image cannot give its extent and the player's reading is
the second independent source the CHECKLIST already demands. Ask early, not after
narrating.

**Door (21,24,H), round 1472. NOT LOCKED** — B1 door rule d6 = 3.
Swing: hinges on the FAR side, opens AWAY from the party — push. Alternates from
(31,31,V), which was hinged on the near side.
Verified genuine by doorcheck before narration: hole 140, **offset 0.02**, wall 24.

**Tristle's Hear Noise FAILED: d100 = 40 vs 15.** He hears nothing and that
proves nothing (DMG caveat, cached in rules/exploration.md). Note he MAY repeat
it — PHB p.28 says Hear Noise "can be repeated as often as desired", unlike Open
Locks and Find Traps. Each attempt is a full round.

**BEYOND THIS DOOR — READ BEFORE NARRATING FURTHER.** (21,25) is ten feet of
corridor, then (21,26) enters **area 5, WIZARD'S CHAMBER**, which is STOCKED with
the **CARRION CRAWLER**. The DM notes already flag it: *"materially nastier in
1e… AC 3/7 head/body, MV 12", HD 3+1, EIGHT tentacle attacks, paralysis on a
failed save… the single most dangerous placed encounter and can wipe a
1st-level party."* Use MM stats, not the module's flat AC 7 / 6 hp.

The crawler is at least 20-30 ft beyond this door and in the chamber, not against
it, so a SUCCESSFUL listen here would still most likely have returned nothing.
Do not let the failed roll imply otherwise, and do not telegraph.

## Area 5 WIZARD'S CHAMBER — module key (B1 p.9), and a CORRECTION to my own note

**CORRECTION.** The stocking note above says the carrion crawler *"is in Zelligar's
chamber, behind a secret door, so a cautious party need not meet it."* **That is
wrong.** Area 5 is reached by an ORDINARY door — (21,24,H), which the party has
just found unlocked and is about to push open. There is no secret door on this
approach. The most dangerous placed encounter on the level is behind a plain
door at the end of a plain corridor, and the party is standing at it.

**THE CARRION CRAWLER — use MM stats, not the module's.**
AC **3 head / 7 body**, MV 12", **HD 3+1**, **EIGHT tentacle attacks**, each
forcing a save vs paralysis; damage from the tentacles themselves is nil. The
