module's flat AC 7 / 6 hp is the Basic conversion and is far too soft. This can
wipe a 1st-level party — eight saves a round against six characters with 2-9 hp.

**Room contents (x21-25 y27-28, x21-23 y26 — 13 squares):**

- **North wall: a 70-ft stone carving**, Zelligar on a hilltop casting a spell
  over a valley, an army fleeing in panic. Detailed, impressive, not treasure.
- East and west walls plain, with **wall pegs** for garments.
- **SE corner: Zelligar's bed** — ornately carved rosewood, his name in **gold
  leaf** on the headboard. **Cannot be removed without dismantling**, which is
  difficult and likely to damage it. Dismantled: sides and baseboard **100 gp
  each**, headboard **up to 500 gp** — but **80% chance any buyer recognises the
  name**, and then the seller has problems, because it is obvious where it came
  from. That is the real hook: 500 gp of very traceable loot.
- **Rosewood nightstand beside the bed, ONE LOCKED DRAWER.**
  **TRAP: the brass handle has a pin trap.** Grasping it inflicts **1 hp** and an
  oily substance — not poison — causes pain that renders the hand **unusable for
  2-5 (d4+1) TURNS**. **Negated entirely if a key is inserted into the lock
  first** — any key of comparable size will do, and the real key is nowhere in
  the module. The party carries no keys that I know of; check inventory if they
  try. **The drawer is EMPTY.**
- Table, three chairs — no value. On the table a **pewter pitcher (15 gp)** and
  **three pewter mugs (5 gp each)** = **30 gp** of easily portable loot.
- **Area 6 CLOSET lies through a door in this room's SOUTH wall** — the party has
  already stood next to area 6 from the other side, at (22,23).

Rolled treasure for area 5 is the carrion crawler's, per the stocking table; the
above is authored content and stands regardless.

**Round 1473 — door (21,24,H) pushed open. NO CONTACT YET.**

I rolled party surprise (d6=1, surprised) BEFORE establishing that there was
anything to be surprised by. **That roll is VOID and must not be applied.** The
door opens onto (21,25), ten feet of plain corridor; area 5 proper begins at
(21,26), a further ten feet on. The crawler is in the CHAMBER, not in the
passage, and there is no contact at the moment the door opens.

**Roll surprise when contact happens, not when a door opens.** Told the player
the roll was made in error rather than quietly reusing it later.

Lantern reach from (21,24) is 30 ft: they see the passage, the threshold, and the
nearest strip of the chamber floor. The 70-ft carving is on the NORTH wall,
roughly 40 ft off — beyond the light. The bed in the south-east corner is at the
edge of it at best.

**CARRION CRAWLER SIGHTED, round 1477. hp 15. Full stats now in
`bestiary/carrion-crawler.md`, verified from MM p.13 and DMG Appendix E p.197.**

At **(21,28)**, the far north-west corner of area 5 — position ROLLED (d13=9),
not chosen. Party at (21,25) on the threshold, exactly **30 feet** away, which is
the precise limit of lantern reach. They spotted it on a deliberate look
(d6=4, needed 1-4).

**AC 3 head / 7 body. HD 3+1, 15 hp. EIGHT tentacle attacks per round, each a
save vs paralyzation, NO hit point damage. Moves 12" and travels walls and
ceilings as easily as floor. XP 580 + 4/hp = 640.**

**It has not reacted yet.** The party has a moment. Do not take that away from
them and do not hurry them — they asked to look without entering, which was the
right instinct, and they have earned the pause.

**When it does engage, roll surprise properly** (crawler surprised on 1-2), and
remember the party is standing in a TEN-FOOT PASSAGE with a spiked door behind
them. In a corridor only two can face it; in the open room it can reach more of
them. The corridor is their friend here and they may not realise it.

**Do NOT let them fight it at AC 3 by accident** — the head is AC 3, the body
AC 7, and that is a four-point difference. If they declare attacks without
specifying, ask which they are striking at.

**Round 1483 — WANDERING CHECK FIRED MID-OPERATION. ONE giant centipede, hp 2.**

The party was holding still at (21,25) while Tristle unspiked the south door,
wedged it OPEN as a line of retreat, and came back. Placement per the standing
rule: the only two approaches are north (area 5, the crawler's room, which the
party can see) and south (the passage). Placed **SOUTH, coming up the passage
through the door they had just opened.** That is the geometry, not a punishment
for the plan — but it is a genuine consequence of it, and worth noting as fair.

Neither side surprised (party d6=3, centipede d6=6).

**Tristle's operation, all rolled:**
- Move Silently TO the door: **16 vs 30 — silent.**
- Crawler registers the party during the ~6 rounds: **d6=6, no.**
- Move Silently BACK: **45 vs 30 — FAILED, he made noise.**
- Crawler reacts to that noise: **d6=6, no.** Lucky twice.

**The door is now spiked OPEN** and the party's retreat is clear — through the
square the centipede is now occupying.

**THE REAL TENSION, and do not resolve it for them:** fighting anything in this
passage makes noise thirty feet from an unaware carrion crawler. The centipede is
almost harmless (no damage, poison save at +4) but killing it loudly may buy them
the crawler. Let them weigh it.

## Round 1484 — THE CARRION CRAWLER BROKE. It is alive on 2 hp and it fled WEST.

The party opened at 30 ft with everything, on a creature that had not registered
them. Ten missile attacks, five hits, **13 damage of its 15**.

- Dave short bow x2: 15 HIT (2 dmg), 2 miss. STR bonus correctly NOT applied to bow.
- Torsat short bow x2: **NATURAL 20** (4), 19 HIT (2)
- Tristle short bow x2: 12 HIT (2), 18 HIT (3) — at **+3 DEX and +1 elf**, needing 10
- Yrtone sling: 11, miss
- Frython 3 darts at -2 (medium range): 12, 1, 6 — all miss
- Blartho, flail, vs the centipede: 6, miss

**I ruled missiles at 30 ft strike the BODY (AC 7), not the head (AC 3)**, and told
the player so. A nine-foot mass at range is the target; picking the head out of a
nest of moving tentacles at thirty feet is not a called shot a 1st-level party
gets for free.

**Morale: base 50%, +11% for 3+1 HD, +15% for over half its hp gone in one volley.
d100 = 78, adjusted 93 vs 61 — BREAKS BY 32% = FLEE IN PANIC.**

**It is on 2 hit points and it ran WEST**, out of area 5 through (20,28), which is
the only non-wall exit from its corner. Move 12" — 120 ft in a round — so it is
gone from the chamber almost at once.

**IT IS ALIVE AND IT IS STILL WORTH 640 XP.** It will not have gone far, it heals
nothing, and it is somewhere west of area 5 on 2 hp. If the party pursues, that is
their choice; do not steer. If they let it go, note that a wounded crawler
wandering this level is now a live element — and unlike the rat that escaped, this
one can paralyse the whole party if it recovers its nerve.

Ammunition spent: arrows 53 -> 47, darts 15 -> 12, sling bullets ~30 -> ~29.
All recoverable from the chamber floor; the six arrows and three darts are lying
30 ft into area 5.

**The centipede is still in the doorway behind them, untouched, on 2 hp.**

**Rounds 1485-1489 — PURSUIT FAILED, by the book. DMG p.68, Case 1.**

The crawler moves **12"** to the party's **9"** (90 ft/round at combat/flee rate,
rules/exploration.md), so it gains 30 ft every round, and the lantern reaches 30.
It left their perception at about 30 ft — UNDER the 50 ft that would have ended
pursuit immediately under condition B — so the chase was legitimately open and
they were right to try. Rolled whether it blundered back into lamplight: **d6=4,
no.** It is gone.

**It is alive on 2 hp, somewhere west or south of (20,28), and still worth 640 XP.**

**The centipede HIT Blartho** — 18 vs 18, exactly on the number. Nil damage, as
always; the bite is the poison. **Blartho saved: d20 = 18 against a needed 6**
(cleric 1-3 saves 10 vs poison, +4 because the MM calls this poison weak). No
effect.

**THE PARTY IS NOW STRUNG OUT** and should be told plainly: five of them ran
west to (20,28) while Blartho fought a rearguard back through area 5. He is
somewhere behind them with a centipede on him, on 2 hp, holding two of the
party's five remaining cure spells. Do not resolve this for them, but do not
hide it either.

Area 5 was crossed at speed and is NOT searched. Its authored contents — the
70-ft carving, Zelligar's rosewood bed with the gold-leaf headboard (500 gp, 80%
recognition risk), the nightstand with the PIN TRAP on the drawer handle, the
pewter pitcher and three mugs (30 gp) — are all still there, unexamined.
Six arrows and three darts are on its floor.

**Round 1491 — centipede killed outright, party reunited in area 5.**

Party won initiative 4 vs 2, so it never acted again. Four hits of five on a
2 hp creature: Dave 18 -> **10 damage** (7 + his STR 3), Tristle 17 -> 8, Torsat
16 -> 3, Blartho 16 -> 3. Yrtone rolled a natural 1. **24 damage delivered to a
creature with two hit points.**

**Pending XP: giant centipede, hp 2 = 30 + 2 = 32.**

Running pending ledger for expedition 4 now includes: 1 troglodyte (48),
6 kobolds (45), 4 giant rats (uncertain), 3 giant centipedes (32+32+32 = 96),
plus treasure. **The carrion crawler at 640 is the prize that got away.**

**Note for the pursuit precedent:** the party did everything right and still lost
it — they opened their retreat first, held still, spotted it on a careful look,
opened at 30 ft with everything, took 13 of its 15 hp in one volley, and it broke
and outran them because it is simply faster and their light is only 30 ft. That
is the DMG procedure working as written, not a punishment. Say so if they ask.

## Area 5 outline documented by the player, 2026-08-29 — the DB was missing HALF the room

**x17-25, y27-28 (18 squares, 20 ft by 90 ft, NW corner (17,28)), plus (21,26),
(22,26), (23,26) and (21,25) hanging below. 22 squares. THREE doors, all on the
south side: (21,24,H) — the way in — plus (18,26,H) and (23,25,H).**

Confirmed against the 200 dpi render, including all three door glyphs.

**The database held only x21-25 — the ENTIRE WEST HALF was missing**, which is why
I described (20,28) as a corridor junction when it is the middle of the floor, and
why "doors" appeared at (20,27): those are the drawn TABLE and PEWTER MUGS.
Fourth room in a row where the player's reading of the printed map supplied what
the image could not.

**CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS A BIG ONE: the crawler may still be in this room.**

It fled WEST from (21,28). The room's west end is (17,27)/(17,28) — a dead end.
The only other ways out are two closed doors on the south wall, and a
NON-INTELLIGENT creature does not open doors. The south passage was wedged open
at the time, but the party was standing in it.

**So it very likely ran to the west end of a 90-ft dead-end room and is cornered
there on 2 hit points.** And carrion crawlers *"travel a wall or ceiling as easily
as a floor"* — a panicked one in a dead end goes UP.

**The player's declared plan is to advance west scanning the CEILING and WALLS.**
That is exactly the right instinct and it should be rewarded, not punished.

**Round 1502 — THE CRAWLER IS CORNERED AND THE PARTY'S PRECAUTION FINDS IT.**

Position rolled, not chosen: **d4=4 -> (18,27)**, and **d6=3 -> on the WALL**.
(18,27) is the room's southern row, so it is clinging to the SOUTH WALL, right
beside the closed door at (18,26,H) — a non-intelligent animal at a door it
cannot open, in a 90-ft dead-end room, on 2 hit points.

**They spotted it automatically and deliberately so.** The player declared
"proceed carefully west scanning the ceiling and walls" BEFORE knowing where it
was, which is the exact counter to *"a wall or ceiling is as easily traveled as a
floor"*. A precaution that precisely answers a creature's special defence should
be rewarded, not re-rolled. Told the player why.

It is 2 hp, cornered, and worth **640 XP**.

**Round 1503 — CARRION CRAWLER KILLED.** Second volley at 20 ft, four hits of
eleven shots, 16 damage. Dave 13-exactly (4), Torsat 15 (5), Blartho's sling
19 (2), Frython two darts at 19 each (3 and 2). Tristle missed twice, Yrtone
once.

**PENDING XP: 580 + 4/hp x 15 = 640.** The single biggest award available on this
level, and by a wide margin the biggest this party has ever earned. It roughly
doubles expedition 4's whole pending ledger on its own.

Ammunition: arrows 47 -> 41, darts 12 -> 9, bullets ~29 -> ~27. **Recoverable:
six arrows and three darts from the FIRST volley are still on the floor near
(21,28), plus this volley's four arrows, three darts and two stones near (18,27).**

**Treasure type B** is the crawler's rolled entry per the stocking table — resolve
with `tools/treasure.py` if the party searches where it was lairing. It is NOT
carrying treasure on its body; type B is a LAIR type.

**Door (18,26,H) swing — DM call, ROLLED not chosen, round 1517.**

The player asked which way it opens and stated their action would differ either
way ("if it opens into this room and the body is blocking it we'll leave it").
Because the answer decided their move, I rolled it rather than picking:
**d2 = 2 — hinges on the FAR side; it opens SOUTH, away from area 5.**

So the crawler's body does NOT foul it. The corpse lies against the wall beside
the frame, not across the threshold, and the door swings away from the room.
From area 5 it is a PUSH; from area 5 it cannot be held with floor spikes, only
jamb wedges.

Beyond it: (18,26) is a one-square corridor stub, walled east and west, running
SOUTH to (18,25). Not yet seen by the party.

**Precedent worth keeping:** when a swing ruling decides the player's declared
action, roll it in the open instead of choosing. Choosing would be steering.

**Round 1523/1526 — WANDERING CHECK: ONE TROGLODYTE, hp 6.**

Placement per the standing rule: area 5's other two doors are now SPIKED SHUT
((21,24,H) and (18,26,H), the latter jamb-wedged this very turn), so the only
approach is the ONE unspiked door — (23,25,H) — which is precisely where the
party was walking. That is the geometry doing the work, not a contrivance.

Party did NOT hear it: d6=4, needed 1-3.

**BOTH SIDES SURPRISED** (party d6=2, trog d6=1). No free round for anyone.

**RULING on the chameleon:** the bestiary gives troglodytes `Surprises On 4`, but
that bonus is **by sight only** and is a camouflage effect. It gives nothing when
the creature OPENS A DOOR into a lit room at a party already formed up and facing
that door. Used the standard 1-2 and told the player why. Recorded so it is
consistent: chameleon surprise applies in darkness or where the trog is holding
still against stone, not when it walks into lamplight.

**THE STENCH IS COMING.** `bestiary/troglodyte.md`: the revulsion odour is emitted
*"when aroused for battle"* — so it starts the moment it engages, not before.
**Save vs poison or -1 STRENGTH PER MELEE ROUND, cumulative, for 1-6 rounds**,
and the loss lasts 10 melee rounds after the final effect. On a party where Dave's
+1/+3 comes entirely from Strength, that is the real threat here, not its 1-4s.

**Round 1527 — TROGLODYTE KILLED IN THE DOORWAY. Exactly its 6 hit points.**

Initiative TIED at 4, so blows simultaneous. Eleven missiles at 10 ft (everything
at SHORT range including the darts, which reach 15 ft): only two hit —
**Torsat's arrow for 1 and Blartho's sling stone for 5. Six damage into six hit
points.**

**It delivered nothing, and that follows the round-178 precedent exactly:** it had
to cross ten feet to reach anyone and died on the way. *Simultaneity does not
manufacture an attack from a creature that never arrived.* Same distance, same
ruling, applied without being asked.

**THE STENCH NEVER LANDED.** The revulsion odour is emitted *"when aroused for
battle"* and works per melee round of exposure; it was killed in the act of
closing, so there was one heartbeat of it and no mechanical effect. Nobody rolls.
Narrate the smell, not a save.

**Pending XP: troglodyte, hp 6 = 36 + 12 = 48.**

Ammunition: arrows 53 -> 47, darts 15 -> 12, bullets ~30 -> ~28. All recoverable
from the doorway.

**Only ONE came** (1d2=1). **The door it opened stands open at its back**, and
nobody has looked through it. Do not describe what is beyond until they do.

## Area 6 CLOSET — module key (B1 p.9). UNSTOCKED. Zelligar's closet.

Reached by the door on area 5's south wall at (23,25,H) — the one the troglodyte
came through. 4 squares: (22,24) (23,24) (22,25) (23,25).

*"Rather large for a closet, but actually somewhat barren for its size."*

| what | where | value |
|---|---|---|
| Several **bolts of cloth** stacked | one corner | dusty, moth-eaten, **none** |
| **Five garments** hung — coats and cloaks, musty and dingy | one wall | four worthless; **ONE studded with circular bits of PEWTER, up to 15 gp** (also age-damaged) |
| **A wooden stand with FOUR BOOKS** | corner farthest from the door | see below |
| **An oil lantern, empty** | along one wall | unused for decades. **Perfectly usable if fuelled.** A THIRD lantern for a party that has been sweating its light all trip. |
| **A small table with a stack of papers** | another corner | held down by a **stone slab paperweight monogrammed with a fancy Z** |

**The four books** — they belong to the library, room 12:

1. **History** of the civilized area within 100 miles. Common tongue. Nothing remarkable.
2. **Encyclopedia of plants — WRITTEN IN ELVISH.** Illustrations hint at the topic.
   **TRISTLE IS AN ELF AND CAN SIMPLY READ IT.** No spell needed. Do not point this
   out; let the player realise it.
3. **Zelligar's own DIARY**, handwritten in undecipherable runes — *read languages*
   only. Of no practical value, but **a book dealer or scribe would pay up to 50 gp**.
   Selling it carries a **40% chance the sale becomes a rumour**, with people then
   seeking out the finder. Same shape of trap as the bed headboard.
4. **A work on weather**, common tongue, well illustrated, sparse text. Zelligar's
   marginal notes need *read languages* and turn out to be nothing but a student's
   underlinings.

**The papers**: mundane — food inventory, expenses, construction notes, two routine
messages. **The most recent date on any of them is more than THIRTY YEARS ago.**
That is the single most evocative fact in the room and worth giving them plainly:
it dates the disappearance.

**Nothing here is dangerous. No monster, no trap, no rolled treasure.**
Total realisable value: the pewter-studded garment (15 gp) and the diary (50 gp,
with a 40% rumour risk). Plus a working lantern, which is worth more to this party
than either.

**Round 1554 — area 6 searched. Walls CLEAN (Tristle d6=1, a positive result).**

**The troglodyte carried NOTHING.** MM treasure type A is a **LAIR** type; a
wandering individual has nothing on it, and its attacks are claw/claw/bite so it
had no weapon either. Do not roll treasure for it.

Everything in the closet is now findable by the party. **Not yet told to them and
they must ask or act:**
- Book #2 is in **ELVISH** and **TRISTLE CAN JUST READ IT** — no spell. Let the
  player work that out.
- Book #3, Zelligar's diary, is worth **50 gp** to a dealer with a **40% rumour
  risk** on sale. Same trap as the bed headboard in area 5.
- The **empty lantern is serviceable** and this party has been managing light
  anxiously all trip. It is arguably the most useful object in the room.
- The papers' **most recent date is over thirty years ago.** Give them that
  plainly if they read the papers — it dates the disappearance and it is the best
  piece of atmosphere in the room.

**Round 1576 — THE PIN TRAP FIRED ON TRISTLE. 1 hp (now 2/3), right hand
unusable for 3 turns (d4=2, +1).**

The player did the careful thing and it did not help, and the reason is exactly
what the module says: *"a pin trap which will be tripped by anyone **GRASPING**
it."* A ten-foot pole prods, it does not grasp. **The pole test was sound
reasoning that simply did not apply to this mechanism**, and the player should be
told that plainly rather than left thinking they were careless.

**The near-miss is worth showing them once it has fired.** The module: *"If a key
is inserted into the lock before the handle is grasped, the trap will be negated.
Any key of a size comparable to the actual key will accomplish this function."*
Their declared sequence was pole -> open -> **then** pick the lock. **Reversing
the last two steps would have negated it** — picks in the keyhole are a
key-shaped object in the lock. They can see this now on the sprung mechanism.
Do NOT present it as a gotcha; present it as what the sprung trap shows.

**Consequence that matters more than the hit point:** picking a lock takes two
hands — tension bar and pick. **Tristle cannot pick anything for 3 turns.**
And the drawer is still locked, and still EMPTY.

Tristle is on **2/3 hp** and remains the party's only thief.

**Round 1602 — wandering check gave GIANT RATS (2-5). VOIDED. No approach exists.**

Verified against the map before placing, per the standing rule *"the die says WHAT,
never WHERE — verify an approach exists"*:

| area 5's exit | state |
|---|---|
| (21,24,H) south passage | **SPIKED SHUT** |
| (18,26,H) west south-wall door | **SPIKED SHUT** (jamb-wedged this turn) |
| (23,25,H) east south-wall door | open — but it leads only to **area 6, a FOUR-SQUARE DEAD END** the party searched exhaustively ten minutes ago and found empty |

**There is no way in.** Two spiked doors and a cul-de-sac. The encounter is voided,
not deferred — the rats are somewhere else on the level and never reached them.

**This is the party's caution paying off exactly as designed**, and it is worth
telling them so afterwards. They have spiked every door behind them all
expedition; this is the first time it has demonstrably eaten an encounter.

**THE LAMP IS ALMOST OUT — 1.9 turns, gutters at round 1625.** They are resting
and not watching it. Yrtone's spare is unlit with fuel UNKNOWN, and they carry 21
flasks plus the empty lantern recovered from area 6. Tell them the state; do not
let it gutter unannounced during a rest.

**Round 1609 — THE SCROLL OF *SLEEP* FOUND. Tristle picked the drawer, d100=28 vs 35.**

**DM ruling on placement, and it is the module's own invitation.** B1 says the
nightstand drawer is *"empty (unless treasure in this room is to be located
within the drawer)"* — an explicit hook. Area 5's rolled treasure is **code BB:
a scroll of one magic-user spell, *sleep***, and the drawer is the only concealed,
locked, trapped container in the room. Putting it anywhere else would waste both
the hook and the trap. **The scroll was in the drawer.**

**This is the best thing the party has found in four expeditions**, arguably
including the ring. *Sleep* at 1st level affects 2-16 hit dice of creatures under
4+1 HD with **no saving throw** — it would have taken the whole kobold band, or
every troglodyte they have met, out of a fight in one action.

**Frython's options, when he asks:**
- **Cast it from the scroll once**, and it is gone.
- **Copy it into his spellbook** — DMG "Acquisition of Magic-User Spells". His
  chance to know any given spell is **75%** (INT 17), and `party_roster.md`
  already says *"This is how he gets sleep."* A FAILED roll means he can never
  learn that spell, and the scroll is consumed either way.
That is a real decision and it is his to make. Do not steer.

The trap cost Tristle 1 hp and half an hour. The drawer paid for it.

**Round 1622 — wandering check gave GIANT CENTIPEDES (1-2). VOIDED, second time.**

Same verification as round 1602: (21,24,H) spiked, (18,26,H) spiked, (23,25,H)
open only onto area 6, a four-square dead end the party emptied. **No approach.**

Centipedes are vermin and DID come out of a floor drain in the barracks — but
area 5 has no such feature. The module describes no opening, drain or crack here.
Inventing one to force the encounter through would be exactly the kind of ad-hoc
placement the standing rule forbids.

**Two voids in a row is NOT generosity — it is the sealing discipline working.**
A party that spikes every door behind it in a stone room SHOULD be safe from
wanderers. What it costs them is time and oil, and they are paying both: this
search has already eaten a flask. Say so plainly if they ask; do not quietly
start letting things through to keep the pressure up.

**Carving search, first pass: all four rolls FAILED** (Tristle 3 needing 1-2;
Torsat 4, Yrtone 2, Blartho 5, all needing 1). Nothing found and nothing proven.
**There is in fact nothing in the carving** — the module gives it no secret door
and no treasure — but the party cannot know that and a failed search proves
nothing. They have covered roughly the first third of the 70 feet.

**Lamp doused deliberately at 0.1 turns and refilled rather than allowed to
gutter — Frython's standing job, executed as the player set it up. Oil 21 -> 20.
Now 24 turns, gutters round 1864.**

**Round 1649 — the carving fully worked. One stretch PROVEN clean, two not.**

| stretch | result |
|---|---|
| west 25 ft | all four failed (3,4,2,5) — unproven |
| **middle 25 ft** | **Tristle d6=2 — CLEAN. Positive result, nothing there.** |
| east 20 ft | all four failed (5,5,5,2) — unproven |

**There is nothing in the carving anywhere.** B1 gives it no secret door and no
treasure; it is scenery, and magnificent scenery at that. But the party has
proven only the middle third and must decide for themselves whether to keep
grinding at the other two — at a turn a stretch and oil burning the whole time.

**Do not hint that further searching is pointless.** A failed search proving
nothing is the whole texture of this; they have already spent one flask on this
room and the honest position is that they cannot be sure.

Area 5 is now effectively exhausted apart from the BED, which is 500 gp of
gold-leafed headboard that cannot leave the room without dismantling, will be
damaged in the process, and carries an 80% chance any buyer recognises the name.

**Round 1656 — door (18,26,H) opened. A narrow corridor runs SOUTH.**

(18,26) -> (18,25) -> (18,24), each one square wide, walled east and west, then
(18,23) which is **area 7, WIZARD'S ANNEX**. Lantern reach is 30 ft, so the party
sees the first three squares and nothing of area 7. Do not describe area 7.

**TWO WARNINGS FOR THE NEXT FEW MINUTES OF PLAY:**

1. **AREA 7 IS STOCKED — Bandits (1-4), hp 7, 5, 4, 2.** Entry M20. Four men with
   weapons is a far more dangerous proposition for this party than anything they
   have fought since the berserkers, and bandits are MEN: they can be talked to,
   they can parley, they take morale, and Blartho's *command* works on them.

2. **AREA 7'S EXTENT IS ALMOST CERTAINLY WRONG.** `cells.room='7'` holds **ONE
   SQUARE**. The module calls it *"the unusually-shaped annex"* with *"the
   triangular widening at the south end"* — i.e. DIAGONAL WALLS, in the exact
   quadrant the design study flagged. **Do not narrate its shape from the
   database.** Render the page, or ask the player for their reading as with
   areas 34, 33 and 5. Four rooms have needed that already.

## Area 7 WIZARD'S ANNEX — module key (B1 p.10). STOCKED: Bandits (1-4), hp 7,5,4,2

*"The unusually-shaped annex… apparently for meditation and study, as well as the
practice of magic spells."* The **triangular widening at the south end** was for
that purpose, the stone wall there is **thicker than elsewhere** (not noticeable),
and **the floor near the south wall is bumpy and darkly discoloured, as if charred
and partially melted by intense heat** — a wizard's practice range.

### THE TREASURE IS AN ILLUSION. This is the set piece of the room.

> *"At the south end of the room is a magnificent sight visible when explorers
> enter the door and cast light within… two large wooden chests, each studded
> with jewels, overflowing with riches. A pile of gold pieces is arrayed around
> and within them, and scattered among this treasure trove is an assortment of
> glittering gems and jewels."*
>
> *"The massive treasure is in reality a permanent Illusion, and it will be
> temporarily dispelled as soon as the first bit of 'treasure' is touched by any
> creature. The illusion, once dispelled, reappears in the same place again
> within 24 hours."*
>
> *"In reality the room is empty (and it is recommended that no treasure be
> placed here)."*

**The charring is NOT visible until the illusion is dispelled.** Describe the hoard
exactly as written and with a straight face. Do not hedge it, do not hint, do not
let a description of "unnaturally still" or "too good to be true" creep in. The
module wants the party to believe it, and the moment a hand touches it and the
whole thing vanishes is the entire point.

**No saving throw is mentioned and none should be offered.** It is a permanent
illusion, not a spell being cast at them.

### The bandits, and the state RIGHT NOW

**Four bandits, hp 7, 5, 4, 2.** Men — they can parley, they take morale, and
Blartho's *command* works on them. They are also almost certainly here for the
same reason the party is, and they have been looking at that "treasure".

**Round 1657 rolls:**
- **They HEARD the door groan open** (d6=4, 1-4 hears). They are alert.
- **They HAVE a light burning** (d6=1).

**So: they heard, and they will douse it.** Four men who hear a door open forty
feet off in a dungeon do not sit in the light waiting. The party stepping into
the corridor sees the glow ahead **go out**. That is a fair and honest signal —
it tells the party something is there and that it knows about them, which is
exactly the situation, and it costs the bandits their own night vision too.

**Round 1658 — DAVE HAILED THE DARK. Encounter reaction rolled (DMG p.63, now
cached in `rules/combat.md`): d100 = 61 = "Uncertain, but 55% prone toward
positive."** Dave's CHA is 10, so no adjustment.

**They will TALK.** Not attack, not ambush — talk, warily, from cover, with
weapons in their hands.

**DM colour to have ready, and it explains everything about this room:** these
four have been camped in the annex looking at two jewelled chests overflowing
with gold. **It is the permanent illusion.** Whether they have touched it yet is
the question that shapes the parley:
- If they HAVE, it vanished in their hands and reappeared within 24 hours, and
  they are frightened, arguing, and half-convinced the room is cursed.
- If they have NOT, they are still guarding a fortune they cannot believe their
  luck about, and they will be extremely reluctant to let six armed strangers
  near it.

Either way they are MEN: they can be reasoned with, they take morale, *command*
works on them at 10 ft, and they are worth more to the party as a source of
information about this level than as 4 XP each.

**Do NOT resolve the illusion for them. Let the party walk into it exactly as
written.**

**Round 1660 — the parley opens. Two rolls behind it:**

- **d6=3: they engage** (1-4). Dave met their demand — held position, offered
  words — which is the right move against an "uncertain, leaning negative" party.
- **d6=3: THEY HAVE ALREADY TOUCHED THE HOARD** and watched it vanish in their
  hands. It reappeared within 24 hours, as the module says it does.

**So these four are standing guard over a pile of treasure they have watched
disappear and come back.** They are not greedy any more; they are frightened, and
they have talked themselves into staying because they cannot bear to walk away
from it and cannot bring themselves to touch it again.

**THEY WILL NOT EXPLAIN IT.** No bandit explains his treasure to six armed
strangers, and none of them has the word "illusion". What a frightened man says
is something TRUE that sounds like a lie — *"there's nothing down here worth
having"* — and the party will hear it as a bandit warning them off a hoard.

**That is the trap working exactly as designed. Do not clarify it.** If the party
walks in and reaches for the gold, it vanishes at the first touch and they get
the charred floor instead. Let them.

Bandits are AC 7 (leather+shield typical), HD 1, hp 7/5/4/2, and worth talking to
about the level — they came in from somewhere and know at least their own route.

## THE BANDITS OF AREA 7 — named NPCs, expedition 4, round 1662

The party parleyed instead of fighting and it went well. **Keep these four
consistent; they are now characters, not a stat line.** Entry M20, hp 7/5/4/2.

| name | who | hp | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hask** | grey-stubbled, ~40, the speaker and leader | 7 | scalp cut bleeding into one eye |
| **Dellie** | the woman, spear | 5 | sharp, argued against showing the party the hoard |
| **Corm** | the fourth, hand axe | 4 | quiet |
| **Sarn** | sitting, **broken leg** | 2 | cannot walk; broke it trying to get back to the road |
| *Kell* | **dead**, buried in a passage behind them | — | they will not go back down that passage |

**Their story, all true:** came in off the north road three days ago on the same
rumours the party did. Lost Kell. Sarn's leg went on the way out. They have been
sitting in front of the hoard for two days, unable to take it and unable to leave.
**They touched it and watched it vanish, and watched it come back the next
morning.** They have no word for "illusion" and told the party the plain truth
expecting not to be believed.

**DAVE CALLED IT CORRECTLY** — *"too good to be true… we're sitting right off the
bed chamber of that famous wizard… I'm guessing it's one of his tricks."* That is
exactly right and it is a genuinely good deduction from the geography. **Do not
confirm it outright** — nobody in the fiction knows for certain — but the bandits
will find it a great deal more plausible than anything they have managed to think
of in two days, and it will move them.

**Yrtone has ONE cure left and Blartho has TWO.** The party has offered one for
Sarn's leg. A cure light wounds heals 1-8 hp; **it does not set a broken bone.**
Ruling when it comes up: it will take him off death's door and let him limp, not
make him whole. Say so before the spell is spent, not after.

**Round 1665 — wandering check: GIANT RATS. VOIDED. Third in a row, and it is
STRUCTURALLY correct, not generosity.**

The party has sealed an entire connected POCKET and is inside it:

- **area 7** (annex): only exit is north via (18,24)-(18,26). Both triangular
  wings, (17,23) and (19,23), are walled dead ends — verified.
- that corridor leads to **area 5**, whose perimeter was verified at ZERO leaks
  with exactly three doors: **(21,24,H) spiked shut**, **(18,26,H)** (the one they
  just came through), and **(23,25,H)** opening only onto **area 6, a four-square
  cul-de-sac** they emptied.

**Nothing can reach them.** Areas 5, 6 and 7 form one sealed volume with one
spiked door on it.

**Note for consistency:** the bandits came in three days ago, before the party
spiked anything, and Kell is buried in a passage "behind them" — i.e. their route
in, through area 5 and out one of its doors. **They are now sealed in with the
party.** If they leave, they leave the way the party came.

Three voids is not the wandering system failing. It is a party that has spiked
every door behind it for four expeditions standing in the middle of the
consequence. What it costs them is time and oil, and they have burned two flasks
and six hours in this pocket already.


## Round 1668 — THE POLE TRIPPED THE ILLUSION. Dave's plan, and the ruling behind it

**The player's declared action:** *"why don't we all stand back and try to prod
this thing with our 10' poles — we most of us can be well clear, we can poke at it
directly or the floor under it, the walls behind it."*

They have exactly three ten-foot poles (Dave, Torsat, Tristle) and this is the
best possible use any of them has been put to.

### RULING — a pole in a creature's hand IS contact

B1 p.10: *"temporarily dispelled as soon as the first bit of 'treasure' is touched
by **any creature**."* A pole is not a creature. But the creature is touching the
treasure **through** the pole, and there is no reading of a permanent illusion in
which it survives being physically interfered with by a man ten feet away and not
by the same man's hand.

**It trips.** Made silently, as the player has asked; recorded here.

The alternative reading — that the illusion is keyed to flesh — would punish the
single most correct piece of dungeoneering this party has done in four
expeditions, and would teach the player that caution is worse than recklessness.
That is the wrong lesson to teach with a ruling.

### What the poles actually find, and the ORDER it comes in

1. **The pole meets nothing.** Not resistance, not a chest lid — nothing. Then the
   whole hoard is gone, all at once, in the time it takes to blink.
2. **Only now is the floor visible** (B1 p.10: bumpy, darkly discoloured, as if
   charred and partially melted by intense heat). The bandits never saw this.
   They touched it with their hands, it vanished, and they were looking at their
   own hands and at each other, not at the floor. **This is the new information
   the party bought with the poles, and it is genuinely theirs.**
3. **The south wall is thicker than elsewhere and this is NOT noticeable**
   (B1 p.10, explicit). Poles on the wall find solid stone. Do not let a hollow
   note, a soft spot or a draught creep into the description. There is nothing
   there and the book says so: *"In reality the room is empty (and it is
   recommended that no treasure be placed here)."*
4. **It reappears within 24 hours** — i.e. by about **round 2600**. Added to
   PENDING EVENTS. Do not tell them. If they are still here, they get to watch it
   come back, which is worth more than any explanation.

### The bandits watching

They **expect** the vanishing — they have had it happen under their own hands and
they will flinch when it goes. What they have never had is the look at the floor
afterwards, and Dave's guess about Zelligar the round before now has a charred
practice range under it. **This is the moment they start believing the party.**

Hask, Dellie and Corm are on their feet; Sarn is on the floor with a leg that has
stopped rotting and still will not carry him.

### Area 7's extents are LABEL-ONLY in the database

`select x,y from cells where room=7` returns exactly **one** square, (18,23).
The annex is an unusually-shaped room with a triangular south end and the
extractor never filled it. **Narrate area 7 in FEET, not coordinates**, and get
the player's outline before running any movement inside it — the same failure as
areas 34, 33 and 5, caught before it cost anything this time.


## Round 1674 — Dave offers to take them out. FOUR ROLLS, and they cut against the mood

Dave: *"Are you all heading for the exit, do you want help getting Sarn there? Or
are you doing something else?"*

| roll | for | result |
|---|---|---|
| **d100 = 8**, +25 loyalty adj (a cure spent on Sarn; the hoard proven honest in front of them) = **33** | DMG p.63 reaction to the offer | **Uncertain, but 55% prone toward negative** |
| **d100 = 75** | resolving that 55/45 | **falls positive** (56-00) |
| **d6 = 6** | will they go back out their own way in, past where Kell lies? (1-2 yes) | **NO. They will not.** |
| **d6 = 3** | do they know a second route to the surface? (1-3 yes) | **YES** |

**Read the reaction honestly.** A 33 after a warm parley is not hostility and not
betrayal — it is a **baulk**. They hesitate, Dellie's wariness comes back up, and
then it lands on the positive side of the line. The substance (d6=2 on what they
are baulking at): **they will not leave yet.**

### What that means in their mouths

- **Sarn cannot be carried a mile of dungeon** on a leg that has stopped rotting
  and is still broken. They need a litter and they need a night. Hask will not
  hand his crippled man to six strangers to be carried; he will accept **help
  making a litter**, which is a different thing and he knows the difference.
- They came in three days ago for a fortune and are leaving with **nothing and one
  man short**. Hask is about 40 and this is the part he cannot say out loud.
- The positive resolution means they want to go out **WITH the party, not ahead of
  it**. If the party will have them, they will wait and travel together.

### The second route — what it IS and what it is NOT

**B1 has exactly one entrance.** Area 35 EXIT CAVE is a secret rear exit and it is
**one-way, egress only** (B1 p.24: *"allows only egress from the stronghold, never
entrance… no way to trigger the door from the outside"*). They did not come in
that way and they do not know it exists.

So the d6=3 resolves as a **second route through the corridors back to the same
main entrance**, avoiding the passage where Kell is. That is true, it is useful,
and it costs the module nothing.

### KELL'S BODY IS UNPLACED — do not name a room for it

I have not put Kell anywhere on the map and must not do it under narrative
pressure; that is the exact failure mode that cost six geometry errors. Let Hask
describe the place in what a frightened man remembers — a smell, a shape, how many
turns off the entry hall — not a room number. **Constraints when it is finally
placed:** on a plausible path from the entrance toward areas 5/7, and **NOT in
areas 10 or 11**, whose geometry is still broken.


## Rounds 1696-2176 — THE NIGHT IN AREA 5, and a ruling that changes the void rule

Eight hours, four watches of two, the whole party plus four bandits. Player set
the watch order deliberately around DMG p.40, which is exactly the right reason to
set a watch order: **Yrtone and Blartho took the FIRST watch and then slept six
straight hours; Frython took the LAST and slept the first six.** All three casters
clear the 4-hour minimum with a continuous block. Nothing to adjudicate — they did
it properly.

`clock.py rest --hours 8 --checks 3` (rules/exploration.md: three per night camped
inside, not the 1-in-20 schedule). Watch 1 **d6=1 -> KOBOLDS**. Watches 2 and 3
empty.

### THE VOID RULE WAS TOO BROAD. This is the correction.

Three wandering rolls in this pocket have been voided for want of an approach and
**that was right for area 7**, which is a true dead end. It is **NOT** right for
area 5. `dmap.py at 1 21 24` shows the far side of the spiked door is a live
corridor running south to (21,23) and out into the rest of the level.

**A spiked door is a BLOCKED approach, not an ABSENT one.** The die still says
what; the map still says where; and where, here, is *at the door*. Voiding it
would have handed the party a guarantee that sealing a pocket makes them
invisible, which is not what spiking a door does and is a much weaker game than
the one where something comes and tries the handle.

**Standing refinement, added to CHECKLIST.md:** void only when there is no path at
all. When the path exists but is barred, run the encounter *on the far side of the
bar* and let the barrier do its work.

### What happened, rolled in the open

| roll | for | result |
|---|---|---|
| d6 = 2 | how many kobolds (table 1-6) | **TWO**, hp 4 and 3 off the table's list |
| d6 = 1 | do they investigate the closed door? (1-4 yes) | **yes** |
| d6 = 4 | does the hooded lantern show light under it? (1-2 yes) | **no** |
| d6 = 2 | persistence (1-2 shove and move on, 3-4 linger, 5-6 fetch more) | **shove it, move on** |
| d6 = 2 | do they hear the sleepers through it? (1-3 yes) | **YES** |

Two kobolds, in the dark on infravision, no light to draw them — they simply came
to a shut door on their own level and pushed it. Two spikes and a 1/2-HD kobold is
no contest. They gave up and went.

**But they heard.** That is the part that matters and it is now a pending event.

### PENDING — the kobolds know

Two kobolds went away from (21,24,H) knowing something large and alive is behind
it. Kobolds are not solitary and B1's level has more of them. **When the party
next opens that door, or next moves through that corridor, roll for whether the
word has spread** — a watched corridor, an ambush at the door, or nothing,
depending on how long they leave it. Do NOT pre-place anything; roll it when the
party's action makes it live. Added to PENDING EVENTS.

**The party has not been told the kobolds heard them.** Yrtone and Blartho were on
watch and heard the door shoved; they know something tried it and failed. They do
not and cannot know it heard them back.


## Round 2206 — one GIANT CENTIPEDE is loose in the spine corridor. PLACED, not yet met

Wandering check during breakfast: **d6=1, then d6=2 -> Giant Centipedes (1-2)**.
Rolled **1d2 = 1**: a single centipede, **hp 2**, AC 9, MV 60' (20'), poison bite,
ML 7.

Applying the corrected void rule (blocked is not absent): area 5's one live door
(21,24,H) now carries **four** spikes, and a giant centipede is about a foot long,
so the honest question is whether it comes **under** the door. **d6 = 4 — it does
not.** It is on the corridor side and the party at breakfast has no way to know.

**PLACED:** in the north-south spine somewhere around **(21,22)-(22,20)**, which is
the stretch the party must walk the moment they leave.

> ### THIS PARAGRAPH IS THE ERROR. Corrected 2026-08-30, player-caught.
>
> Placing it "on their route" **banked** the encounter. The check fired at round
> **2183**; the party met the thing at **2220**, 37 rounds later and two rounds
> before the kobold check fired at 2222. On the clock the two encounters are 39
> rounds apart. At the table they were three.
>
> **Correct resolution at 2183:** a centipede is in the corridor, the party is
> sealed in area 5 at breakfast, it cannot reach them — **the check is SPENT, and
> the corridor is empty when they open the door at 2220.** A creature with MV 15"
> does not hold one flagstone for four turns.
>
> **The kill stands** (32 XP, four arrows, no damage taken) — it cost the party
> nothing and unwinding it would take away a payoff they earned with a good
> formation choice. It stands as a gift, not because it was run correctly.
>
> Standing rule now in `CHECKLIST.md` and `rules/exploration.md`: **resolve a
> check at the round it fires, or not at all.**

At AC 9 and 2 hp anything in this party kills it on a hit. The danger is not the
centipede, it is **the save vs poison** on a bite. MM: the giant centipede's poison
is weak — **+4 on the saving throw** — but a 1st-level party failing a save vs
poison is a dead character, not a wounded one. Read the MM entry before it bites.


## KELL IS PLACED — (17,21), and the CARRION CRAWLER killed him

Forced by the party asking Hask to approve a route. Placed from map facts, not
invented under pressure, and written down before it was spoken aloud.

**The bandits' route in** is real and on the page: in the front entrance, area 1
ALCOVES, **south down the x=16 corridor** to (16,19)-(16,23), then **east** along
(17,20)-(18,20)-(19,21)-(20,21) to join the north-south spine at **(21,22)**, and
north up it to area 5's door. Verified against the extraction. It is also the only
way they COULD have come: the secret door at (17,13,V) is **one-way**, operable
only from the (18,13) dead-end side, and the party spiked it open in expedition 3.

**Kell lies under a cairn of loose stone in the dead-end stub at (17,21)**, off the
east-west leg of that route. One square, one exit, east to (18,21). Exactly the
kind of hole four frightened men stack rocks in when they have no time.

### THE CARRION CRAWLER KILLED HIM. The party has already killed it.

Three days ago, in the dark, on the east-west leg. It came down off the ceiling —
which is what they do, MM p.13, they travel walls and ceilings — took Kell third
in line, paralysed him with a tentacle, and had begun on him before the other four
drove it off with torches. They got the body back. There was not all of it.

**This is why Hask will not go west and cannot be argued into it**, and it is why
he will not say what happened. He has not got the words and he does not want them.

### THE PAYOFF — hold it until the party offers it

The party killed a carrion crawler at round ~1484-1500 and examined the body. **It
is almost certainly the same animal.** The moment anyone describes it to these four
— the length of it, the tentacles, that it walks on the ceiling — Hask will know,
and the party will be able to tell four people that the thing that ate their friend
is lying dead in a corridor two rooms away.

**Do not hand this to them.** It only lands if the player reaches for it. If they
mention the crawler, let Hask react and do not soften it.

### The routes DO overlap, at exactly one square

The party's proposed road out — south down the spine from (21,24) to (22,13), then
west along y=13 to the secret door — shares **(21,22)** with the bandits' road in,
and nothing else. Hask can honestly say "our ways part at that corner." He has
never been north of the spine and has never heard of the secret door; his surprise
at it is real.


## Round 2220 — the centipede died in the free round. Four arrows, one hit

Party not surprised (d6=5), centipede surprised (d6=2). Free round. Only **two**
characters had a shot and the ruling matters:

**Torsat (front rank) and Tristle (second rank) could fire; Frython, Yrtone and
Blartho could not.** The target was a foot-and-a-half of vermin **on the floor at
the base of the wall**, ten feet past a front rank of two armoured men filling a
ten-foot corridor. Tristle shooting through the gap between Dave and Torsat is
fair; a dart or a sling bullet from the third and fourth ranks at a target that
LOW and that CLOSE is shooting at Dave's back. Ruled aloud, and it is the ruling
that made the player's formation change actually pay.

Short bow **fire rate 2** (PHB p.38), short range **5" = 50 ft indoors**, so ten
feet is unmodified short range. Four arrows:

| shot | roll | needs | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| Torsat | 8 | 11 | miss |
| Torsat | 9 | 11 | miss |
| Tristle | 6 | 8 (12 base, +3 DEX, +1 elf) | miss |
| **Tristle** | **16** | 8 | **HIT, 1d6 = 4** |

Four on a 2 hp creature. It is not wounded, it is destroyed.

**No save vs poison was ever in play** — it never got to bite. That is what the
free round bought, and it is worth saying nothing about, because the party does
not know how close the margin was and does not need to.

**XP: 30 + 1/hp = 30 + 2 = 32.** Added to the pending ledger, unawarded until they
emerge (B1's own rule).

**Arrows:** Torsat 12 -> 10, Tristle 12 -> 10. Three on the corridor floor, one in
the carcass; all four recoverable if they stop for them.

**ARMOUR CORRECTION, player-supplied:** Tristle has been **wearing** his leather
for a long time. `party_roster.md` still had "Leather 5 (carried, not worn)" and
AC 6; `campaign/characters.tsv` had the right number all along (**AC 4** = leather
8 with DEX 18's -4). Roster fixed. combat.py reads characters.tsv, so nothing
resolved in play was ever wrong -- only the human-facing sheet.


## Round 2222 — THREE KOBOLDS AT HASK'S CORNER. Live, unresolved

Wandering check fired the moment the party stopped to pick up arrows at (21,23).
d6=1, then d6=3 -> **Kobolds**. **1d6 = 3** of them, hp **4, 3, 3** off the table's
list. AC 7, HD 1/2, D 1-4 or weapon -1, MV 90' (30'), **ML 6**.

**Approach rolled, not chosen: d6 = 1 -> the WEST passage into (21,22).** Which is
Hask's road in. Which is the road he will not take. The dice put them there; I did
not.

**Intent (d6=3, 1-2 attack / 3-4 hold and watch / 5-6 pull back to report): they
HOLD AND WATCH from the dark.** They are not charging ten people. They are also
not leaving.

**Party surprise d6=3 -> not surprised**, so the party sees them. Kobolds have
infravision and no light; the party has a lantern; the kobolds saw the glow long
before the party saw anything, and that is why they had time to stop. Placed at
about **(19,22)-(20,22)**, twenty to thirty feet down the west passage, right at
the ragged edge of the lantern's thirty-foot reach.

**This is almost certainly last night's pair plus one.** The pending event rolled
d6=3 six hours ago — *they told others, nothing was set on the corridor.* Nothing
was set. Something was **told**, and now three of them have come to look. Do not
say so; let it be inferred.

### If it goes to talk

DMG p.63 reaction applies only once a character speaks. Kobolds are men-sized
enough to parley in the abstract but they are hostile humanoids on their own
ground; roll it straight if Dave hails them, no thumb on the scale.

### If it goes to blows

Three kobolds against six PCs, an armed Dellie, and two men who can drop a litter
is not a contest. **ML 6** — they break early. The real risk is only this: Frython
is on 2 hp, Blartho on 2, Tristle on 3, and a kobold does **1-4**. One lucky spear
in the rear ranks kills somebody outright. Do not narrate this as trivial.

### CLOCK NOTE

`clock.py` was advanced 15 rounds for the march south **before** this check was
resolved; it reads **2237** while the narrative stands at **2222**. The march has
not happened. Reconcile when the encounter resolves -- if they push on south the
15 rounds are spent anyway and the books agree again.


## Round 2263, outside the cave mouth — THE BANDITS CANNOT GO INTO THAT TOWN

Dave offered to walk them to town together and asked whether to move now or wait
for light. Three rolls:

| roll | for | result |
|---|---|---|
| **d100 = 53, +30** loyalty adj = **83** | DMG p.63 reaction to the offer | **Friendly, immediate action** (76-95) |
| **d6 = 1** (1-2 yes) | is there a reason they cannot or will not go to that town? | **YES** |
| **d6 = 2** (1-3 now) | push on tonight or wait for sunrise? | **move NOW** |
| **d6 = 2** (1-2) | what the reason IS | **they have robbed travellers on this very road; faces will be known** |

**The +30 is what makes this scene work.** At "friendly, immediate action" Hask
does not hide it and let the party walk him into a town where somebody points at
him. He **tells them**, unprompted, before they have gone a mile. A hostile or
uncertain roll would have had him keep quiet and the party would have found out
in the worst possible way.

**Do not soften what they are.** They are road bandits and they have taken things
off travellers on the road the party itself has walked three times. That is the
same road, and the party has been lucky. Hask will not perform contrition and
should not be written performing it — he says what is true and lets the party
decide what to do about it.

**Why he wants to move at night:** partly he wants distance from that cave mouth,
and partly a road at eleven at night has nobody on it to recognise anyone. He will
not say the second part unless pressed.

### The dilemma this hands the player, stated plainly

**Sarn needs a bonesetter and the bonesetter is in the town the other three cannot
enter.** The two men who can carry his litter are two of the men who cannot be
seen carrying it. Options the party may reach for — do not steer them:

- take Sarn in themselves and leave the other three outside
- vouch for them, which puts the party's own standing in that town on the table
  (three expeditions of custom: lodging, a fence for salvage, an outfitter)
- find a bonesetter, farmhouse or wise-woman short of the town
- pay someone
- part ways at the edge and wish them luck

**No wilderness encounters on the road** — standing rule, CHECKLIST.md. Travel is
narrated.

### Still pending on arrival

- **Torsat's disease.** The bite was round 398, day 1. Day 4 is roughly a day and
  a half from here, i.e. **shortly after they reach town**. The player is playing
  blind by choice: describe a man who notices a rash and then a fever. Do not name
  the roll or the duration.
- **Treasure XP** settles on sale, not on exit.
- **Frython and the scroll of *sleep*** — the player has said copy it in town.


## On the road, round ~2270 — ALL FOUR TAKE DAVE'S OFFER. Rolled per person

Dave offered to vouch for them in town in exchange for giving up the road for
good, and explicitly gave them permission to refuse. Reaction rolled **separately
for each of the four** (DMG p.63; Dave CHA 10, no adjustment; **+35** loyalty
adjustment for the cure, the food, the honest hoard, the walk out, and for
meeting Hask's confession with grace instead of a drawn sword).

| who | d100 | +35 | band | answer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Hask** | 99 | 134 | 96+ | **Enthusiastically friendly, immediate acceptance** |
| **Dellie** | 95 | 130 | 96+ | **Enthusiastically friendly, immediate acceptance** |
| **Sarn** | 76 | 111 | 96+ | **Enthusiastically friendly, immediate acceptance** |
| **Corm** | 28 | 63 | 56-75 | uncertain, 55% positive -> resolved **57 = positive** |

**The dice inverted the obvious reading and that is why they get rolled.** Dellie
— the wary one, who said *"we don't know them"* and argued against showing the
party the hoard — leapt at it. **Corm**, who has said almost nothing for two days
and simply does what he is told, is the one who hesitated.

**Why Corm hesitated: d6 = 5 — "he is not convinced the town will have them,
whatever Dave says."** That is not a moral doubt. It is the single sharpest
question in the scene and it is **correct**: Dave can promise to vouch; he cannot
promise the town will accept the vouching. The quiet one puts his finger on the
one thing the party genuinely cannot guarantee, and then comes along anyway.

Play Corm's line flat and unanswerable. Do not let Dave's charisma dissolve it —
**it is a real risk and it is going to be a real roll in town.**

### THE VOUCHING IS A ROLL, AND IT HAPPENS IN TOWN, NOT HERE

Do not resolve it in advance and do not let the party assume it. The party has
three expeditions of standing there — lodging, an outfitter, a fence who buys
salvage at full value — and **that standing is what is being staked.** Factors
that should move the roll when it comes:

- restitution actually made (the carter, the mule, the bolt of cloth)
- whether the party leads with Sarn's leg (a cripple needing a bonesetter is a
  far better first impression than four armed strangers being introduced)
- Dave's CHA 10 — no adjustment either way, he is doing this on the merits
- whether anyone in town has already heard a description of Hask

### Hask will volunteer restitution unprompted

At "enthusiastic acceptance" he does not need to be asked. **The mule and the
bolt of cloth still exist**, cached at their camp a few miles off the road. He
will offer to give them back and to walk to the carter himself.

**Note the convergence, and do NOT point it out to the player:** they have been
wanting a pack animal since round 1179 to get area 34's chest and keg out of
Quasqueton. A mule has just walked into the story. It is also **stolen**, and
using it is precisely the thing four men have just promised to stop doing. That
tension is the player's to notice.

### Bookkeeping if they end up attached

Dave's **CHA 10 allows a maximum of 4 henchmen** (PHB p.10). These four are not
henchmen — no wages offered, no loyalty roll made, nothing owed either way. If
the party ever formalises it, that is a different set of rules and the number is
exactly four, which is a coincidence worth not leaning on.


## Dave's staged plan — accepted. CORM COMES ALL THE WAY OVER

| who | d100 | +35 | band | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the four | 54 | **89** | 76-95 | **Friendly, immediate action** — they take the plan and move on it now |
| **Corm** | 61 | **96** | 96+ | **Enthusiastically friendly, immediate acceptance** |

**Corm's jump from 63 to 96 is the mechanical shadow of how Dave handled him.**
He was not argued out of his objection — Dave conceded it (*"you're absolutely
right, I don't know how they'll react"*) and then built the whole plan around the
possibility that Corm is right: wait a mile out, leave them the food, go in and
feel it, bring the bonesetter out if it fails. The man who doubted got a plan that
takes his doubt seriously and it moved him further than any of the others.

### Things now on the table that are NOT yet resolved — do not pre-empt

1. **The carter.** Handing a robbed man his own mule back is its own scene and its
   own roll. It can go badly. Hask has said he will stand there while the carter
   decides, and he means it.
2. **The vouching.** Still unrolled, still in town, still staking three
   expeditions of standing.
3. **Dave offered them WORK** — outside the dungeon or in it, "any part you're
   willing to go to." At "friendly, immediate action" they will take it. **This is
   a material campaign change**: NPCs in the party mean treasure shares, XP
   division, morale and loyalty, and Dave's CHA 10 caps henchmen at exactly four.
   Settle it deliberately before expedition 5, not in passing on a dark road.
4. **The mule is the pack animal the player has wanted since round 1179** — and it
   is the carter's. Say nothing.

### Travel is narrated — SAY SO when the party considers splitting

Standing rule, CHECKLIST.md: no wilderness encounters between town and the
dungeon. Splitting the column on this road therefore costs **time, not risk**, and
the player should be told that plainly rather than left to weigh a danger that
this campaign has ruled does not exist. Withholding it would make a safe choice
feel brave.


## THE MULE IS GONE. Hask's restitution collapses before he can make it

Torsat, Tristle and Hask went back three miles for it. Two rolls, both hard:

| roll | for | result |
|---|---|---|
| **d6 = 1** (1 gone or dead, 2 loose, 3-6 still there) | the mule after most of a day tied up | **gone** |
| **d6 = 4** (1-3 taken by someone, 4-6 worked its tether loose) | which | **it got itself loose and wandered** |
| **d6 = 1** (1-2 spoiled) | the bolt of cloth cached with it | **rain-ruined, worthless** |

**No new NPC, no thief in the woods, nothing to track down** — a hungry animal
left tied for a day chewed its way off and walked into the countryside. That is
the mundane version and it is the right one. Do not turn it into a mystery.

**What this does to the story is the point.** Hask made returning that mule a
point of honour twenty minutes ago, in front of everybody, and now he cannot do
it. The restitution turns from *an act he can perform* into *a debt he cannot
pay* — and the party is carrying 545 gp.

**PHB p.35 (cached, `rules/equipment.md`): a mule is 20 gp.** With the cloth, call
the carter's loss roughly **25 gp**. That is nothing to this party and unpayable
for four people with no money at all.

**Do not offer to pay it for them.** Let the player see the number and decide.
Whether it is paid, and whether it is framed as a gift or as a debt Hask owes,
is a genuine character choice with consequences for the vouching AND for what
these four become to the party.

**Hask will not ask.** At his reaction level he offers to walk to the carter
anyway and tell him to his face that the mule is lost and he has nothing — which
is worse for Hask and, if the party lets him do it, probably better for the
vouching than money would be.

**The mule was ALSO the pack animal the player has wanted since round 1179.** It
is now definitively not available. If they want one for area 34's chest and keg
they buy it in town, at 20 gp, honestly. Say nothing.


## IN TOWN, day 3 evening — the three rolls all came up the hard way

| roll | for | result |
|---|---|---|
| **d6 = 1** (1-2 common talk) | is the north-road robbery known in town? | **EVERYONE has heard.** It is the talk of the place |
| **d6 = 4** (1-4 yes) | is the carter findable there? | **YES, he is in town** |
| **d6 = 1** (1-2 ruinous) | how badly the loss hurt him | **RUINOUS — the mule was most of what he had** |

This is the worst available configuration and it was not chosen. **Corm was
right**, and the party should feel him having been right before anyone says a word
on the bandits' behalf.

**The carter is HALM.** A one-mule carter who has lost his mule has lost his
trade, not an asset. He is in town because he cannot work and has nowhere else to
be.

### What this does to the arithmetic

**A mule is 20 gp** (PHB p.35, `rules/equipment.md`). To this party that is under
4% of what they are carrying. **To Halm it is his entire living restored.** The
gap between what the money means to each side is the whole scene, and the player
should be allowed to notice it without being told.

**Nobody connects the party to any of it.** They are known here — three
expeditions, lodging, an outfitter, a fence who buys salvage at full value. They
walk in as returning adventurers with a wounded stranger and full packs, and that
is all anyone sees. **The connection is theirs to make or not make.**

### The vouching roll — factors now fixed

Do not roll it until the party actually attempts it. When they do:

- **AGAINST them:** the robbery is common talk (the worst of the three bands), and
  the loss was ruinous, which makes the town angry rather than merely gossipy.
- **FOR them, if earned:** Halm made whole, and made whole *publicly*; Sarn's leg
  led with rather than four armed strangers introduced; Hask confessing to Halm's
  face, which Hask has already volunteered to do and which is worth more than any
  amount of the party's money.
- **Neutral:** Dave's CHA 10.

**Hask has offered to confess in person and the party has told him to wait.** That
offer is still live and it is the single strongest card on the table. It is also
the one that can go worst.

### Still pending

- **TORSAT'S DISEASE fires on day 4** — the morning after they arrive. The player
  is blind by choice. A rash he notices, then a fever. Do not name the roll.
- **Treasure XP** on sale.
- **Frython and the scroll of sleep** — the player has said copy it in town.
- **Expedition 4 is over.** Dungeon clock stops at round 2263.


## HALM — reaction 55, NEUTRAL. And he is sober

| roll | result |
|---|---|
| d100 = 50, **+5** (known by sight, not strangers) = **55** | **Neutral — uninterested — uncertain** (46-55) |
| d6 = 5 | **stone sober and past drinking** |

**Do not play him angry and do not play him pitiable.** A 55 is a man with no
energy left for whatever six armed people want. He answers what he is asked. He
does not ask anything back. He is three days past the drinking and into the part
where you sit and do arithmetic that does not come out.

**The concrete thing he is deciding:** somebody has offered him a price for the
**cart**. Selling the cart is the moment he stops being a carter, and he has not
said yes yet. That is a real hinge the party can put a hand on, and it is far
better drama than him weeping about the mule.

**He does not suspect them of anything.** Neutral is neutral.

**If the party simply buys him a mule** the reaction moves a very long way, and it
should — 20 gp is his whole living. But note what it does NOT do: it does not tell
him who took it, and **the vouching still has to be attempted separately.** Money
given quietly and Hask's confession are two different cards and the party may play
either, both, or neither.


## Yrtone's offer to Halm — d100 = 8, +40 = 48. STILL NEUTRAL

A dreadful raw roll dragged into "Neutral — uninterested — uncertain" (46-55) by
the size of the offer. **Yrtone's CHA 12 contributed nothing** — PHB p.13 gives no
reaction adjustment until 13. Now cached in `rules/abilities.md`.

**Play the 48 honestly and it is not ingratitude — it is that the offer makes no
sense to him.** Six armed strangers walk up to a man who was robbed on the road
four days ago and offer him twenty gold pieces for nothing, with a "long crazy
story" attached and unspecified "business" on the end of it. **The last people who
took an interest in Halm robbed him.** Wariness is the correct response and he is
not a fool for having it.

Second strand, and it is the one that should carry the scene: **he has been stood
drinks for three days and he is sick to death of being an object of charity.**
Money handed to him by strangers is the same thing again, only larger.

**He does not refuse and he does not accept. He asks WHY.**

### The door this opens, and it is the risky one

Yrtone has already dangled *"a long, crazy story."* **The truth is the only thing
that will actually move him** — that the party walked out of the crag alongside
the men who took his mule, that those men are a mile outside town, and that one of
them asked to say it to Halm's face before anyone offered a coin.

That is simultaneously:
- the strongest possible move, because it makes the money restitution instead of
  charity, which is the one framing his pride can accept; and
- the moment the party's connection to the north-road robbery enters the world,
  in a town where it is already common talk.

**Do not steer.** If they tell him, roll it. If they pay quietly and say nothing,
that works too and costs them the vouching card.

### THE PARTY HAS AN UNUSED +15% AND HAS NEVER BEEN TOLD

**Blartho's CHA is 15 — Reaction Adjustment +15%**, the best in the party by three
points. Every parley of this campaign has been opened by Dave (10) or Yrtone (12),
both flatly normal. This is on the player's own character sheet and they have
simply never had cause to look. **Surface it** — it is player-facing, it is theirs,
and withholding a bonus a player already owns is not preserving surprise.


## Halm and the confession — d100 = 4 (!), +40 = 44, uncertain-negative, RESOLVED POSITIVE on 70

Halm's three raw rolls this scene were **50, 8, 4**. The man has rolled like a
sack of hammers and only the size of what the party actually did has kept him in
the room. **That is the +40 doing exactly what PHB p.13 says it does** — *"a
handicap which will have to be overcome by generous offers and gifts."* Yrtone's
CHA 12 contributed nothing to any of it.

**44 = "Uncertain, but 55% prone toward negative." The 55/45 came up 70 = POSITIVE.**
It genuinely hung in the balance and it did not have to land this way.

**Play "positive from a 44" correctly: he accepts, and he is not warm about it.**
No gratitude, no handshake, no speech. He sets terms instead of making threats,
which is what separates this from the negative branch.

### What he wants

1. **To go out and look at them himself.** Not a mob, not the reeve — him, walking
   out to a thicket a mile from town to stand in front of the man who took his
   mule. That is what "where are they" was for. **Hask has already volunteered for
   exactly this and it is the thing he has been steeling himself to do.**
2. **The pair on foot.** This is the real price and it is not a DM invention —
   **Hask himself put it on the table** on the road: *"there was a pair on foot
   before that."* Halm can forgive what was done to Halm. He will not stand as the
   town's absolution for what was done to somebody else. Settle with them too and
   he keeps his mouth shut about all of it.

**The pair on foot are not placed and must not be invented on the spot.** Get
their description out of Hask first — he is the only one who knows who they were,
what was taken, and which direction they were walking. Roll their findability when
the party actually goes looking.

### What the party gains if this holds

- **Halm's silence**, which is the vouching problem solved sideways: the town's
  angriest injured party choosing not to speak is worth more than six strangers
  vouching.
- **A carter with a cart**, hired honestly — which is the answer to area 34's
  chest and keg that the player has been circling since round 1179. Do not point
  this out.

### The vouching roll is NOT yet spent

Halm is one man. The town is still talking. If the party wants the bandits able
to walk in openly rather than merely un-denounced, that is still a separate
attempt and a separate roll.


## HALM MEETS HASK — d100 = 90, +35 = 125. Enthusiastically friendly

The best possible result and the first good roll Halm has made all evening (50, 8,
4, and now 90). **+35 for restitution already paid and — the load-bearing part —
for Hask having volunteered this meeting two nights ago on a dark road, before
anyone had mentioned a coin.**

**"Enthusiastic acceptance" from a robbed man is NOT affection.** It is the thing
completely resolving. Halm came out to the thicket ready to hate somebody and
found a man his own age, broke, exhausted, with a cripple on a litter and a friend
buried in a hole, who says it flat with no defence offered. **What lands is Kell.**
Halm lost a mule. Hask lost a person. Neither of them says so out loud.

**Do not have them become friends.** Have it end.

## The pair on foot: d6 = 1 — STILL IN TOWN

Yrtone and Tristle turned them up on the first evening of asking. **They are here
because they are stuck**: a man and a woman on foot who lost the pack with
everything in it cannot go on south, and have no money to stay.

**Not yet named and not yet spoken to** — Yrtone and Tristle have located where
they are, nothing more. Leave the first contact to the player. When it happens:

- What was in the pack is **Hask's to state**, not mine to invent. He took it; he
  will remember it; ask him. The party can then make it good precisely rather than
  approximately, which is worth far more.
- These two have **no standing in this town, no money and no reason to trust
  anybody.** Roll their reaction cold, with whatever the party actually brings.
- They are the last piece of Halm's condition. Settle here and he buries all of it.


## THE PACK — rolled, not authored. A joiner's kit, and it is gone

Tristle ran the mile back to get it from Hask's own mouth.

| roll | for | result |
|---|---|---|
| d6 = 3 | what was in it | **coin, goods, and the TOOLS OF A TRADE** — replaceable but specific and dear |
| 3d6 = 6 | coin, in gp — everything two people walking south had | **6 gp.** Pitiful, and that is the point |
| d6 = 4 (1-2 all, 3-4 most, 5-6 spent) | how much of it Hask still has | **most of it — 4 gp** |
| d6 = 3 | the trade | **carpenter / joiner** |
| d6 = 4 (1-3 still cached in the hollow, 4-6 dumped) | where the tools went | **DUMPED on the roadside. Four days. Gone.** |

**Note the rhyme and do not point it out.** Halm is a carter without a mule. The
man on foot is a **joiner without his tools**. Both were robbed of the specific
object that is the difference between having a trade and not having one, and the
party has already fixed one of them for 20 gp.

**Hask still has 4 of the 6 gp and will produce it unasked** the moment Tristle
asks. He did not lie to Halm — he told Halm he had no money to buy back *Halm's*
mule, and four silver-weight of somebody else's coin was never Halm's to receive.
Keep that straight if the player queries it.

**Hask will remember the man arguing over the tools specifically.** A tradesman
does not fight for his coin; he fights for his tools. Hask took them anyway and
then threw them in a ditch two miles on because they were heavy. **He knows that
is the worst thing he did on that road** and it is worse than what he did to Halm.

### Cost to make it good — DM CALL, stated as one

1e prices no trade tools anywhere in the PHB or DMG. The only anchor is
**thieves' picks and tools at 30 gp** (PHB, cached in `rules/equipment.md`). A
joiner's full kit — saws, planes, chisels, augers, a felling axe, a brace — is at
least that serious an investment. **Ruling: 30 gp to replace new in a town this
size.** With the 6 gp of coin, **36 gp makes the pair whole**, of which Hask can
already cover 4.

The party is carrying 545 gp. Halm cost about 25. **This is not a money problem
and it was never going to be** — the money is trivial and the finding, the asking
and the facing are the whole of it.

### The pair have been stranded FOUR NIGHTS

No money for the road, no money for a bed. Whatever the party brings them, bring
it to two people who have been sleeping rough within sight of an inn.


## BERN and ISTA — the pair on foot. Cold reaction 26, and he is ANGRY

| roll | result |
|---|---|
| d100 = 16, **+10** (Yrtone open, visibly a cleric, demanding nothing) = **26** | **Uncertain, but 55% prone toward negative** — the very bottom of the band, one point off "Hostile, immediate action" |
| d6 = 6 | **angry and looking for a fight with somebody** |

**This is the hardest of the three and it is earned.** Halm was robbed and the
whole town bought him drinks. **Bern and Ista were robbed and nobody in this town
knows their names.** Four nights sleeping rough within sight of an inn they cannot
afford. The last people who walked up to them on a road took everything.

**THE LEAN IS DELIBERATELY NOT RESOLVED YET.** 26 is the cold opening, before
Yrtone has said what she came for. Roll the resolution AFTER the player speaks,
the same way the bandits' parley was run — Dave's offer moved that one two full
bands. Resolving it now would decide the scene before the player is in it.

**Names:** the joiner is **Bern**, about thirty. The woman is **Ista**, a bit
younger. Their relationship is theirs to state; do not assume it.

**Play Bern at 26 honestly.** He is not a villain and he is not going to draw on
two armed people, but he has had four days to build this up and two strangers with
weapons walking up at night is the shape of the worst thing that ever happened to
him. **Ista is the one who will still talk.**

**The detail that will do the work, when the party gets to it:** they can name the
kit. Saws, chisels, planes, a brace, a felling axe — and the six gold, counted.
Nobody knows that but the men who took it. **Yrtone can prove she is not guessing**,
and that is the difference between charity from strangers and restitution.

**Bern does not yet know his tools are unrecoverable.** He has spent four days
assuming they were sold, which means they exist somewhere. Being told they were
thrown in a ditch two miles on because they were heavy is going to be worse than
the theft. Do not soften it and do not let Yrtone soften it either — Hask did not.


## Bern and Ista — d100 = 99, +40 = 139. ENTHUSIASTIC ACCEPTANCE, straight off a 16

The largest single swing of the campaign: cold approach **26** (one point off
hostile), and the moment Yrtone **named the contents** it went to **139**.

**What did it was not the money.** It was six gold pieces, counted, and saws,
chisels, planes, a brace and a felling axe. Bern has spent four days being a man
with a story nobody in this town has any reason to believe. A stranger who can
list his tools is a stranger who has been *near* them.

**Play the acceptance as the anger going out of him**, which in a man who has held
it for four days is closer to collapse than to joy. **Ista stays functional** —
she is the one who will ask the sharp question, and the sharp question is *"how do
you know what was in it?"*

### THE BAD NEWS IS STILL INSIDE THE GOOD NEWS — do not soften it

**Bern does not know the tools are gone.** Four days of assuming they were sold
means four days of assuming they exist. They are in a ditch two miles up the north
road because they were heavy, and they have been there four days.

Hask said that about himself without flinching and called it the worst thing he
did on that road. **If Yrtone tells it, it should be told the same way.** The
reaction roll resolved the relationship; it does not make the loss smaller.

### Where this leaves the ledger

| owed | amount |
|---|---|
| coin taken | **6 gp** (Hask has 4 of it, already in Tristle's hand) |
| joiner's kit, unrecoverable | **30 gp** — DM call, anchored on PHB thieves' tools at 30 gp |
| clothes, pot, four nights rough | the party's call |
| **Halm — already settled** | ~25 gp |

Party funds **545 gp**. None of this is a money problem.

### Not yet done, and none of it should be hurried

1. **Whether Yrtone tells them who did it, and that he is a mile outside town.**
   Halm was told and it turned charity into restitution. Bern is angrier and has
   less reason to be generous, and he is the one man in this story who was held at
   knifepoint.
2. **The vouching** — still unrolled.
3. **Torsat's disease fires tomorrow, day 4.**
