4. Fence the salvage; treasure XP; Frython's scroll.


## Bern and Ista accept everything — d100 = 70, +40 = 110. And they have NOWHERE TO GO

| roll | result |
|---|---|
| d100 = 70, **+40** = **110** | **Enthusiastically friendly, immediate acceptance** (96+) |
| **d6 = 6** | **they were not going anywhere. They were going AWAY.** No destination, nobody expecting them |

**The d6=6 is the important one and it reframes the whole offer.** Yrtone's last
line — *"maybe we can help you get there, or if you need help setting up shop
here"* — was a courtesy. It turns out to be the only thing on the table that
matters. **You cannot help someone get somewhere when there is no somewhere.**

**WHY they left is theirs and is NOT YET DETERMINED.** Do not invent it to fill a
silence. If the player asks, roll it; if they do not ask, it stays a closed door
with a hand resting on it, which is better. Ista will deflect once. She will not
deflect twice from someone who has just done this for them.

### The tools still hurt and the acceptance does not fix it

Bern will want to go and look in that ditch himself, at first light, whatever
anybody buys him. **Let him.** A man does not take a stranger's word that his own
tools are gone.

**If the party searches the ditch, roll it** — I ruled the kit *gone* (d6=4,
dumped on the road, four days on a public road). That ruling stands, but a
physical search is a real action and deserves a real roll: something bent, an axe
head, a single chisel. Not the kit.

### What the party has actually bought here

Not gratitude — **a joiner and his partner with no destination, in a town where
the party has standing, who owe them everything and are the only two people in the
district with a reason to speak well of Hask's crew.** If Bern sets up here, that
is a second independent voice for the vouching and a permanent asset. **Do not
point this out.**

### RUNNING TOTAL of what the party has spent making this right

| | |
|---|---|
| Halm — mule and cloth | ~25 gp |
| Bern — 2 gp of the 6 (Hask returned 4) | 2 gp |
| Bern — replacement joiner's kit | 30 gp (DM call) |
| four nights rough, clothes, pot, setting up | player's call |

Out of **545 gp**. Under 11% so far.


## The job offer to Bern — he BALKS. And ISTA wants to come

| roll | result |
|---|---|
| d100 = 8, **+30** = **38** | **Uncertain, prone toward negative** — he balks at *this job*, not at the party |
| **d6 = 5** | **nowhere near those caves.** He will teach them and lend them the tools |
| **d6 = 5** | **ISTA WANTS TO COME** |

**Keep the two things separate.** The 38 is his reaction to *being asked to go into
Quasqueton*, not a cooling toward the party — the restitution landed at 110 and
stands. A man who has just been handed his life back, by people who described the
place as nearly killing all six of them, declining to go down there is the sane
answer and should be played without a shred of shame in it.

**What he gives freely, and it is worth more than his presence:** the trade
knowledge. A joiner takes things apart for a living — that is half the work.
**Yrtone did not mention the locked chest**, so he answers generally, and the
answer happens to be the solution to a problem the party has been stuck on since
round 1178:

> Tristle failed area 29's chest lock by **ONE** (36 vs 35) and cannot try again
> until 2nd level. **A chest does not have to be unlocked to be opened.** Pins,
> pegs, hinges, the boards the lock plate is screwed to.

**DO NOT CONNECT IT FOR THEM.** Bern is describing his trade. If the player makes
the leap, it works, and it should work well — it is a genuinely good idea and it
came from them.

**Say nothing about what is in that chest** (garments, a broken dagger, and the
walnut-and-silver plaque, 25 gp) and nothing about area 34's two chests being
empty.

### Ista

**Not a fighter, no class, no armour, no training.** She is steady, she has been
sleeping in a stable yard for four days, she has nowhere to go, and she has just
watched six people walk out of that place alive and rich enough to buy strangers a
life. That is the whole of her reasoning and it is enough.

**Do not make her secretly capable.** If the party takes her in, she is a
civilian in a dungeon and everything that means. If they say no, she takes it.

**Her reason for leaving wherever they came from is still undetermined.** Still do
not invent it.


## Day 4, dawn — THE DITCH HAS NOTHING (d6 = 5), and TORSAT'S DISEASE FIRES

**The ditch: d6 = 5, "nothing whatever."** Four days on a public road. Not the
tools, not the bag, not a chisel. Somebody took it, or several somebodies took
parts of it. **Do not give him a consolation object** — the roll said nothing and
the replacement kit is the answer, which is what makes the 30 gp mean something.

Bern knew before he went. He needed to see it, which is why letting him go was
right.

### TORSAT'S DISEASE — pending event now FIRED, day 4

Rolled 2026-08-28 off DMG p.14 and untouched since. Contracted round 398 from a
giant rat bite (d100=2 against 5%, then failed the save vs poison, 6 needing 14):

- **SKIN**, **ACUTE**, **MILD**, duration **1 week**, incubation **3 days**
- **Effect: no strenuous activity, one full week of rest.**
- **No Charisma loss** — the DMG's 10% CHA risk is for CHRONIC mild, not acute.
- *Cure disease* is a **3rd-level cleric spell**. Neither Yrtone nor Blartho is
  remotely close. **It has to be waited out.**

**THE PLAYER HAS BEEN BLIND ON THIS BY THEIR OWN REQUEST SINCE ROUND 398.**
Describe symptoms only: a man who has been scratching at his forearms without
thinking about it, and now, in daylight, a raised red rash on the arms and throat,
and he is hot. **Do not name the roll, the table, the duration or the word
"disease"** until the fiction produces it — a cleric examining him, or a healer in
town.

**Timing is kind and that is luck, not mercy.** The party habitually rests a week
in town between expeditions (they did before expedition 4). If they keep to that,
this costs them nothing but a bad week. **If they try to go back early, Torsat
cannot go.** Do not soften that if they push.

**Corvie the bonesetter** was fetched out to Sarn overnight by Halm — a man who
four hours earlier had been told these were the people who ruined him.


## DAY 4 IN TOWN — FRYTHON LEARNS *SLEEP* ON EXACTLY 75

**d100 = 75 against a 75% chance to know (INT 17, PHB p.10). Made on the number.**
One higher and he could never have learned it, ever, and the scroll would have
been gone regardless. This is the largest single upgrade the party has had.

**Note for the record: `read magic` was not optional.** PHB requires it to read
a scroll at all, so Frython trading out *shocking grasp* was the prerequisite for
the transcription, not just curiosity about the runed book. The player's plan was
correct for a reason they may not have been aiming at.

## Blartho's *detect magic* sweep — what it does and does not show

He dropped *command* for it. Duration 1 turn, a 1" wide by 3" long path, so he can
walk the whole pile in one casting.

**RADIATES:** the **ring** from area 27, and the **scroll** (before it was
consumed). Nothing else the party is carrying.

**DOES NOT RADIATE — and this is the useful negative result:**

- **Zelligar's diary, the runed book, is NOT MAGICAL.** It is a cipher in an
  undecipherable hand and needs ***read languages***, not *read magic*. B1 p.9.
  Frython's *read magic* is simply the wrong tool and finds nothing at all in it.
  **The detect magic sweep saves them from concluding the book is warded or
  cursed** — it is just handwriting.
- The plant encyclopedia, the history, the weather book: mundane. **The
  encyclopedia is in ELVISH and Tristle can simply read it. Still do not point
  that out.**
- The stone Z paperweight, the pewter garment, the chain, the bearskin, the
  armoury salvage: all mundane.

**The ring is confirmed magical but NOT identified.** *Detect magic* establishes
that a thing radiates, never what it does. Ways forward: an *identify* spell (a
100 gp pearl, and 8 points of Constitution off the caster, 6 turns' rest per
point — brutal at these levels), a sage or magic-user in town, or simply wearing
it and inferring. **Until it is established, its bonus still does not apply and it
still earns no XP.** Do not volunteer the routes; answer if asked.

## The day's spending — 85 gp, and none of it was a problem

| | |
|---|---|
| Halm: mule 20 + bolt of cloth 5 | **25 gp** |
| Bern: replacement joiner's kit (DM call, anchored on PHB thieves' tools 30 gp) | **30 gp** |
| Bern: balance of the stolen coin (Hask returned 4 of the 6 himself) | **2 gp** |
| Bern: 3 months down on a shop-and-room at 4 gp/month, plus bench and timber | **22 gp** |
| One night's lodging, six at 1 gp (the established rate) | **6 gp** |
| **Funds 545 gp 3 sp 25 cp -> 460 gp 3 sp 25 cp** | |

**Nothing has been SOLD yet** — the armoury salvage, the four books, the bearskin
and the pewter are all still in hand, and **treasure XP settles on that sale.**

**Lodging is charged one night at a time deliberately.** Torsat needs a week; the
party has not said how long they are staying and should not be committed to 42 gp
by assumption.

## HALM'S MULE — do not let this pass unremarked

The party bought a mule for a man they had never met, four hours after learning
their own associates ruined him. **He now has a working cart again**, and he is
the obvious answer to hauling area 29's chest and barrel off the crag. **Do not
propose it.** They have been circling a pack animal since round 1179 and the
solution is now standing in an inn yard eating.


## THE TOWN'S MACHINERY — reeve OTTIR (pragmatic) and WEND the cloth trader (vocal)

Dave asked Halm for the lie of the land before bringing Hask in. Two rolls:

| roll | result |
|---|---|
| **d6 = 4** | the reeve is **pragmatic — cares about order, not justice** |
| **d6 = 2** | **there IS somebody else with a stake and they are VOCAL** |

### Reeve OTTIR

Answers to a distant lord, has no standing men, and wants no trouble and no
expense. **He does not act unless a complaint is laid.** He is not moral and he is
not corrupt — he is a man who would like this to go away.

**The people who could lay a complaint are Halm, Bern and Ista, and all three have
said they will not.** That is the whole legal position and it is very strong.

**But a pragmatic reeve who finds out from GOSSIP has to be seen to act**, because
his standing depends on not looking like a man who was kept in the dark. One who
is told first, quietly, by people with money and standing, can be managed. **This
is Halm's advice and it is good advice.** Timing is the entire variable.

### WEND, cloth and wool trader — the one who cannot be paid off

She is both:

1. **A victim** — the bolt on Halm's cart was **hers**, a consignment he was
   hauling. Halm will say so unprompted, because the party just handed him 5 gp
   for a bolt that was never his. **He will try to pass that 5 gp straight to
   her.** Let him; it is exactly what he would do.
2. **The loud one.** She has spent a week saying the north road wants patrolling
   and somebody wants hanging, and people listen because her goods move on it.

**Her real grievance is the ROAD, not the bolt, and money does not fix a road.**
Five gold squares her loss and does nothing whatever to her position. **Do not let
the party buy her.** She can be persuaded, out-flanked, or given something that
actually addresses her problem — four men who know exactly how the road is worked
and are done working it is, in fact, an answer, if anyone is clever enough to
frame it that way.

**She is not a villain.** She is right: the road IS unsafe, and the party is
proposing to quietly absorb the people who made it so.

### The vouching roll — when it comes

Still unrolled. Factors now fixed:

- **FOR:** Halm won't complain and will say so out loud; Bern and Ista won't and
  are being set up in trade by the party; the party has three expeditions of
  standing; the reeve wants no trouble.
- **AGAINST:** the robbery is common talk (worst band rolled); the loss was
  ruinous; **Wend is loud and has an audience**; and nobody in the party has a
  Charisma reaction bonus.
- **Order of operations matters more than any modifier.** Reeve first and quietly
  is worth real points. The town hearing it as rumour first is worth losing them.


## THE VOUCHING SUCCEEDS — Ottir, d100 = 82, +25 = 107. Enthusiastic acceptance

**Dave CHA 10 and Yrtone CHA 12 both contributed nothing.** The +25 was earned
entirely by what the party did before walking in the door:

- **+35** — all three injured parties squared, willing to say so, and none will
  lay a complaint; three expeditions of standing; a reeve who wants no trouble;
  and **they came to him FIRST and quietly**, which was Halm's advice
- **−10** — the robbery is common talk (worst band rolled) and Wend has an audience

**"Enthusiastic acceptance" from a pragmatic reeve is RELIEF, not warmth.** He has
a loud woman in his ear, no men, and no way to solve any of it. Two respectable,
moneyed people walk in and hand him a finished solution that costs him nothing.

### What Ottir does, and it is in character rather than generous

1. **No complaint, no case.** He will confirm that with Halm himself and Halm has
   already offered to say it to his face.
2. **He wants a FORM.** A pragmatic man needs a story he can tell. **Dave stands
   surety for the four** — answerable for them, by name, in front of the reeve.
   That is a real medieval mechanism and it is the right shape for 1e. **It is
   also a genuine liability the player is taking on**: if any of the four offends
   again, it lands on Dave.
3. **He wants them visible and working**, not idle in a thicket. Idle men are what
   he is afraid of.
4. **He hands the party the Wend problem**, because he does not want it either.

### THE ROAD IS ALREADY FIXED and nobody knows it

d6 = 2 — **Hask's crew were essentially the whole of the north-road trouble.**
There is no second band and nothing out of the caves. Halm worked it out himself
from the dates. **Give it a fortnight and the road goes quiet on its own and Wend
runs out of things to point at** — the party does not actually have to solve her,
only outlast her. Whether they realise that is theirs.

## The sale — 170 gp, and the DIARY WAS HELD BACK

The party sold the whole non-magical haul at full value (the expedition-3
precedent). **Zelligar's diary was deliberately not sold** once the dealer
mentioned it would be talked about — so the **40% rumour risk has NOT been
triggered** and the 50 gp is still on the table whenever they want it.

**Two DM calls made openly:** weapon salvage at half of new, and the perfume at
5 gp a bottle (B1 gives no price for it).

**The plant encyclopedia was sold for 15 gp because it is in ELVISH and scarce.**
The dealer said so in as many words, in front of Tristle, who is an elf and could
simply have read it. **That hint was supplied by the fiction and not taken.** Do
not raise it again — it is gone, and it was never anything but flavour.

**Treasure XP awarded at 1 gp = 1 xp** (DMG p.85; the guardians were of comparable
or greater strength than a 1st-level party, so no discount band applies).
**Totals now: Dave 567, Tristle 535, Yrtone 483, Frython 483, Torsat 439,
Blartho 439.** Tristle is **716** from 2nd level and is the closest.


## THE RING IS ACTIVE FROM NOW ON — my earlier ruling was WRONG

I wrote that the ring's *"bonus still does not apply in play"* until identified.
**That is not a 1e rule and it is not defensible.** A ring of protection has no
attunement and no activation; it works on the finger of whoever puts it on,
knowing or not. Blartho has put it on.

**BLARTHO IS AC 2, NOT 3, AND SAVES AT +1, FROM DAY 5 ONWARD.** Apply it every
time. `campaign/characters.tsv` still reads 3 because 3 is his *armour*; the ring
is tracked here and must be added at the table.

**He perceives nothing.** No warmth, no glow, no weight. It comes off freely,
which is itself a real result: **a cursed ring cannot be removed**, so taking it
off proves it is not cursed. That is genuine information and the party earned it
by the crude method.

**Do not announce the number.** They will see it the first time something swings
at him and the to-hit line reads AC 2. That is discovery through play and it is
what the player asked for.

**Still no XP for it** — the DMG pays either the item's value if kept or the sale
price if sold, and neither can be set until they know what it is.

## Frython cracks the DATES — d20 = 10 under INT 17

The player supplied a real crib: the area 6 papers are **dated logistics records
in the common tongue**, and the journal's blocks each open with a date. Matching
known dates against runed date-blocks is **numeral cryptanalysis, not
translation**, and it is a legitimately different and much smaller problem than
the one B1 seals off. Rewarded.

**What he gets: the numerals only. The content stays shut** — that still wants
*comprehend languages* or a thief's Read Languages at 4th, exactly as before.

**What the dates say, and this is all he gets:**

- The journal runs in regular entries for years.
- **It stops.** The last entry is dated a few days AFTER the most recent of the
  logistics papers, which makes it **the last dated thing written in Quasqueton**.
- The final entry is **one line long**, where every entry before it runs half a
  page. He can see that from the block length without reading a word of it.

That dates the abandonment to within days and it is genuinely chilling with no
content attached. **Do not invent what it says.**

## Tristle and Bern — 39, +30 = 69, resolved 92 POSITIVE

The hesitation is the good part and it should be played: **Bern works out what he
is actually being asked to teach.** A man asks a joiner to show him how to get
into other people's boxes without a key, and the man asking is four days removed
from having taken the joiner's own property back off the people who stole it.

He hesitates. Then he says yes, because of who is asking and what they did.

**By the end of the week Tristle can take a chest down** — pins, hinges, the
boards a lock plate is screwed to. That is a real capability and it **routes
around the once-per-lock-ever rule entirely**: area 29's chest beat him by one
point on the dice and can no longer beat him at all.

## WEND — 18, +25 = 43, resolved 44 NEGATIVE. d6=3: SHE WAS TOLD SECOND

**The one hard result of the day and it is entirely earned.**

Her grievance is not the cloth (already settled — Halm passed her the party's 5 gp)
and it is not the road. **It is that she spent a week hammering on Ottir's door
demanding action, and the reeve was quietly told the whole truth by strangers and
settled it behind her back, and she found out last.** She has been made to look
like the town fool shouting about a problem everyone else had already fixed.

**That is a grievance about STANDING, and money will not touch it and neither
will a patrol.** Do not let the party buy her. Do not let her be unreasonable
either — she is not wrong.

**She does not go public, yet.** 43 is uncertain-negative, not hostile: she is
cold, suspicious, and now a live problem. **Leave the thread open.**

The route back, if the party finds it: she wants to be **part of** the answer
rather than managed around it. Something that restores her standing publicly. They
cannot undo having gone to Ottir first.

## Hask on the patrol — 73, +30 = 103, enthusiastic

Four men who worked that road patrolling that road is either poetic or provocative
depending on who hears it, and **Wend is exactly who will hear it.** Note the
collision and do not resolve it in advance.


## THE RING IS ON DAVE NOW — apply it every time

Blartho took it off, Dave put it on. **DAVE IS AC 2, NOT 3, AND SAVES AT +1**,
from day 5 onward. `campaign/characters.tsv` still says 3 because 3 is his
armour. **The ring lives in this note and must be added at the table.**

Dave is front rank, so it will surface fast — which is exactly why the player put
it there.

## Wend, second approach — 44, +25 = 69, resolved 7: NEGATIVE AGAIN

**A whole band better than the first attempt (43 -> 69) and it still came out
negative.** She is the campaign's genuinely hard NPC and she has now refused twice.

**Play the split, because the dice describe it precisely.** Two things were said
and they did not land the same way:

1. *"You weren't making a fuss, you were right."* — this is the exact point of the
   grievance, conceded outright instead of argued, and **it visibly lands.** She
   softens. That is the +35.
2. *"...the Gods gave us a weird turn of fate… that's a blessing."* — **this
   undoes it.** A practical trader who has been made to look a fool in the street
   for a week has just been told that her humiliation was part of a providential
   good outcome. From where Wend stands that means *your grievance is a small
   price worth paying*. She hardens again on the spot.

**This is the useful signal for the player and it should be legible in the scene
without being explained:** the apology worked, the theology did not.

**Her injury is PUBLIC and every attempt so far has been PRIVATE.** Nothing said
to Wend alone in a shop can fix a thing that happened to her in the street. Do not
hint; let them get there.

## Hask and Dellie recast as scouts — 12, +30 = 42, resolved 61: positive, but he BALKS first

**The reframe scored WORSE than the original idea** (103 -> 42), and the reason is
Hask's whole arc: **he does not want to be kept out of sight.** He stood in front
of Halm rather than be managed. Told that the visible patrol will be other people
and he will be the guide who is not seen, he hears *hide*.

**And his objection is tactically correct, so give it to him straight:** four
ex-bandits skulking unseen along the road they used to work is, if anybody
discovers it, far worse than four ex-bandits walking it openly. Concealment is
what guilty men do.

He takes it anyway (61, positive). But it is accepted, not welcomed, and the
collision with Wend is now doubled: she is angry about being managed around, and
the party's answer to her is a patrol whose most useful members are being kept out
of view.

## Frython, second attempt — d20 = 11 against 12. SUCCESS, and it is BOUNDED

Frequency analysis, journal against the dated papers. He gets **proper nouns, not
language.**

- Two clusters recur constantly throughout, and both match names in **the area 6
  papers** — the household and construction records, which name people.

**CORRECTION, 2026-08-31.** I originally wrote that Frython matched them to *"the
R of the area 29 plaque and the Z of the paperweight."* **He could not have** —
the plaque was inside a locked chest he had never opened. The **Z** is fine (it is
on the paperweight, in his hands). The **R** came out of the papers. When the
chest is finally opened and the plaque read, it **confirms** what he worked out
from the papers, which is better than contradicting it.
- **The book is therefore provably Zelligar's own journal.** Not a curiosity in
  runes — an identified, dated, attributable personal diary of the man the whole
  district still tells stories about.
- Structural, content-free, and true to B1's own text: **the entries get shorter
  toward the end**, and the last is a single line.

### VALUE CHANGED — and so did the risk

The dealer's **50 gp** was for an unreadable curiosity. **A provably-attributed
Zelligar diary is worth appreciably more to the right buyer — and is
correspondingly far more likely to be talked about.** The 40% rumour chance was
priced for the curiosity. **If they sell it now, treat the rumour as near
certain**, and say so before the sale, not after.

### DO NOT BUILD A PLOT ON THE CONTENTS

**B1 p.9 says the diary is "of no practical value."** That stands. The dates, the
names and the shortening entries are atmosphere and provenance, which is a real
and sufficient payoff for two clever rolls. **Do not invent a recurring mystery
word or a secret in the last entries.** If it is ever read, it is a private man's
private preoccupations, and the party will have earned 50-plus gold and a good
story rather than a revelation.


## THE PATROL — week of day 5. Twelve half-day checks, three hits

Level-0 road table, 1 in 6 per half-day. Hits on **half-days 1, 9 and 11.**

**Half-day 1 — a merchant's cart (table 4).** Reaction **49, neutral** until Dave
named Reeve Ottir, then **98 +25 = 123, enthusiastic.** The name of a checkable
authority was worth two full bands and it is the single most efficient thing
anyone has said all campaign.

**OSK** the carrier, three days up and three days back every fortnight. Rolled
**d6 = 6 — he has seen NOTHING**, the whole length of it, and had been looking
hard for six days. **He will say so in town and say who he said it to.** That is
the patrol paying out in the only currency that reaches Wend: a disinterested
third party, in public, unprompted.

He did not recognise Hask or Dellie (d6=2; only a 1 would have).

**Half-days 2-8: seven checks, nothing.** Do not apologise for it. Seven empty
half-days IS the result the party is buying and it is the evidence they will take
back to town.

**Half-day 9 — DESPERATE MEN (table 6), 1d3 = 2, hp 5 and 3.** AC 9, HD 1, ML 6.

**HASK SPOTTED THEM** (d6=1, party first) from a fold of dead ground he had
pointed out on day one as a place he would have used himself. **That is the entire
justification for bringing him, delivered.**

### DO NOT LET THIS BECOME A FIGHT BY DEFAULT

The table entry says it in as many words: *reaction roll BEFORE assuming a fight.*
These two are **what Hask, Dellie, Corm and Sarn were six days ago** — hungry, not
organised, sitting in a hollow waiting for somebody weaker than them.

**Hask knows exactly what they are and exactly what will make them run or fight**,
because he has been the man in that hollow. What he will say, if asked:

- **Leave them a way out.** A cornered man fights; a man with a road behind him
  talks. Do not spread out and do not get above them.
- **Send few, not many.** Six people walking at them in a line is a sweep.
- **Show food, not steel.** That is what was done for him and he knows what it did.

**If the party surrounds them or approaches with bows drawn, run it as a fight and
do not soften it** — two 1-HD men with nothing to lose against Blartho and Frython
on 2 hit points each is genuinely dangerous.

**Half-day 11 is also a hit and has NOT been rolled yet.** Roll it when reached.


## The two in the hollow — d100 = 18, +35 = 53. NEUTRAL. And they are LOCAL

**d6 = 1: local men put off a farm or out of work.** Not travellers, not
professionals. **They are from this district**, which changes everything about the
scene: they can be known, they can be placed, and they can be shamed.

**A rotten raw roll (18) dragged to neutral by an approach that could not have
been better executed** — Dave followed Hask's advice to the letter and it was
worth +35. Without it this was hostile.

**Play neutral as HUMILIATION, not menace.** Two local men caught sitting in a
cold north-facing hollow off a road, by six armed people, one of whom is a cleric.
They stand up. They do not run and they do not swing. And the first thing out of
their mouths is **a transparent lie** — resting, looking for a strayed beast —
because a local man does not want to be the man who was found doing this.

**They are two hired hands off the same farm**, let go: one about forty, one about
eighteen. No armour, a billhook and a staff between them. **hp 5 and 3.**

**They have not actually robbed anybody yet.** The road has been empty for six
days — that is exactly why they are still hungry and still sitting there. **Do not
let the party assume otherwise and do not confirm it unasked.** If they are asked
straight, the older one will not lie about it twice.

**Hask is standing right there**, and the parallel is not subtle, and it should not
be pointed at. Let the player see it.

**If it goes to violence** it is two 1-HD men with a billhook against a party
containing two characters on 2 hit points. ML 6 — they break fast — but a billhook
is d6+ and Frython dies to one hit.


## HALF-DAY 11 — A SECOND PAIR, AND THE PATTERN IS THE REAL RESULT OF THE WEEK

Table **6 again**. 1d3 = **2 men**. And the "who are they" die came up **2 — local,
off a farm**, exactly like Coll and Wat.

**They saw the party first (d6=6) and went to ground.** Spotted anyway (d6=2)
because the party now walks with **two men who have each spent days lying in
exactly such a hollow.** Give the spot to **COLL**, not Hask — he was doing it
himself yesterday, and it makes him worth his bread inside a day.

### THE FINDING, AND IT IS BIGGER THAN "THE ROAD IS CLEAR"

Twelve half-day checks. **Seven empty. One merchant who had seen nothing in six
days. And two separate pairs of local men, off farms, sitting in hollows.**

**The north road is not infested with bandits. The district has more hungry men
than work**, and Hask's four were a symptom, not the disease. Halm's arithmetic
was right about the *robberies* and wrong about the *cause*.

**You cannot patrol that away.** A week of armed men walking the road moves the
hungry men off the road for exactly as long as the armed men are on it.

**This is the honest answer to Wend** and it is a far harder one than she asked
for. It is also the answer Ottir will least want to hear, because it is not a
policing problem and he has no more money than he has men.

**DO NOT PREACH IT.** Let the party arrive at it by counting: two pairs in two
days, both local, both the same story. If they say it out loud, Coll will confirm
it in one sentence — he knows how many hands came off Rell's land alone.

### Rell — the party means to ask around discreetly

Noted for when they are next in town. **d6 = 1 already established: their story
checks easily.** Other turned-off hands are scattered around the district and the
name is known. Asking will confirm Coll and Wat AND surface how many others there
are, which is the same finding again from a second direction.

## THE RING — WHY NO XP HAS BEEN AWARDED, AND IT IS NOT A CHEAT

DM-side it is a **ring of protection +1**: DMG p.122 gives protection rings
**2,000-4,000 XP if KEPT, 10,000-20,000 gp if SOLD**, and the two are exclusive.

**The player asked directly and was told the truth about the reasoning:** any
award — including zero — tells them which kind of ring it is, because three of
the twenty-four rings are cursed and worth nothing. **So it is held, they know it
is held, and they have been promised it lands in full and backdated the day they
establish what it is.** Do not quietly forget this. It is roughly **366 XP each**
with the prime-requisite bonus, which is more than half of what most of them have
earned in five expeditions.

## RULES SETTLED THIS SESSION — all in rules/, all cited

- **XP is for what you take off an enemy, never for what you already own.**
  Property, not geography. Bandits and their loot, yes; the party's own cargo,
  no. `rules/experience.md`, and `CHECKLIST.md` §3b.
- **Training 1,500 gp/level/week; XP freezes at the threshold** (DMG p.86).
- **DMG p.25 upkeep is NOT USED** — house rule since 2026-08-25, 1 gp per person
  per night. **Check `rules/` before flagging anything as a gap; I raised this as
  missing when it had been settled and cached for a week.**
- **Charging: +2 to hit, own AC one worse, no initiative at the end of a charge**
  (DMG p.66).
- **Breaking off from melee gives the opponent a FREE ATTACK ROUTINE at +6** —
  rear (+2) on a stunned opponent (+4). There is no attack of opportunity in 1e
  (DMG p.70).
- **Missile cover and concealment ADJUSTS THE TARGET'S AC DOWNWARD** — "+2" means
  two points harder to hit. Got this backwards once (DMG p.64).
- **Sleep: range 3"+1"/level = 40 ft at 1st, 3" circle, 5 rounds, NO SAVE, 4d4
  creatures of 1 HD or under** (PHB p.68).
- **Wandering checks anchor every 30 rounds, not 20** — B1 p.4, "every third turn
  of adventuring". Ran at 1.5x the module rate for the whole campaign.
- **Resolve a check at the round it fires or not at all. Never bank one.**
- **Blocked is not absent** — a spiked door still has a corridor on the far side.
- **Mule: 2,000 gp weight at pace, 6,000 at half (MM p.72).** Pack animal loads
  are in the MONSTER MANUAL, not the PHB or DMG.
- **Standard hirelings and the Ludbury tax system** — DMG pp.28 and 90, cached in
  `rules/equipment.md`.

# RULINGS — day 39, 2026-09-05: the three henchmen become characters

**All of these are DM rulings on questions the three core books do not answer.**
The research and every page citation are in `rules/zero-level.md`; what follows
is only what was *decided*, so a later session does not silently decide it
differently.

**The gap being filled.** 1e has no 0-level → 1st-level progression. 1st level
*begins* at 0 XP (PHB pp. 20, 22), so there is no threshold to cross; the gate is
DMG p. 35's "1 in 100 suitable for level advancement", which is a capacity, not a
score. The books describe no transition, and searching all three for one returns
nothing.

| # | ruled | reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **They are among the advancement-capable. No die rolled.** | DMG p. 35 derives its 1-in-1,000 henchman pool *from* the 1-in-100 capable pool — in the book's own model, taking service with an adventurer is the evidence of capacity. Rolling to see whether the person who did the thing is the kind of person who does the thing is backwards. **If we RECRUIT someone new, roll the 1 in 100 then** — that is the case the ratio is written for. |
| **2** | **1st level immediately. No waiting period, no XP threshold.** | There is nothing to wait for. Inventing one would be a 5e-shaped intuition with no 1e text behind it. |
| **3** | **Class: ability scores gate, then background picks among the legal options.** | DMG p. 35's d100 class roll is written for a faceless applicant pool. These three have forty sessions of history. Scores were rolled first and in the open so the gate was honest. |
| **4** | **Scores by DMG p. 11 "Special Characters, Including Henchmen"** — 3d6 full range, germane ability boosted (+1 to each die under 6) **after** the class was fixed. | The alternative, "General Characters", forces every die to 3 or 4 and yields nothing but 9-12 across the board — no prime-requisite bonus possible, ever. |
| **5** | **Hit points: keep the higher of current hp and a fresh class die.** | Silence in the books. Losing hp for advancing would be perverse; the only analogous printed mechanic (DMG p. 119 energy drain) *removes* hp for losing a level. **Moot in the event — all three rolled exactly what they already had.** |
| **6** | **MONSTER xp is now a FULL share. TREASURE xp stays HALF.** | DMG p. 85's halving sentence sits inside "Division of Experience Points", which opens on *slain monsters* and is explicitly "strictly your prerogative". The next heading, EXPERIENCE VALUE OF TREASURE TAKEN, never mentions henchmen — treasure xp is 1 gp = 1 xp on coin gained, so a half share of coin already halves it. **Held prospective by a day-39 anchor row**; without it the pot divisor going 7 → 8 would have retroactively taken xp off the six PCs. |
| **7** | **They remain HENCHMEN, not PCs.** | Corm and Sarn are not, and should not be swept in. |
| **8** | **Training at 1st→2nd costs them the same as anyone: level × 1,500/week.** | DMG p. 86 says "character" throughout and exempts nobody. Say plainly that the free ride ended. |

**Open, and deliberately not decided:** who pays if one of them reaches 2nd level
(Dave, the party, or themselves) — put it in `campaign/articles.md` when it
arises, and **do not let an NPC settle it mid-scene.**

**One printed contradiction, flagged to the player and resolved his way.** The
Constitution Table prints at score 5: *"Here or lower the character can only be
an illusionist."* Ista rolled CON 3. Read literally that bars her from cleric,
thief and magic-user — and she fails the illusionist's own minimums (INT 15,
DEX 16), leaving her 0-level forever. It contradicts the class descriptions on
PHB pp. 20-28, none of which set any constitution minimum for those three.
**Ruled: the "General Information" column is shorthand; the class minimums
govern.** The stricter reading was offered and not taken.

## THE 86 MARKS — Frython's research task. Sealed d6 = 3, day 39

**The player proposed it:** does the count of eighty-six under the area 2 kitchen
table line up with anything in Zelligar's diary or the area 6 papers?

**Existence check, d6 = 3** — rolled in the open, value recorded here only, same
treatment as the second troglodyte return timer. **Partial.** There is something
to find and it does not close the question:

- The **papers** (party's own salvage, area 6, exp.4 turn 156) are mundane
  household and construction records. **Most recent date: 30+ years ago.** They
  carry *dates*, which is what an 86-day count needs and what nothing else the
  party holds provides.
- **A run can be reconstructed but not anchored.** The household records thin and
  then stop; the stopping is datable to within a season, not to a day. Eighty-six
  days back from a season is a season.
- **What it CANNOT do is name the counter.** No name, no room, no reason. It can
  tell them *roughly when the household stopped being a household*, which is the
  question behind the question, and it will make the eighty-six marks worse
  rather than better.

**Do not deliver this until the work is actually done.** It needs the diary
(Toller has it, and see below) plus days of Frython's time. Roll Frython's INT
when he sits down to it; INT 17 is 75% territory but the *ceiling* is the d6 = 3
above — a good roll finds all of the partial answer, not more than it.

### TOLLER WILL NOT RELEASE THE DIARY — d6 = 1, day 39

Flat refusal to let it leave the shop, and he has three good reasons, all true:
he has a **cut** in it (Frython declined to sell and gave him a share), there is a
**Draycot enquiry** already sniffing at his stock, and a dealer does not send his
most-talked-about object across town to a sickroom.

**But he is not refusing to help.** Offer, in this order, only if pressed:
1. Frython is welcome in the shop the moment he is off his back — **day 45**.
2. Tristle may read it *there*, evenings, during tuition.
3. **Toller will do the date-work himself** — he reads, dates are his trade, and
   it is his cut too. This is the one he actually wants.
