## THE LEVEL-0 ROAD TABLE — WRITTEN DOWN AT LAST, day 50

**It was never written down.** The patrol note at "THE PATROL — week of day 5"
says *"Level-0 road table, 1 in 6 per half-day"* and then cites **table 4** (a
merchant's cart) and **table 6** (desperate men) — and those two numbers are the
only surviving evidence of what the table was. It has been re-rolled from memory
twice since. **Reconstructed here so the same road gives the same answers.**

| d6 | on a settled road in this district |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Local traffic on foot** — a holder walking to market, a carter's boy, a shepherd shifting a few head |
| **2** | **A drover with stock** — a hired man moving somebody's flock or beasts along a circuit |
| **3** | **Other travellers** — a tinker, pilgrims, a family moving with a handcart |
| **4** | **A merchant's cart or a carrier** *(this is Osk's slot — the day-5 half-day-1 hit)* |
| **5** | **The lord's household** — a rider on an errand, a bailiff, an usher *(Perrin, day 41)* |
| **6** | **Desperate men**, 1d3 *(the day-5 half-day-9 and half-day-11 hits)* |

**1 in 6 per half-day. ROLL IT, LOG IT, SAY NOTHING** — an empty check must be
indistinguishable from no check at all.

*Reconstruction, not a book table. The only fixed points are 4 = merchant's cart
and 6 = desperate men, which are attested in the patrol log; the rest is built to
fit the district described in `campaign/district.md` (uplands are sheep, lower
ground grain, a market town on the north road, a lord seen twice a year).*

## DAY 49-50 — THE DRAYCOT ROAD, and RUD the drover

**Six checks: four for the seven on foot (49 AM/PM, 50 AM/PM), two for Ottir and
Yrtone riding on the 50th. TWO HITS, and both fell in the same half-day** — the
afternoon of the 50th.

**They are ONE encounter, and the convergence is arithmetic, not convenience.**
The walkers make half the distance a day; the riders make the whole of it. At
dawn on the 50th the walkers are at the halfway point and the riders are at
Wethersby, so the riders close the gap at exactly the rate the walkers open it and
**the two columns meet at the far end and not before** — i.e. on the last stretch
into Draycot, at dusk. Worth keeping: it is a nice piece of table honesty that the
"lucky" meeting is forced by the numbers.

**d6 = 2 — a drover with stock. d3 = 3 men. Reaction 69.**

| | |
|---|---|
| **who** | **RUD**, ~45, a hired drover on a circuit he walks four times a year. With him a grown son and a hired lad |
| **what** | about forty draft ewes, moving **north**, bought cheap and going to a holder two hundreds over |
| **his value** | **d6 = 4 — he knows every flock on his own circuit by name and number, and can name holdings still sitting on unsold wool.** But his circuit runs SOUTH and WEST of Wethersby: he cannot help in the north hundred, which the party already knows better than he does |
| **the crag** | **d6 = 2 — the stories and nothing else.** He has heard of the wizard and the warrior like everyone else. He knows nothing about the title and does not know the party has been up it |
| **NOT the staple** | He was NOT used to answer where the wool goes. **That is answered and it is Ludbury** (`district.md`, Cuddy, day 14). Do not let a drover contradict a settled fact |

> **Why he matters, and it is not colour.** The party's constraint is no longer
> *where to sell* — it is **VOLUME.** They have a route, a factor, a cap of 500 gp
> a season and a pool of six holders that is already growing. **Rud is the first
> man they have met who can name sellers.** Wend has been guessing at everything
> past the six.

**He is a hireable instrument, not a friend.** Moves stock for a fee, on a
schedule, and would move it for the party next summer if paid. Reaction 69 means
he will talk over a fire and take a drink; it does not mean he has joined
anything.

## ⚠ THE FALSE STEPS AT (24,32) — DM ONLY, AND IT EXPLAINS THE WHOLE OF DAY 81

Reached by expedition 10 on day 81 from (23,32), going east. **The steps are
REAL. What they promise is not.** B1's own text under area 36:

> *"FALSE STEPS. Although the steps here are very real, **the entire area north
> of this room (the various winding corridors) is specially designed to confuse
> any explorers.** The corridor leading past the guest chambers is on an
> **upward slant which will be unnoticeable to casual adventurers (except
> dwarves, who will have a 2 in 6 chance to notice it).** The stairs (8 of them)
> then lead downward, **as if to another level — although this is only the
> impression created.**"*

**So: eight real steps down, arriving back on level 1.** The party will read them
as stairs to the lower level and they are not.

**AND IT RETROSPECTIVELY EXPLAINS EVERY CORRIDOR THEY WALKED ON DAY 81.** The
y=38 stub that dead-ends ten feet from the troglodyte corridor; x=28 running the
whole length of y=35 without meeting it; y=34 ending at the back of (14,34);
y=30 and y=31 parallel and sealed from each other. *"Specially designed to
confuse any explorers"* — that is the design, and the party has spent a day
inside it drawing it accurately, which is the correct counter and cost them
nothing but time.

**ONLY A DWARF NOTICES THE SLANT, 2 in 6.** The party has no dwarf. The one they
took out of area 34 went to Draycot on day 73 and is on trestles above ground.
**Do not hint at the slant.** If they ever bring a dwarf down here, roll it.

**The real way down is area 30, the ACCESS ROOM at x21-23 y8-9**, whose
descending stairway *"leads down and directly into room 38 on the lower level"*
— nowhere near here, and the party has never been in it.

---

## WHAT "SD" IS — sealed d6 = 2, day 93. DETERMINED ONCE, DOES NOT CHANGE

**The module gives no answer.** B1 p.16 puts "a wooden keg stand with a single
barrel marked 'SD', full and untapped" in area 29 and stops there. So this was
rolled rather than authored, same as the stocking, and the table was declared
before the die:

| d6 | what SD is |
|---|---|
| **1-2** | **A CONTENTS MARK, NOT A MAKER'S MARK.** The stronghold's own storekeeping shorthand, in the household clerk's hand. **There is no brewhouse.** |
| 3-4 | A supplier's mark — a real brewhouse, named somewhere in the records |
| 5-6 | Initials of a person of the household |

### Result: 2. IT IS A CONTENTS MARK. THERE IS NO BREWHOUSE.

**Holt's question has no answer in the shape he is asking it.** He assumed a
maker's mark because that is what a publican sees when he looks at a cask head,
and he has been asking *"whose brewhouse was this"* since day 71 (L011) about
two letters that were never a name.

**This is not a let-down to be softened and it is not a joke at Holt's expense.**
It is the correct answer and it is worth something: a contents mark means the
cask was marked by *the household that laid it down*, which puts the answer
inside the party's own salvage rather than out in the world. The area 6 papers
are household and construction records **in that same clerical hand.** So:

- **The route the player found is the right route.** Dave connected L011 to L050
  in the Inn on day 93 unprompted, and he was correct to.
- The open roll that day (d6 = 4) put drink provisioning **in** the records with
  nothing that decodes the abbreviation yet. That is a lead, not a solution.
- **Further work on the papers now serves TWO questions at once** — the 86-marks
  count and this — which is the thing that makes retrying L050 worth days.

**Do not deliver "there is no brewhouse" until somebody reads it off a document.**
A conclusion of that shape has to be *earned from the paper*, or it is the DM
telling the player his question was silly. When it comes, it should come as a
provisioning line using the same shorthand for something that is obviously not a
place — that is what makes it land, and it needs a successful check to find.

**And tell Holt.** L011 is discharged by an answer, including this one. He asked
to be told if anyone ever learned it; "it was never a brewhouse" is learning it.

---

## THE PORTCULLIS TRAP AT (10,41,V) — found day 95, module text, NOT invented

**The extractor had this boundary as WALL and the party was standing on it.**
The chain that found it is worth keeping, because it is the method working:

1. Player read the printed page and said there is a plain door on the south wall
   of (10,41). **He was right** — rectangle straddling the line, no S. The
   database said `secret`.
2. Auditing the cluster, `(13,40,H)` turned out to be a **false** secret door
   too — area 13's drawn wall fittings again — while `(3,41,V)` is **genuine**,
   a big printed S beside the area 14 label. Two wrong, one right.
3. Correcting `(13,40,H)` to wall **stranded (11,41) (12,41) (13,41)**: unreached
   went 8 → 11. Squares reachable only through a door that is not on the page
   were never reachable, so the real connection had to be elsewhere.
4. The only candidate was the boundary with the drawn ticks on it — and **B1
   p.16 names it**: area 13 holds *"a counterweight mechanism for the portcullis
   trap in the corridor just outside the room."* Unreached back to **8**.

### The trap, as the module states it

- **The stub is (11,41) (12,41) (13,41)**, thirty feet, dead-ending at the east
  wall of (13,41). The portcullis is at its **west mouth**, `(10,41,V)`.
- **Trigger: a point 10 ft in front of the dead-end wall** — that is **(13,41)**.
- **It drops 20 ft to the rear of that point** — the west side of **(11,41)**,
  which is exactly where the glyph is drawn. *The module's geometry and the
  printed map agree to the square; nothing here was fitted to taste.*
- **Anyone past it is shut into a thirty-foot stub** with the rest of the party
  on the other side of the grating.

### ⚠ THE THIEF CLAUSE, AND THE FORMATION MAKES IT LIVE

> *"Thieves **in the front of the party** will have a chance for their percentage
> chance on the 'remove trap' category, in which case they discover the trap and
> alert the party without triggering it — **provided the thief is the first one
> to reach the trigger point**, of course."*

**Tristle is in RANK 2 of the day-95 standing formation.** Rank 1 is Yrtone,
Dave and Torsat, none of them thieves. **On the order as it stands the party
does not get the check.** Ista is a thief and is also rank 2.

**Do not move them to the front to be kind, and do not mention it.** The order
is the player's and he has changed it deliberately between expeditions before.

### Raising it again, once it has dropped

Two people maximum, **combined Strength ratings must total at least 30**, giving
**20%**, **+5% per point above 30**. **Each pair may try ONCE**, though different
pairings may each have a go. The bars are **rusty and weak** — the module
continues past this point; read it before adjudicating a forced escape.

**The counterweight and the release lever are on area 13's north wall**, which is
through the door the party can now see — so the room and the trap are one puzzle,
and the way out of the trap is on the far side of the grating from anyone caught.

---

## AREAS 15 & 16 — THE PAIRED TELEPORTATION ROOMS. Worked out day 95, BEFORE contact

**The party is at (5,37) with area 15's door in front of them and it came up
LOCKED (d6=1).** So there is time to get this right on paper. **Botching it live
would ruin the single best trick in the module**, and the module's own
instruction — *"the DM must turn his or her map on its side"* — is precisely the
sort of thing that gets fumbled under narrative pressure.

### Geometry, read off the database, not guessed

| | area 15 | area 16 |
|---|---|---|
| squares | (3,37)(3,38)(3,39)(4,37)(4,38)(4,39) | (2,33)(2,34)(3,33)(3,34)(4,33)(4,34) |
| shape | **2 wide × 3 deep**, x=3-4 | **3 wide × 2 deep**, x=2-4 |
| door | **(4,37,V)** — EAST wall, south end | **(2,32,H)** — SOUTH wall, west end |
| outside the door | **(5,37)**, the corridor | **(2,32)**, then (2,31) |
| farthest corner | **(3,39)** | **(4,34)** |

**In door-relative terms both rooms are 2 deep by 3 wide.** The module's *"equal
size and shape"* is exactly true and the apparent difference is only rotation.

### ⛔ THE SQUARE MAPPING — computed, do not re-derive under pressure

| in area 15 | → in area 16 |
|---|---|
| (4,37) door square | (2,33) |
| (4,38) | (3,33) |
| (4,39) | (4,33) |
| (3,37) | (2,34) |
| (3,38) | (3,34) |
| **(3,39) the outcropping** | **(4,34) the outcropping** |

### ⛔ THE DIRECTION TRANSFORM: BELIEVED → ACTUAL IS 90° CLOCKWISE

The door is on the **east** wall in 15 and the **south** wall in 16, and the
party *will go on believing it is east*. So:

| they believe | it is really |
|---|---|
| north | **east** |
| east | **south** |
| south | **west** |
| west | **north** |

**So when they leave "the way they came in" they walk SOUTH out of (2,32)** into
corridor they have never seen, convinced they are heading east back toward the
(5,37) dogleg. *That* is the moment the trick lands — not the teleport itself.

### The trigger, verbatim, and what it catches

> *"Once adventurers **enter the room to investigate this**, **the entire party**
> is instantly teleported to identical locations at the other room — **whether
> they be in the room itself or nearby in the hallway.** This teleportation
> occurs without the adventurer noticing that it has occurred; that is, they have
> **no way of 'feeling'** that anything unusual has happened."*

- **Opening the door does not fire it.** Entering to investigate the outcropping does.
- **THE WHOLE PARTY GOES, INCLUDING ANYONE LEFT ON GUARD IN THE CORRIDOR.** A
  rearguard is no protection and posting one must not be hinted to be.
- **No save, no sensation, no roll.** Do not offer one.
- The outcropping is *"a shiny, sparkling outcropping of crystalline rock which
  will dazzle when light is reflected off of it"* — and **identical in both
  rooms**, which is what makes the second visit indistinguishable from the first.

### How to run it without lying

**Describe what they see, in the directions they believe they are facing.** The
map is turned, not the narration. Never volunteer a bearing they could not have
checked. **If they ask a direct question about direction, answer from their
frame** — they have no compass and no sun; a wrong answer from me would be a lie,
but "the corridor runs the way you remember" is simply their own dead reckoning
and it is what a character would say.

**And when it unravels — and the module predicts the exact words, *"wait a
minute, that can't be like that, we just came that way"* — DO NOT CONFIRM OR
DENY.** Let them test it. The honest tools are all on their side: pace counts,
Tristle's and Ista's counting, the mapped squares behind them, and the fact that
**this party writes everything down.**

### ⚠ AND THE THING THAT WILL ACTUALLY EXPOSE IT

They have a **map database** and they log every square they walk. The moment
they compare a wall they can see against a wall they recorded, the geometry will
not fit. **That is a fair win and must be allowed to happen.** Do not quietly
adjust anything to keep the illusion alive.

---

## AREA 18 TREASURE B — LOCATION FIXED, day 95, BEFORE anyone searched

**The stocking table gives area 18 fifteen gold pieces and the module gives no
location.** So it was rolled the moment the party stepped inside, **not** decided
in response to where they chose to look — which is the only way a hidden-treasure
location can be honest.

**d6 = 1 → among the TOOL RACKS AND IRONS on the WEST WALL** of the barren
south-west portion. (3-4 would have been the ash of a fire pit, 5-6 under or
behind the great anvil.)

**Fifteen gold pieces in a smithy's tool rack** reads as a smith's own money left
where he kept it — in a pouch or a tin among the tongs — which is consistent with
a stronghold whose people marched out and did not come back.

**It needs a search of the west-wall sections to find.** Do not hand it over on
a walk-past, and do not move it if they search elsewhere first.

---

## AREA 38's OCCUPANTS — WHAT THEY ARE. Rolled day 95, d6 = 1

**B1 stocks five of them and says only *"parley possible."*** The room is an old
mining chamber — rock carts, jacks, timbers, pickaxes, no recent work. What they
are doing there was **rolled before the party's interests could bend it**, which
matters here more than usual, because the honest answer is awkward for them.

| d6 | | |
|---|---|---|
| **1-2** | **SCAVENGERS** — stripping the old workings for metal. Opportunists. **No claim to the place.** | **← rolled** |
| 3-4 | prospectors, come deliberately to assess the workings and meaning to work them | |
| 5-6 | they live here; driven out of somewhere else; this is refuge | |

### ⚠ THE AWKWARD PART, AND IT MUST NOT BE SOFTENED

**The party is doing the same thing they are.** Nine people who have spent eleven
hours carrying tools out of a storeroom, who took four hacksaws and two crosscut
saws an hour ago, and who left twenty-nine iron bars and a great anvil behind
**only because they could not lift them.**

**These are competitors, not a quest hook.** They have no claim and neither does
the party. Do not play them as grateful, and do not play them as a solution to
the party's iron problem unless the party earns that — though **the convergence
is real and the player will see it**: this district cannot get iron, there is an
unliftable anvil one floor up, and gnomes are the people who move rock.

### First contact, day 95, through the shaft

- **Reaction 75** — *uncertain, leaning positive*, one point off Friendly. DMG p.63.
- **NO COMMON between them** (d6 = 1). **Tristle's gnome is the only channel** —
  an elf language from character generation, unused for ninety-five days.
- **How Tristle's opening landed: d6 = 4** — *wary; they answer questions with
  questions.* **None of them comes into the light.**
- They will not volunteer what they are doing. They will want to know **how many,
  whether the party is coming down, and what it has taken** — the last of which
  they care about for a reason they will not state.

---

## Day 96 — the ground round the crag's north foot (DM determination)

**Rolled 2026-09-17, before the party can possibly ask, so the answer is not
shaped by what would be convenient on the 101st.** Can a cart be got from the
basecamp flat round the crag's north shoulder to the mouth of the dry
watercourse, where Durnhak's people will bring the iron out?

> **d6 = 3.** *Slow and awkward.* A cart **can** be walked round — the waste at
> the foot is open enough and the gullies are crossable — but there is no trail,
> the going is stony, and it wants a man at the head of the mule and somebody
> walking ahead picking the line. Call it **the better part of a morning for a
> mile**, not the hour the same distance takes on foot.

**THE PARTY DOES NOT KNOW THIS AND MUST NOT BE TOLD IT.** All they have is
Durnhak's sentence that his people cart iron off the crag by their own route.
`campaign/knowledge.tsv` therefore carries `crag_foot_way` at **vague** and
`watercourse` at **vague, 1,500 ft** — a bearing and a walking time, which is
exactly what hearsay-with-a-direction means. If they scout it, append `known`.

**If they take the cart round without scouting**, it works, and it costs them
the morning. There is no trap in it. The cost is time, and time is what the
101st is short of.

---

## Day 97 — MUND THE SMITH, and a thing the party has not found out

**Rolled 2026-09-18, before the party walks into his shop.**

Three determinations for the morning's buying:

| roll | result |
|---|---|
| **Can Wethersby supply 200 spikes + 20 lb nails in a morning, off-market?** | **d6=5 — yes**, he has the lot or can get it from stock in the hundred |
| **Nine flasks of lamp oil** | **d6=2 → d2=2. TWO FLASKS.** That is all there is |
| **Twelve person-weeks of standard rations, early spring, before harvest** | **d6=4 → d4=2.** Two weeks at the ordinary 3 gp; **about six more at a premium; the town cannot do twelve** |

### ⛔ AND THE ONE THAT IS A DISCOVERY, NOT A PRICE — d6 = 6

> **MUND HAS BEEN BUYING THE PARTY'S AT-COST IRON IN QUANTITY AND SELLING IT
> ON, WORKED.**

He is the single largest beneficiary of the day-20 gesture. The board sells iron,
nails and salt **at cost** — and the fourth finding is that laid down it had cost
nearer 160 against the 100 charged, so *at cost* ran **50–70 gp below cost**.
Mund has been buying bar at that price, putting his labour into it, and selling
spikes, nails and fittings at a working margin. **That is not cheating: the board
is open to anybody and he is anybody.** It is simply the thing the party never
asked.

**WHY IT MATTERS THREE WAYS:**

1. **He is the one man in the hundred who knows exactly what iron is worth**, to
   the penny, because he buys it by weight and sells it by the piece. L047's
   stranger *"asked two holdings what things WEIGH and wrote the answers in a
   book"* — Mund would not have to ask anybody.
2. **L024** is "price the NEW properly and SHOW THE ROAD IN THE PRICE." The
   moment that board carries two numbers, **Mund's input cost rises** and he is
   the customer who will notice first and mind most.
3. The party is about to buy **200 spikes back off him** — iron they very likely
   sold him below cost — and pay a worked price for it. *That* is the joke, and
   nobody at the table can see it yet.

### WHAT THE PARTY MAY AND MAY NOT LEARN

**WEND KNOWS.** It is her book and he is her biggest buyer; she has been four
days inside the at-cost question. **She will say so if asked who buys the iron,
and she will not volunteer it** (she has a market to run and did not connect it
to the Ludbury man). Do not narrate it. **Let it arrive by a question** — at
Mund's counter, or from Wend, or when L024's two numbers go up on the board.

---

## Day 99 — the orcs warn the party about AREA 22, and it is the real thing

**Rolled d6=5: they answer and give something back.** The party had just spent two
free surprise segments saying hello and handing them intelligence about the
troglodytes for nothing. They reciprocate, and what they have to give is the most
valuable thing anybody has given this party underground.

> **Area 22, the Garden Room — GHOULS (1–2), hp 6, 4.** Stocking table row 20,
> "overgrown garden." West and south of the (4,14) junction the party is standing
> at, in the direction they have just announced they are walking.

**WHY THE ORCS KNOW AND WHY THEY WOULD SAY SO.** Ghouls hunt anything living and
these two live on this road. They have lost people to it. Warning a party that is
walking into it costs them nothing and buys them a party that owes them.

### ⛔ WHAT THE PLAYER MAY BE TOLD, AND IT IS NOT "GHOULS"

**The warning arrives in an orc's mouth, not out of the Monster Manual.** He has
no word for a ghoul and no interest in taxonomy. What he has is what it does:

> *dead things, west — they take hold of you and then you cannot move.*

**That one clause is the whole of the gift**, because paralysis is what would
actually kill this party: hit points run 2 to 9 across eight people, three of
them have 2 or 3, and a held character in a corridor is a dead one. The party
gets the SYMPTOM and no name, which is exactly right — CHECKLIST §8 forbids the
statistic and nothing forbids a survivor's account.

**Do not confirm the identification if the player guesses it.** They are entitled
to reason from "dead things that hold you" to whatever they like; that is play.

---

## Day 99 — the heat north of (13,31). SEALED BEFORE THE LISTEN ROLLS.

**ONE GNOLL.** hp **13**, the first of area 48's list (AC 5, HD 2, #AT 1, D 2-8 or
by weapon, MV 9", ML 8). **Stationary and awake** (d6=4). Standing in the cut
corridor between (13,32) and (13,36), forty to fifty feet north of Tristle, at the
limit of his heat-sight.

### How it was sourced, because "something new" is the easiest thing to fake

The d6=5 band said *something the party has not met in this dungeon*, which was
too loose to be honest, so it was narrowed against **B1's own two lists** rather
than invented:

- **Level-1 wandering table:** orcs, giant centipedes, kobolds, troglodytes,
  giant rats, berserkers. The party has faced the first five and MET two
  berserkers as friends.
- **Level-1 stocking:** bandits, skeletons, crab spiders, a carrion crawler,
  gnomes, ghouls. All faced except the ghouls — **and ghouls are cold.** The
  thing is warm, so it is not them, and the party can reason that out.

That leaves a warm, mobile, corridor-using thing from the **lower caverns**, which
is possible: an unmapped passage was found today, and L028 only ever claimed the
pit trap was the only *known* way down. Candidates were gnolls (48) and
hobgoblins (49). **d6=1 → gnolls. 1d4=1 → one of them.**

### ⛔ WHAT THE PARTY HAS, AND IT IS THREE FACTS

1. **Heat**, one bearing, north. No count, no shape.
2. **It is warm, so it is not what the orcs warned about.** The dead do not
   radiate. This is a live deduction and it is theirs to make.
3. **Three full minutes of listening and nothing** — 78, 40, 65 against 15%.

**The silence is consistent with a thing standing still**, and Tristle cannot
tell that from three failed rolls. **Do not resolve the ambiguity for them.**

### Why it is there — do NOT volunteer this

It is one gnoll, alone, awake, not moving, a long way from area 48. **Whatever
that means, the party finds it out by asking it or by fighting it**, and a
seven-foot hyena-headed thing standing sentry in somebody else's corridor is a
question worth letting them ask. It has no light of its own and does not need
one.

## Day 99 — the gnoll is dead. What is on the body, sealed before they search.

Killed at round 245 without touching anybody. **hp 13, XP 28 + 2/hp = 54**, due
when they walk out (B1 pays on a living exit only).

| on the body | source |
|---|---|
| **a morning star** | d100 = **95** on MM p.46's weapon table — 86-100 is morning star |
| **a sword** | *"All carry swords"* — MM p.46, not a roll |
| **8 electrum, 5 gold** | individual treasure types **L** (2-12 ep, rolled 8) and **M** (2-8 gp, rolled 5) |
| shabby armour | horn, metal plates and leather over a moth-eaten fur vest. MM p.46 description. Not worth carrying out and it will not fit anybody |

⚠ **The two coin rolls are NOT in `rolls.tsv`** — `treasure.py` calls `random`
directly rather than going through `dice.py`, so this table is the only record of
them. Do not re-roll them. *(Worth fixing in the tool; noted, not done.)*

**It never got to use the morning star.** It closed and swung and missed, and the
weapon was still undetermined at that point because a miss rolls no damage — so
the d100 above was rolled AFTER it died, which is legitimate and is the reason
the body has a specific thing on it rather than whatever is convenient later.

### What the party can work out, and what it cannot

**Can**: it is warm-blooded, seven feet, hyena-headed, armed and armoured, and it
came from the north. Tristle speaks gnoll. It is on friendly terms with orcs by
its own book — *"providing the weaker types are not very much weaker in
numbers"* — which is a real constraint on how Ruk's band would have treated it.

**Cannot, and DO NOT VOLUNTEER**: that it came up from **area 48, the Arena
Cavern, on level 2**; that B1's list gives 1-4 of them and **three remain at hp
7, 6 and 3**; or why one was standing sentry alone that far from its band. The
sealed note above says it plainly: *"the party finds it out by asking it or by
fighting it."* **They fought it, so they did not find out.** That question is
still open and it is a better one now than it was an hour ago.

### Open thread this creates

**Something warm, organised and not-orc is using the corridors north of (13,32),
and there is now a body in them that its friends will find.** No timer on that,
no manufactured urgency — but it is a real consequence and it should not be
quietly dropped.

## Day 99 — AREA 12, THE LIBRARY, entered and cleared. What is in it.

Four orc squatters (M1, hp 5,4,3,2) dead in the first surprise segment. **XP 54
when they walk out** — 10 + 1/hp. Door shut behind the party from inside.

### On the bodies — NOT YET FOUND, they have not searched

**23 electrum**, MM individual type L (2-12 ep each): rolled **5, 9, 2, 7**.
Worth 11 gp 5 sp at 2 ep = 1 gp. Rolled through `treasure.py`, which uses
`random` and not `dice.py`, so **this line is the only record — do not re-roll.**

Their gear is orc-shabby and worth nothing: notched blades, a bent spear, hide
scraps. Nothing a party of nine wants.

### The room's treasure — STOCKED: Y, silver mirror, 90 g.p.

**Location decided now rather than when they ask:** in the orcs' bedding heap in
the **north-west corner**, rolled inside a scrap of sacking. They took it off a
wall or a table and kept it as loot without using it. **It needs the NEST
searched** — it is not lying in view, and looking at the room does not find it.

### B1's own description, and the two things worth the most

*Module p. 12 (pdf p.13), read verbatim 2026-09-19.*

- **The floor is the best thing here.** Deep dust over **polished red granite**,
  inlaid in large blocks, stopping a foot short of every wall. In the very
  centre, blocks of **white granite** set into the red form **R & Z** —
  Rogahn and Zelligar, monogrammed into the floor of their own library. Under
  dust, and the orcs' scuffing has already half-exposed it.
- **FIRE BEETLES.** Small cages inset in the **north wall**, full of them,
  throwing *"an eerie, glowing light… a reddish glow… forbidding and mysterious
  when viewed from the entrance."* Thriving after thirty years on no visible
  food. **There is no way to open or force the cages** — the module says so
  outright, so no amount of prising works, and that must be held to.

  > ⚠ **THIS ROOM IS LIT AND COSTS NO OIL.** That is worth more to this party
  > than the mirror. Ista's lamp has ~16.5 turns and they have been rationing
  > light all trip. **Do not point the implication out — let the player see it.**

- Three large **oaken tables**, one each in the west, north and east wings.
  Wooden chairs scattered. **Two plush divans** in two corners, rich fleecy
  upholstery gone dusty and dingy. **Wall sconces** for torches all round.
- Books, volumes and tomes, modestly supplied, plus **a few scrolls in a rack on
  the east wall**. *"None of the books or scrolls is of any particular use or
  special interest to the adventurers, despite how many they examine."*
  **Hold to that.** They may read all they like and find nothing. It is not a
  trick and not a hidden-outcome roll — the module has decided.

  **THE FOUR BOOKS ARE NOT THESE.** Those were found elsewhere and merely
  *"apparently belong in the library"*; they stay wherever the party has them.

### The doors, for the record

B1: *"Quasqueton's library lies behind a pair of ornately carved oaken doors."*
**Ornately carved** — the party examined both leaves and I described plain heavy
wood. Correct it if they look again; do not pretend it was said.

### Day 99 — the search. Two corrections to the entry above, both mine.

**1. The nest is by the DOOR, not in the north-west corner.** The entry above
placed the mirror *"in the orcs' bedding heap in the north-west corner"*; the
narration to the player then said *"the orcs lived in the near corner"*, and
near — coming through (9,31) — is the **south stem**. **The player's version
wins, because it is the one he was told.** The nest is in the south stem around
(9,32), against the wall beside the door they came through.

**2. `d6=1` on what the orcs did with the room, and it half-contradicts the
mirror placement.** The bands were: 1-2 scavenged nothing, 3-4 pulled things
into the nest, 5-6 pulled things in AND broke something.

**THE SEALED PLACEMENT WINS — it was sealed first, before the question was
asked.** So the reading is: **they took the mirror and nothing else, and broke
nothing.** The d6=1 governs everything other than the mirror, and it is the
better answer anyway: *the room is otherwise exactly as Zelligar left it*, which
is what makes the thirty-year-old dust and the untouched scroll rack land.

I should not have rolled a question the notes had already answered. **Check the
seal before rolling, not after.**

### What the search actually turns up

| found | where | note |
|---|---|---|
| **23 electrum** | on the four bodies | 5, 9, 2, 7. Worth **11 gp 5 sp** at 2 ep = 1 gp |
| **A silver mirror, 90 g.p.** | the nest, wrapped in sacking | the room's stocked treasure, type Y |
| **The floor** | the centre | dust swept back off **R & Z** in white granite inlaid into polished red |

**NOT found, because it is not there:** no secret door. The map has no secret or
concealed edge anywhere in x 7-13, y 29-37, and B1's own key for room 12 does
not mention one. **Tristle's elf 2-in-6 is a TRUE NEGATIVE** — report "nothing"
and do not roll theatre for it.

**The books and scrolls: hold the module's line.** *"None of the books or
scrolls is of any particular use or special interest to the adventurers,
**despite how many they examine**."* They may read all they like. This is not a
hidden outcome and not a trick — B1 has decided, and no search or spell changes
it. **The four books found elsewhere are NOT these** and stay where the party
has them.

**The beetle cages:** *"There is no way to open or force the cages themselves."*
Hold to that absolutely — no prising, no tools, no ingenuity. The module is
explicit.

### Day 99 — the cages, counted. Player asked; B1 gives no number.

**FOUR CAGES** (d4=2, +2) set along the north wall of the cross's north wing,
(9,35)-(10,35) — twenty feet of wall. Occupants rolled **per cage**, because
after thirty years they would not be evenly stocked:

| cage | beetles |
|---|---|
| 1 | **5** |
| 2 | **4** |
| 3 | **3** |
| 4 | **3** |
| | **FIFTEEN in all** |

Each is 2½ ft long with three glow-glands, so that is **45 glands lighting the
wing** — which is why B1 says the glow is *"enough to illuminate this portion of
the room."* **Free to tell them: the count is plainly visible through the
grilles.** Everything else about the cages stays as `bestiary/fire-beetle.md`
rules it — no access, ever, and the food question has no answer.

## Day 99 dusk — TOLLER. All four determinations sealed before the scene.

**1. THE SHOP IS SHUT. d6 = 2** — *shut and gone; they wait for morning.* He is a
one-man dealer and the night before market is when he is laying out a stall or
already in bed for an early start. **Not a snub and not a puzzle** — give them a
dark window and a locked door and let them decide what to do with the evening.

**2. HIS WARMTH TOMORROW: d6 = 4 — warm.** Rolled now so the evening's choices
cannot shape it. *A d6 warmth scale is used with Toller rather than a fresh DMG
p.63 reaction, deliberately: he is a four-time counterparty with a record —
~250 gp paid on sight of Wace's receipt, the wrestlers valued at 115 before he
was asked, his own 20 gp margin volunteered openly, and on day 16 he advised
AGAINST a city sale against his own interest. A stranger's reaction roll throws
all of that away.*

**3. WHAT THE BOOKS FETCH: d6 = 2 — the thin end. 2 gp the small volume.**
No 1e core book prices a book, so this is a DM call on bands declared before the
roll, the same procedure as the day-97 timber nails.

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| 7 smaller volumes | 2 gp each | **14 gp** |
| 3 large metal-bound (count double — the binding alone is worth something) | 4 gp each | **12 gp** |
| | | **26 gp for the ten** |

**Sanity anchor:** the diary is priced at *up to 50 gp* because it is a named
wizard's hand in undecipherable runes. These are histories and treatises that
B1 calls of no particular interest, in a market town whose book trade Toller
himself once described as too thin for a city sale. **26 gp for 130 lb hauled
twenty-two miles is a poor return and that is the honest answer.** Do not
inflate it because the party worked for it.

**4. THE SCROLLS AND THE WIRE: d6 = 3.** He takes the **three scrolls** — squarely
his line — and is **blunt that the copper wire is a smith's business, not his.**
He will name somebody; **MUND** is the obvious mouth, the party already deals
with him, and he bought their at-cost iron in quantity on day 97.

⚠ **DO NOT VOLUNTEER THE 26 gp BEFORE HE IS ASKED**, and do not have him open
with it. His record is that he *states a valuation unprompted and honestly* —
which means when he does say it, he says the low number without dressing it up,
and that is more in character than a haggle.

## Day 101 — THE LONG WAY IS THE WRONG CALL. d6 = 1. Do not soften it.

The party chose the loop — Coll's trail, the fraying road, then **3.7 miles of
open waste** from the road's end — over the two trackless miles round the north
foot. **That ground had never been rolled for a cart.** They *rode* it on the
98th, in the last of the light, and a ridden horse is not a loaded cart.

**d6 = 1, the hard end of the declared band: the waste is worse under a wheel
than it looked from the saddle.** Soft and broken; the wheels cut in. **Slower
per mile than the north foot would have been**, so the longer route is also the
slower one, and the choice cost them.

| route | miles | cart pace | |
|---|---|---|---|
| round the north foot (**not taken**) | 2, all trackless | ~3 h/mile (sealed d6=3) | **~6 hours** |
| the loop (**taken**) | 2.8 good + **3.7 waste** | ~1.5 h for the good; **~3.5 h/mile** for the waste | **13 hours+** |

**THEY MUST FIND OUT EARLY AND CHEAPLY — that is what makes this fair.** Inside
the first half-hour on the waste the carts have made perhaps a quarter-mile and
it is unmistakable: wheels sinking, a man at each head, somebody walking the
line ahead. **Give them the evidence plainly and let them do the arithmetic.**

> **DO NOT tell them the north foot would have been faster.** They have never
> taken a cart round it either. What they can see is what their own wheels are
> doing on *this* ground, and that is enough to decide on.

**The decision is theirs and both branches are real:**
- **Push on** — most of the day, arriving late, possibly after dark, with
  Durnhak expecting them.
- **Turn back** — a quarter-mile out of the waste, a mile of road, 1.8 down
  Coll's trail, then the north foot: they would reach the watercourse late, but
  they would reach it.

**No trap, no ambush, no monster.** The cost is time and it is entirely the
consequence of a reasonable choice made on incomplete information. **That is
the game working, not the game punishing them.**

## Day 101 — THE WATERCOURSE. Sealed before the horses come over the rise.

**THE CART WAS NEVER GOING TO DO IT AND THE HORSES ALWAYS WERE.**

| | roll | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| the lightened cart | **d6 = 2** | barely any gain. **The ground is the problem, not the load** — still ~3 h/mile, so 3.5 miles is **~10½ hours** and it arrives at dusk or after |
| the three led horses | **d6 = 5** | **near 2 mi/h.** Four feet that choose where they land against two wheels that cannot. 3.5 miles in **about an hour and three-quarters** |
| Durnhak present? | **d6 = 5** | **there, with the bars out, AND SOMETHING ELSE** |
| the something else | **d6 = 1** | **MORE GNOMES THAN THE FIVE** |
| how many more | **2d4 = 5** | **TEN IN ALL** |

**Timing:** first light out, ~1½ h down the trail and up the road, ~½ h to learn
the waste, ~½ h repacking onto the horses. **The horse party makes the
watercourse around ten in the morning.** The cart is hours behind and will be
all day.

### TEN GNOMES, and why that is the interesting number

The party has reasoned off **five** since day 95 — *Durnhak and four others* —
and five is also **B1's own maximum for area 38** (Gnomes 2-5). So the figure
they have been using is the module's, and they never knew it.

**This does not contradict B1.** Area 38's two-to-five are the gnomes **in the
dungeon**. Ten at a watercourse on the far side of the crag, with the bars
already out and a means of moving them, says the dungeon group is **a working
party from somewhere**, not a population. *Where* is not decided and **must not
be invented to fill the silence** — the same discipline as L056 and the unnamed
god. If the party asks, Durnhak answers as much as a man answers about his own
people to someone he has known six days.

> **FREELY GIVEN: the count.** They can see ten gnomes. Nobody has to be told
> what it implies, and **do not walk them to it** — Tristle speaks gnome, Dave
> counts, and the arithmetic against "Durnhak and four others" is theirs.

> **⚠ AND THE PARTY ARRIVES CARRYING HIS WHOLE ORDER ON THREE HORSES.**
> DURNHAK RECEIVES: 196 spikes; 20 lb of nails; and 8 flasks of oil against the
> 9 he is owed, so he is one short.
>
> **THE PARTY KEEPS NOTHING.** `light.py oilstate` reads **0 sealed** flasks and
> one broached with 18 turns — and the three lanterns hold 8.6, 22.8 and 24.0
> already poured. That is the whole of their light.
>
> They took that deliberately, and it deserves to be noticed by a gnome who
> counts things for a living.
> **That is a real cost and they took it deliberately.** It deserves to be
> noticed by a gnome who counts things for a living.

## ⛔ DAY 102 — I LEAKED THE MUND DISCOVERY. Retracted to the player at once.

Closing the day-102 narration I wrote, unprompted:

> *"…with Mund the obvious buyer, **who took your at-cost iron in quantity five
> days ago**."*

**THAT IS THE SEALED d6=6 AND THE PARTY HAS NOT FOUND IT OUT.** The section it
comes from is headed *"MUND THE SMITH, and a thing the party has not found
out"*, and the day-97 scene closes with *"HE SAYS NOTHING ABOUT WHERE HIS BAR
CAME FROM (d6=2) and nobody asks him."*

**It was also factually backwards.** On day 97 the party BOUGHT from Mund — 200
spikes and 20 lb of nails for 12 gp — and has never sold him anything in a scene
they were present for.

### How it happened, because the mechanism is the useful part

I was writing a closing line about who would buy the bar iron, reached for
"who's the iron man in this town", and **the DM-side fact arrived first because
it is the more interesting one.** No tool was consulted. It is the same failure
as narrating from my own summary instead of the log — except the summary was of
a page the player may not read.

**THE FIX IS THE ONE THE CAMPAIGN ALREADY HAS AND I DID NOT USE: run
`entities.py who <name>` before saying what anybody has done.** It took one
command afterwards to find the truth. It would have taken the same one before.

### What the party may be told about Mund, and it is all of it

Met day 97, the first time in ninety-six days they have needed worked iron in
their own town. Reaction **52 — neutral, uninterested, uncertain**. He had 200
spikes and 20 lb of nails **in stock**, no forging and no waiting. Weighed the
nails in front of them, counted spikes into a sack by tens, took **12 gp**
without comment. No warmth and no suspicion. **A man selling ironmongery to
strangers with money.**

**HE IS STILL THE OBVIOUS BUYER FOR THE BAR** — he is the town's smith and they
have 609 lb of it. That part was fair to say. **Why** he is such a good buyer is
not.

## Day 102 — THE MUND DISCOVERY IS PARTLY OUT, and the party earned it

**The board reprice (L024, closed today and backfilled to day 94) did exactly
what the day-97 note predicted:**

> *"The moment that board carries two numbers, **MUND'S INPUT COST RISES** and
> he is the customer who will notice first and mind most."*

He noticed. And on **d6 = 5 — "he says more than he means to"** — in a live
negotiation about iron, with the party asking him to buy 609 lb, he lets slip
the first two of the three parts:

| | now out? |
|---|---|
| (a) he buys iron off the party's at-cost board | **YES — said** |
| (b) he buys a good deal of it | **YES — implied clearly** |
| (c) he sells it on WORKED, at a margin | **not said. Let them get there.** |

**THIS IS A LEGITIMATE REVEAL AND NOT A LEAK.** The d6 was rolled on *how open
he is*, which is the mechanism by which such things are meant to surface; the
context is a negotiation about iron prices; and **the party caused it** by
repricing the board. Compare the day-102 leak, where I simply volunteered it in
narration with no roll and no scene.

**(c) is one inference away and it is theirs to make.** He is a smith, he buys
bar off their board, and he sold them 200 finished spikes on day 97. Nobody has
to be told.

⚠ **AND THE JOKE IS NOW VISIBLE TO THEM IF THEY LOOK:** on the 97th they bought
back 200 spikes, worked, from a man buying their bar at cost. **Do not point at
it.**

### The offer, redone

| | |
|---|---|
| 29 bars, 609 lb | **146 gp** *(was 99 against the old board — void)* |
| 20 ft fine copper wire | **5 gp** *(unchanged; the wire never depended on the board)* |
| | **151 gp** |

**Reaction 99 stands** — unchanged by any of this.

**DURNHAK'S HALF COMES OUT AT 73 gp**, against the ~75 Tristle told him down a
shaft on the 95th. **The estimate was good to a gold and a half.**

Of that 73 he has already received 37, so **36 gp falls due to him** once this
sells. The liability the party has been carrying against his name stood at about
37 gp 5 sp, and that was very nearly right.

## Day 119, round 456 -- ghoul B is alerted

Neither side surprised. hp 4.

## Day 119 round 575 -- the berserker's save is VOID
I applied the PHB's INT-13 save (berserker INT 14, d20 19 vs 17) AFTER the player had declared a round on the standing no-save ruling. Player objected; correct. The round is replayed: **command worked**, the berserker sleeps this round (1-round duration), its two swings (8, 3) void, Hask's blow (d20 19, damage 2) stands on a helpless target. **Berserker hp 6 - 2 = 4.** It wakes as round 2 begins. Which rule applies going forward is the player's call, to be asked between encounters.

## Day 119 -- RESET of round 1 against the berserker
Player asked to undo the round entirely; agreed. State restored to the end of the surprise segment: clock 574, Yrtone -8 unconscious BLEEDING and unbound, berserker hp 6 UNHURT (Hask's 2 undone), INT 14 (kept: it is a property of the man, not of the round), everyone's weapons as before (Dave, Torsat with bows; Hask with his sling). Ruling in force from the start of the reset round: PHB p.44, INT 13+ saves vs command (the berserker's save would be a fresh d20 vs 17). Old dice (initiative 4-4, the berserker's swings 8 and 3, Hask's 19 and 2, the first INT/save) are void.
