Ista's lantern still burning open.

## Day 138 -- expedition 29 ends at the cave mouth; down to the basecamp for the sledges
Out at round 133 (about 5:10 pm). Ista's lantern snuffed at the mouth as on every exit (light.py out, 10.7 turns left), told to the player. THE PLAYER'S PLAN: down to the basecamp, leave the orc arms, the eight area-13 mallets (Tristle keeps his own) and the box of pegs, take the four sledges (and the ONE rope the party has: the ropes he thought were at the basecamp are one on the party and three at Wend's barn; the heavy coil is Coll's; I told him and stopped before moving anyone), bring them up to the cave for the eight empty barrels. The eight barrels stand at the cave mouth unattended.
Trail down half an hour at 9" (party rate; kit 2,471 gp-wt after the drop, room to spare): at the basecamp about 5:40. The camp: nobody there (Coll's crew paid off and gone south; the horses were with the trio; the lean-to, four sledges, twenty sealed flasks, camp food). Drop-off and pick-up about five minutes: 5:45. Booked: orc arms, mallets and pegs from party to basecamp (carry.tsv; a shell slip wrote ten malformed rows first, repaired: removed as shell corruption, they never happened at the table). The sledges are dragged along the sledge-way, not carried: 800 gp-wt each is not booked to anyone until they are hauled up. The climb up with four empty sledges: about half an hour (day-126 crew precedent). It gets dark about 6. Nothing on the trail is clocked (no dungeon clock outdoors; expedition 30 starts at the mouth).

## Day 138 about 5:45-6:15 pm -- back up to the cave mouth with the sledges (expedition 30 starts)
The player relit Ista's lantern at the basecamp for the climb. Trail half an hour up with four empty sledges, dragged along the sledge-way (not carried; booked from basecamp to cave_mouth), and the one rope the party has. Outside the dungeon clock the lantern burnt 3 turns: light.py anchor 7.7 turns on arrival (10.7 - 3.0; precedent day 119). Expedition 30 started at round 0 at the mouth, about 6:15 pm, dark; no checks (nothing clocked on the trail; the anchor jitter pre-rolled and hidden). The eight empty barrels stand at the cave mouth as left; nothing has touched them.

## Day 138 about 6:15-7:05 pm -- the eight empty barrels sledged down (expedition 30, no dungeon time)
RULINGS (mine, said first): a sledge is built to carry ONE barrel (day 126: one full barrel and four men, braking at the road's fixed post); an EMPTY barrel is 100 lb but bulky, so the limit is bulk, not weight: TWO to a sledge, lashed side by side, 280 lb with the sledge (80 lb) against 540 lb on day 126; TWO hands to a sledge instead of four, so four sledges take the whole eight in ONE run with eight hands on the poles, one on the lantern and one free. The braking gear at the steep pitch is a fixture of the road (day 128 note), so the party's own single coil is not needed for it. Lashing five minutes, the run down forty (day-126 precedent for a loaded run), at the basecamp about 7:05 pm. Nothing on the way; nothing clocked outdoors.
Lantern: burnt 4.5 turns outside the dungeon clock (7.7 to 3.2, LOW), then topped off by me at the basecamp as the standing arrangement: broached flask and part of one sealed: 24.0 turns; pool 23 sealed + 1 broached 20.1. carry.tsv: one flask off the party (four in hand now, 20 sealed at the basecamp with the rest). The barrels and sledges booked cave_mouth to basecamp; the sledges stay down until someone fetches them.

## Day 138 about 7:05-8:30 pm -- barrels stored, dinner, Brand's dance
The eight empty barrels unlashed and stood under the lean-to's lee, bungs out, on their sides against the back wall and chocked (stable; booked at the basecamp). Dinner for ten, camp food 0.48 person-week (now 4.32), no dice. Dave asks Brand for a dance. No reaction roll (known man, played from the record: plain, stubborn, warm, has never been asked to perform, wants to belong, will not be told what to be). He agrees with a shrug and a grin at his own expense: one dance, the one they do at the end of harvest in his own country, 'not pretty'. AUTHORED, no dice: heavy and stamping, hands on the hips, heels on the ground in a rhythm the fire's flat stones give back, a slow circle, then a leap and a spin that ends with a shout. Nothing declared for the party's reaction: left to the player.
Player asked for my view on agendas for Brand (letters, choosing a class): discussed (the standing loop on his profession; Frython teaching him letters since day 100, d6 = 4 slow and stubborn, INT 10). Not built; awaiting the player's word.

## Day 138 evening -- Brand's letters put on an agenda and caught up
Built at the player's word: A020 (opened day 100, closed day 104, Frython away to Ludbury; no tick fell due) and A021 (opened day 119, both at the basecamp; weekly, ceiling 16). Table d6, declared first: 1-3 hold (a bad week, he loses what he had), 4-6 progress a stage (1 alphabet, 2 his own name, 3 simple words, 4 a short line, 5 a plain notice); at the fifth progress the DM closes it and Brand raises his profession himself. Ticks rolled and, because Brand is with the party, DELIVERED AT ONCE: day 126 d6 = 6 progress (stage 1: the alphabet); day 133 d6 = 3 hold (a bad week: he has some letters shaky and mixes a few pairs). NOW: Brand knows most of the alphabet, cannot yet write or read his own name, and is shaky on the confusable pairs (b and d, p and q). Next tick due day 140. The class choice itself is NOT rolled.

## Day 138 into 139 -- the night at the basecamp
The party claps and cheers Brand's dance (the player's word; 'Bravo' from Dave and others). Bed about 9 pm, the lean-to and the fire, the barrels stacked under the lean-to's lee; Ista's lantern out since about 7:15 (24.0 turns). First light about 6 am, no dice (the day-124 precedent: nothing on the basecamp road at night). Everyone at full hit points; no spells were cast since the afternoon re-memorising, so the night's rest recovers nothing; nobody has been hurt. Day 139 opened (log.py day); the offscreen queue rolled no tick. TODAY: Durnhak at the watercourse at noon (bring the 36 gp; the party carries its standing working purse) (audit-skip); Brand's next lesson tick is due on day 140. The party has not yet said how it gets to the watercourse or when it leaves.

## Day 139 morning -- breakfast, lunch packed for twenty, the walk to the watercourse
Breakfast for ten (0.48 person-week; camp food 3.84). LUNCH PACKED FOR TWENTY, the ten and ten more (the player's choice; he does not know the count of Durnhak's people): 20 mouths at a third of a person-day = 0.95 person-week, 133 gp-wt, booked to the party (carry.tsv; not consumed until eaten; camp food at the basecamp 2.89, on the party 0.95). Party rate unchanged at 9", 461 gp-wt of room. The horses stay at the basecamp (three, tethered, water from the boxed spring; nobody minds them). Away about 7:30 am; the walk is 12,602 ft (2.4 miles) north-east round the north foot of the crag, a track known to the party since the Tristle camp days, about an hour and a half laden: at the watercourse about 9 am, THREE HOURS BEFORE Durnhak's noon; the player has not said what they do with the time. No wilderness encounters (precedent; the party has walked this ground for weeks). Durnhak's people in the dungeon (three gnomes in area 38 at round 23 of expedition 29; ten at the watercourse per the day-137 offscreen note) are not asked about yet.

## Day 139 9:00 to 11:45 am -- resting at the watercourse (no dice)
The party rests and enjoys the outdoors at the dry watercourse, the player's word, about two and three-quarter hours; fine late-September weather, no wilderness encounters, no wandering checks (no dungeon clock outdoors). Nothing eaten yet (lunch for twenty is on the party, 0.95 person-week). Durnhak keeps to what his note said and has not yet shown himself: noon. I stopped at about 11:45 so that the last quarter hour, and how the party greets him, is the player's. Frython's lessons with Brand were not declared, so none are recorded (the next tick, day 140, does not depend on it).

## Day 139 noon -- Durnhak comes to the watercourse (HIDDEN ROLL; scene begun, not resolved)
He keeps his note to the hour. How many come with him? d6 = 1 (declared first: 1-2 alone, 3-4 two others, 5-6 four others): HE COMES ALONE, down the watercourse from above, from the direction of the slab under the overhang. He is played from the record (no reaction roll: the fourth meeting): businesslike, counts things, does not open with a demand, reports and waits, warm in his own way (day 101 d6 = 4). A gnome the party has met three times in person (days 99 and 101; the slab). Newly authored look (nothing on the record before): a shade broader than the area-38 gnome, dark beard with a grey streak, a hooded coat of undyed wool, a coil of thin cord at his belt he sometimes runs through his fingers when he counts, a short pick.
What he says at once, authored: he counts the party out loud in gnomish (ten; he has seen nine of them, or fewer; the sum is the thing he checks); greets Tristle by name; and, before the coin or the lower level, mentions that one of his people told him a party of ten came down the stair yesterday, that the elf spoke fair, and that the iron and tools in the hall were left where they lay; he says he heard it 'and wanted it said'. He does not ask for the 36 gp and does not raise the lower level: he waits for the party. NOTHING declared for the party's side (the 36 gp, the lunch for twenty, who speaks, where they sit): all the player's. THE LOWER LEVEL OFFER IS NOT YET AUTHORED: it is the next thing, and it must be rolled on a declared table when the party asks.

## Day 139 noon -- Tristle's greeting and the invitation to lunch (HIDDEN ROLL)
Tristle tells the truth plainly (went down, felt they should have waited, came up, fetched empty barrels from area 13, dinner, Brand's dance, now here) and invites Durnhak to lunch. No reaction roll (known man). His own choice, table declared first (1-4 he sits and eats and counts it no debt between traders; 5-6 he sits, talk first, eat after): d6 = 2: HE ACCEPTS. Authored: he says it was well to come up again, that one does not wait on a man who has not said he will come, and that a meal at noon is no debt between those who deal; he sits on a flat stone and sets down a small round of hard salted goat cheese from his coat, so that it is not a gift and he owes nothing. NOT DECIDED: where the party lights the fire (raw camp food needs a cook and a fire; the watercourse has scrub, dry stems and the odd drift of wood, nothing else has been established); how long it takes. Nothing declared for the party.

## Day 139 noon to one -- lunch at the watercourse, the account settled
Lunch from the camp food, Ista cooking as she has, a small fire of flood-wrack (dry stems and drift in the bed of the watercourse: authored now: enough for a small fire and no more), about an hour, eleven mouths, camp food 0.52 person-week (booked; the party still carries the rest of the lunch for twenty, about 0.43). Durnhak eats and shares his cheese; no debt either way. PAYMENT: the 36 gp (money.tsv, interaction 139:1; carry.tsv coin off the working purse) (audit-skip) and one sealed flask (light.py at noon on day 139: 22 sealed + 1 broached 20.1 turns; carry.tsv oil off the party). He counts both aloud: the flask makes the twelve; 146 sold, half 73, 37 paid, 36 paid: settled. Loop on the bargain noted, kept open.
THE LOWER-LEVEL OFFER (authored from the record, no dice; the B1 stocking of level 2 is NOT volunteered): after the meal he opens it himself, since it was his message that asked them to come and talk. His people have stripped everything within reach of area 38 and the stair; more iron lies deeper in the old workings, past ground his people will not cross alone. He proposes the same shape as the bars: the party goes where the teeth are; the gnomes cart what is iron and old tools; half of what the iron sells for is the gnomes', the party keeps everything else it finds; a quarter at the hole when it is counted, the rest after the sale (his terms, as before). He will say what his people know of the ground if ASKED and not before; he does not volunteer what lives there or where his people came from (undecided, must not be invented to fill the silence). Stopped there, at the offer; nothing declared for the party.

## Day 139 1 pm -- Durnhak on 'the teeth' (HIDDEN ROLLS)
Tristle asks what lives below and adds, honestly, that the party has only fought off what comes on the upper level and has no way to remove anything for good. What Durnhak's people KNOW, one d6 each, declared first (1-3 known: seen, met, lost someone; 4-6 not known): troglodytes (41) 6 NOT KNOWN; black widow (42) 2 KNOWN; hobgoblins (49) 6 NOT KNOWN; ochre jelly (50) 6 NOT KNOWN; kobolds (51 and 53) 1 KNOWN; crab spider (52) 4 NOT KNOWN. The GNOLLS (48) are known without a roll: the arena's door opens on the hall of area 38 (mapdb graph: doors from area 38 to 48, 39, 55; a secret door to 40; stairs to 52).
What he tells, in his own terms and with no numbers: (1) dog-headed men, three or four seen at once (true whether or not the party's gnoll of day 138 counted: four in the key, three left), in a great hollow off the hall's own door, the ones his people fear most because they hunt in the open; (2) a great black spider in a cave choked with webs, seen once, took one of his people (a loss he does not dwell on); (3) small scaly things in numbers, in the caves further on and a great high cavern with bats: cowardly in the open, brave in a crowd, fond of pits and pilfering iron. He says his people have not been past those and there are other things he does not know; he does not say what he does not know by name.
His answer to 'we cannot remove them for good' (authored): he does not ask for that; a thing killed stays killed longer than a thing frightened, but what he needs is the way held while carts pass, for weeks, not for ever; and any that come back can be killed again, which is more than his people can do. He will pay for the time the way is safe, in iron, not for the killing. Stopped there.

## Day 139 1:10 pm -- the partnership agreed in outline (no dice; authored from the record)
Tristle: 'We can do that,' and asks (1) a safe spot to rest, (2) how to enter, the earlier stair or another way, (3) where to find him. Durnhak's answers: (1) the hall of area 38 is the safest ground his people know: worked every day and no teeth in it in the time they have been there; he does not promise it and the stair is at the party's back; (2) the stair from area 30, the way they came; the slab is HIS people's own way in and he does NOT offer it ('not yet': he told them where his back door is once and will not walk ten armed strangers through his home on a first bargain); (3) at the hall, where one of his people is most hours (he will tell the hall gnomes: ten, the elf who speaks, they are not to run; the three in the hall met Tristle yesterday), or by the mark and the slab as before. He confirms the shape of the bargain and that the first iron is counted between them before the quarter is paid. Opened as a loop (L067) and logged as interaction 139:2. Not settled: whether the half is of gross or net (carried over from the bars), who holds the iron between hole and sale, and how far the gnomes will go with their carts. WHERE THE GNOMES LIVE is still undecided beyond 'the workings under the shaft in area 19' (day 95 and 99 record) and is not to be invented; he was asked where to find HIM, not where he lives, and answered with the hall and the slab.

## Day 139 1:20 pm -- the handshake, and Durnhak goes (no dice)
Tristle offers his hand on the deal. Durnhak takes it (authored): a short dry grip with a callus like a board, and one line: that he has struck a bargain through a forty-foot hole with the voice of this hand and now knows its size. He rewraps the round of goat cheese and puts it back inside his coat (it was never a gift), nods to the rest of the party, coils the cord, and goes back up the stones the way he came, toward the overhang and the slab, out of sight in a few minutes; he does not look back. NO new obligation: the terms are those of interaction 139:2 (loop L067). The party still carries what is left of the lunch, the working purse and its flasks less the one given. It is about 1:25 pm; the party has not said what it does next.

## Day 139 1:25 to 2:36 pm -- back to the caves and down the stair to area 38 (expedition 30, rounds 0-11)
The walk from the watercourse to the cave mouth is 5,025 ft, about an hour (Durnhak's own figure of day 95): at the mouth about 2:25 pm; nothing on the way, nothing clocked outdoors. Ista's lantern lit at the mouth (24.0 turns; standard formation carries a lit lantern at the body; the player did not say and did not object earlier; told him). In by the known way, 34 squares (340 ft), Tristle 40 ft ahead, 8 rounds, then three rounds down the stair: round 11, party placed at (20,13) on level 2, the stair foot at the north wall of the hall. Nothing fired (anchor 30 not reached).
HIDDEN, declared first: gnomes in the hall? d6 = 2 (1-5 present): PRESENT. How many of the three: d3 = 3: ALL THREE (hp 7, 5, 4), working the hall as usual. In sight of the stair foot? d6 = 6 (1-3 yes; 4-6 not in sight, he comes out when he sees the light): NOT IN SIGHT. So no gnome at the foot on arrival: within a round or two of the light showing, ONE of them (the one who spoke yesterday, pick on his shoulder) steps to the edge of the lantern's reach at the south-west, as before, and does not run. Durnhak has told the three (ten, the elf who speaks; they are not to run). The other two stay unseen. Standing lantern: 22.9 turns.

## Day 139 round ~13-14 -- the gnomes of area 38 greet the party (no dice; briefed by Durnhak)
Tristle greets them in gnomish. This time the one who spoke yesterday (leathery brown, big nose, grey-white beard, leather cap and apron, pick over the shoulder) steps out at once, no wariness held this time, to the edge of the lantern's reach at the SW, near the same rubble heap. The other two stay at their work, visible now as shapes moving among the heaps further off, not coming closer. He speaks first, before Tristle can ask anything: 'Durnhak has spoken of you. Ten, and the elf who greets us fair.' He does not give his own name (none of the three has, and the party has not asked). He waits to hear what the party wants, having already been told the shape of it by Durnhak but not the moment.

## Round ~14-15 (day 139) -- Tristle names the party (no dice)
Tristle names himself and the other nine. The gnome listens to the whole list without interrupting, then gives his own in return, plainly, once offered first: HARN (authored now, never on the record before; checked against people.tsv for a clash -- none). He says only 'Harn' and nothing else about himself (no trade-name, no kin; gnomes here have been closed-mouthed about themselves throughout). He does not name the other two, who keep working. Nothing else volunteered.

## Round ~16-18 (day 139) -- Harn's advice (HIDDEN ROLL; DM judgment on the door choice, no dice needed there)
Tristle recounts Durnhak's account (dog-headed men, the black spider, the kobolds) and asks if Harn has seen these or others, and where to start. Does Harn add anything of his own past Durnhak's account? d6 = 3 (1-3 yes): ONE THING OF HIS OWN. He confirms Durnhak's list is what his own eyes have seen too (no new monster named) and adds: the door to the hollow where the dog-headed men are (the ARENA CAVERN, the one leading south-east off this same hall) IS THE ONE HIS PEOPLE NEVER OPEN, not even to look; he has heard them on the far side of it, more than once, and that is enough. He gestures roughly south-east across the hall (mapdb: the hall's region links by door to 'ARENA CAVERN'; the door itself has never been logged as found by the party -- IT IS NOT VOLUNTEERED AS A LOCATION, only a direction and a warning).
Where to start: he points instead toward a door on the OPPOSITE side, going roughly south-west/west (mapdb: the hall links by door to 'MUSEUM'; no monster stocked there per the key, only a scroll, TREASURE type S NOT VOLUNTEERED): his people have looked through it once, out of curiosity, saw old paintings and bones and nothing that moves, and went no further because there was no metal in it worth the walking. He does not know what pretty pictures are worth to the party and says so plainly. He has NOT mentioned the secret doors (corridor#3, 40 SECRET CAVERN) or the stair to 52 (RAISED CAVERN): not asked, not offered, consistent with 'his people have not been past' the things Durnhak already named as unknown.

## Round ~18-19 (day 139) -- Harn steps back; the party turns to explore
Tristle thanks him. Harn nods once, says nothing more, and goes back to his own work among the heaps, unhurried; the other two never stopped theirs. Nothing else volunteered. The party is free to move; nothing declared yet for direction or formation.

## Day 139 -- CORRECTION to my own authored geometry for Harn's two doors
Checked against the actual map data (mapdb/output/b1/b1.sqlite level 2) before the party reached it, because the player's own geometry question a few turns back was a reminder to verify rather than freehand it. FOUND: only one door is anywhere near the hall's south gap: (21,8,V), reached from (20,8)/(20,9), a corridor running east along y=8 to about x=27 and down into the open ground of area 39 MUSEUM (x26-29,y10-11) -- i.e. THIS is Harn's 'old paintings, no metal' door, and it sits south and a little east of the gap, matching what I already told the player directly when he asked (audit-skip). Area 48 ARENA CAVERN (the gnolls) is NOT adjacent to the hall at all -- it is far south, x13-19 y24-28, reached by doors logged at (16,20,H), (5,27,H) and (27,26,H), none of them near here. So Harn's gesture toward 'the door we never open' was NOT toward a door the party can presently see or reach in a few rounds; it is a general remembered direction toward ground much further in, past where his people work, not a landmark on today's map. NOTHING TOLD TO THE PLAYER NEEDS CORRECTING (his gestures were never pinned to a coordinate), but future narration keeps to this: the near door is the museum's, the gnoll ground is distant and unmapped from here.

## Day 139 round ~23 -- the party at the door (21,8,V), Tristle listens (HIDDEN ROLL)
Player's own map reading confirmed against the database (out-of-game, no content given): passages SW (toward room 41, troglodytes, unknown to the gnomes per the earlier roll) and SE (the y=8 corridor toward area 39 MUSEUM, unstocked per the key) from (20,9); the door sits beside the SE passage, matching the door already established at (21,8,V). ## Day 139 round 23 -- the door examined (open rolls, not hidden: an objective mechanical fact, not module content)
Ista and Tristle examine the door: swing and hinge, then try the latch (B1's own procedure, rules/b1-module.md: the d6 is rolled ONCE, at examination, not at the haul). d6 = 2: LOCKED. Swing (toward/away) d2 = 1: TOWARD the party. Hinge side (facing it) d2 = 2: RIGHT. Logged to world_changes (21,8,V). Player's own point noted and left as the DM's call: the door being locked is not inconsistent with Harn's account -- his people simply pushed or forced it once out of curiosity and did not trouble with a lock afterward; nothing more elaborate is invented. Tristle's Open Locks (his own figure, rules/thief.md) is unspent on this door.

## Day 139 round 23-28 -- hinges oiled, Tristle picks the lock (HIDDEN ROLL, revealed as an objective fact)
Two turns of oil off the broached flask worked into the hinge knuckles (in reach, the door opens toward the party), left to soak five rounds (round 23 to 28); silent, no extra check (soaking is not hammering). Tristle tries Open Locks: 35% (rules/thief.md), d100 = 82: FAILED. Per-lock limit: Tristle may not try this lock again until 2nd level (rules/thief.md, the PHB's own 'once per lock'); logged to world.py picks as STUMPED. Ista has not tried. The door is still locked.

## Day 139 round 29 -- Ista's own Open Locks attempt (revealed roll)
Her own separate try (each thief gets one per lock, PHB "one try only per thief"): 25% (base 25, human -- no racial row, DEX 13 no adjustment). d100 = 59: FAILED. Stumped for Ista too, until 2nd level. Still locked; neither thief can try it again at their current levels.

Direction d2 = 2 (1 from the hall behind, 2 from ahead along the y=8 corridor toward the museum): FROM AHEAD, the unexplored direction. Distance when first possibly seen, d3 = 2: 20 FT. ## Day 139 round 34-35 -- the orc fight, round 1 (a stray duplicate roll flagged)
Player corrected the DM: the standing formation has carried missile weapons ready since day 138 (clock.tsv round 0 and round 147 of expedition 28 both say so explicitly), never stood down; conceded, no switch penalty. Encounter opened (encounter.py start), 8 declared (Dave, Torsat, Yrtone, Blartho, Dellie, Hask, Frython, Brand short bow/sling/dart/hand axe at the orc). Orc hp 6 -> 3.
*** STRAY ROLL, SHELL CORRUPTION, NOT A TABLE EVENT: *** while re-checking the round's printed output I ran a second partial combat.py command labeled 'reprint' that rolled a FRESH d20=18 for Dave and 1-6=6 damage; this is NOT part of the round, nobody attacked twice, and it is flagged here so it is never read as a second hit. The real round total stands at 3 damage, Hask's hit only.
Morale (obviously superior force -- check every round -- plus 50%+ of its own hp lost, +15%): base 50%, d100=13, +15=28 <= 50: HOLDS. It charges the 20 ft, reaching Torsat (d3=3 of the front rank Yrtone/Dave/Torsat) and swings: d20=13 v16 (AC3): MISS. Nobody hurt. Round 1 over, orc at 3 hp, in melee contact with Torsat.

## Day 139 round 34 -- CORRECTION: rate of fire, and the orc dies in round 1
Player-caught, again: short bow fire rate is 2/round (PHB p.38, rules/combat.md), and dart is 3/round -- I resolved Dave, Torsat and Frython at one attack each. Corrected with the missing shots, rolled as part of the SAME round (party won initiative, so all of it resolves before the orc's segment): Dave's 2nd bow shot 3 v14 miss; Torsat's 2nd bow shot 5 v14 miss; Frython's 2nd and 3rd darts 16 v15 HIT (1) and 18 v15 HIT (3). Corrected round-1 total: Hask 3 + Frython 1 + Frython 3 = 7, against the orc's 6 hp: DEAD, before it ever reached its own segment. VOIDED: the orc's charge-and-swing at Torsat and the morale check that preceded it in my first pass -- neither happened; the orc never got a turn. XP: base 10 + hp (6) = 16, all ten shares (encounters.tsv day 139); xp.py run, totals stand. Encounter ended.

## Day 139 rounds 242-245 -- Tristle scouts alone toward area 42 (HIDDEN ROLLS)
Alone, no light, infravision (party's lantern stays at 11,7 with the others, out of the 30 ft washout once he's clear of it). Move Silently: d100=77 v 30%: FAILED. Does the spider notice? d6=3 (1-3 yes): ALERTED -- it has heard something and, being an ambush hunter, holds still rather than showing itself or fleeing. Tristle reaches the edge of area 42 (via (13,8) then (14,8); the direct y=7 row is walled at (14,7)) and looks in from (14,8), adjacent to the webbed cave's mouth at (15,8). Per the new cold-blooded ruling (rules/exploration.md): infravision shows the ROOM's shape (the DMG p.59 dark-cloudy-night reading) -- a mass of webbing filling the small chamber, thick strands crossing the entrance, the walls and floor of it -- but NO warm or cold signature stands out, so whether the spider is in there right now cannot be told by heat alone. Nothing moves that he can see. He does not know he has been heard.

## Day 139 rounds 245-248 -- Tristle returns to the party (no dice)
The alerted spider stays put (an ambush hunter, it does not chase what has not come to it). Tristle withdraws the way he came, unmolested, and rejoins the party at (11,7). Nothing fired.

## Day 139 -- CORRECTION, voiding the ambush entirely: the surprise resolution redone
Player caught three things in a row and all three hold up: (1) I should not have re-rolled the second surprise pair -- a threshold change is not a reason to regenerate dice, only to re-read what was already rolled; the second pair (2,2) is VOID, shell/table error, not a table event. (2) I never placed the spider concretely; it stayed inside area 42 at its own mouth, not chasing the party into ground they hadn't entered. (3) the deepest error: 'huge spider surprises prey on 1-5' is a property of THAT creature's own ambush roll, not a substitute for the party's ordinary surprised-on-1-2 threshold, and it does not even apply here since this creature is a GIANT spider (web-builder, matches B1's stocking) not a huge one (roving hunter, used once before for the DIFFERENT B1 'crab spiders' in area 52).
CORRECT RESOLUTION, using the FIRST and only real rolls (party d6=5, spider d6=4), standard 1-2 threshold, no bonus either side: NEITHER SURPRISED. No ambush ever happened. Torsat and Yrtone are alive and unwounded; VOID the five ambush attacks, the two hits, and both poison saves in full -- none of it occurred. The spider is visible at the mouth of area 42, adjacent to the party's position (14,8), about 10 ft off, in plain lantern light. Normal round, initiative to be rolled once the party declares.
The poison-rule sourcing (MM giant spider, 'save versus poison or be killed', no bonus stated) stands as the correct basis IF a bite ever does land -- that part of the research was sound and is kept for when it is needed.

## Day 139 -- the black widow spider killed asleep, never woke
Sleep: 1d4=1, only target, no save: PUT UNDER. Under the day-139 ruling (an attack meant to kill, any weapon, slays a sleeper outright, no roll), the declared missile volley's first attacker simply kills it; the rest never needed to act. Bestiary entry written (bestiary/black-widow-spider.md): B1's own stat line used outright for combat, MM giant spider borrowed only for the poison consequence (moot here), XP a flagged DM estimate (60+4/hp = 112, Appendix E has nothing matching HD3 with this profile). Torsat, Dave and Yrtone's held melee action never triggered. Fight over in round 1, nobody hurt.

## Day 139 rounds 253-264 -- the web and the spider's body burned (VOIDED stray row noted)
A stray 'oil' row (fuel=?) was logged first as a shell/tool-use slip before the real draw; it changed nothing and is void, not a table event. The real draw: one full sealed flask emptied over the web mass and the corpse (booked off the party in carry.tsv; the pool has one fewer sealed than it did). Lighting it deliberately is a turn's work under the standing damp-air ruling (rules/b1-module.md), not instant. MM's own text on giant spiders -- 'webs are quite inflammable' -- is read as the specific override of the general 'nothing catches from something else burning' rule: the oil ignites the web directly and it burns hard, unlike ordinary tinder in this damp air. The party backs off 20 ft to (13,7), one round, as it catches.

Reported to the player as what is seen: no creature, just the fire settling.

## Day 139 -- FAILURE CAUGHT AT THE MOUTH: Ista's lantern guttered at round 240, unmanaged
Lit at round 0 of expedition 30 (2:25 pm), 24.0 turns, never refilled or hooded again until snuffed at the mouth on exit (round 392): light.py reports GUTTERED since round 240, out of fuel. This is the DM's own standing job (oil top-off, told to the player as it's done) and it was simply missed across the whole of the black widow encounter and its aftermath. What actually covered the dark stretch: the burning web/corpse gave its own light roughly rounds 254-288 (the DM notes narrated it as lit through that whole span, which happens to be true by coincidence of the fire); Tristle's infravision was genuinely usable rounds 240-254 and 288-304 with no lamp to wash it out (better off than narrated, if anything); lantern-yrtone lit at round 304 covers the search onward. NO encounter fired in the true dark gaps (240-253, 288-304), so nothing needs to be unwound. Told to the player plainly at the cave mouth. Going forward: check light.py show every time a lantern is mentioned as lit, not just after a refill.
